# Changelog

What changed in each build of Ascension Sandbox, newest first.

## 0.13.56 (2026-11-23)

- **A city's smithy refines your gear.** Shops tab, at a smithy: "Have a piece refined...". A weapon, armour or ornament you have fully nurtured rises a grade, its effects a third stronger, as your own refining would make it, without forging skill of your own: the smith asks half the piece's worth and the ore of the next grade, and must have the skill for the grade. The world's cultivators come for it too, and its smiths refine what they wear with their own hands, up to the grade of their realm, so a well-made blade goes on growing with its owner.
- **The world's cultivators bind natal treasures as you may.** Only Golden Cores bound one, and only a weapon. Now anyone from Foundation Building binds a piece nurtured to half: their weapon, or their finest armour or ornament.
- **Another's gear shows on their sheet, and their storage ring to your divine sense.** The sheet lists what they wear, slot by slot, and their natal treasure. When your divine sense is at least as strong as their guard (how well they hide, or their own sense), it shows what their ring holds and their stones too; otherwise it says how far you fall short.
- **Steal with your divine sense.** Interact, "Steal from them...": pick a thing they carry and see your chance. Your sense against their guard and your strength against theirs decide it, three in ten when you stand even; what they wear is about a third as easy, and a natal treasure, a vessel with a spirit or anything sealed away cannot be taken. One try a year at each person. Caught, they count you an enemy, your infamy grows, a bounty is posted, and the proud or stronger draw on you.
- **Pills are named for what they do most.** A pill's effects were ordered by their raw numbers, and breakthrough aid (a point of Spirit makes 1.5) always came behind healing (8) and qi (6): pills made for a breakthrough read as qi pills and the world swallowed them for their qi. A pill now leads with what it does most for its materials, so breakthrough, foundation and attribute pills appear as such; pill designs led by breakthrough aid went from about 15 to 60 in a world of 150 years. Pills already made keep their names.
- **The world keeps its breakthrough pills for the breakthrough.** Its cultivators no longer eat one for its qi, and in the last stage of a realm they buy or redeem one to lay by. Cultivators holding one went from about 1 in 100 to 1 in 40.
- **Pills that open the mind.** Alchemists now and then work out a pill of Wood that raises comprehension (Wood herbs to give it its element, the materials richest in Essence for its strength), and sell it; a house with an alchemy hall makes them too. The world's cultivators take one when it would raise them, so those held back from a method by their understanding can grow into it, as you can.
- **Sect libraries grow with their sects.** A library was filled once, at the founding: a sect whose people rose to Golden Core still taught Foundation Building arts. Now a library behind the realm of its strongest member now and then gains the next grade of method and attack art. Rogue cultivators also come upon arts of their grade half again as often, and look harder for a method that suits them. A house's leader and its ancestor, from Golden Core, now and then write the finest art they know into its library, and devise arts of their own four times as often, every one for its shelves.
- **Stolen manuals in the black market.** A house's greedy and treacherous members now and then copy the finest manual their rank opens and sell it to the fences of the nearest city. That city's black market sells a copy of each a year (the newest four), with the house's name on it. The world's rogues, who otherwise only ever saw a house's arts by luck, buy them too. Three in ten sellers are found out and cast out of their house, with infamy; if it is your house, you hear of it.
- **Lost arts lie hidden in the world.** A few arts of each grade from Earth up (Immortal and Divine above the clouds) are hidden at any time in caches in the ruins, secret realms and wilds of their grade. Each is the only slip of it left, at 85 to 98% quality, finer than nearly anything in a library. You find them as any cache (searching, divine sense, divination, teahouse rumours), and are told when one is a lost art. The world's wanderers find them too, and a house's member gives a copy to its library. One taken is hidden anew in time.
- **The great keep up with their realm.** From Golden Core on, the world's cultivators now and then have the work that has fallen behind their realm made by the most skilled master of its craft in their world: a weapon of their grade, a breakthrough pill in a realm's last stage, pills that open the mind while their method lags, and armour. They bring the materials and pay the master a fifth of the work's price. Measured over two worlds of 300 and 500 years of history, half of Nascent Soul and Deity Transformation cultivators went unarmed and Deity Transformation practised methods two grades below its realm, short of the comprehension a method of its grade asks. Now 85 to 100% of those from Golden Core up are armed, and the methods of Golden Core and Nascent Soul lag less than a fifth of a grade. Deity Transformation lags by a third of a grade to a grade. More cultivators reach Nascent Soul and Deity Transformation.
- **The world holds four times the spirit stones, and the great earn like the great.** Works cost six times more each grade, but a Deity Transformation cultivator's year of work brought in at most 7,290 stones, a quarter of one work of their grade. Now a world is made with four times the stones (older worlds find four times as many in their earth when loaded, and every town keeps its ease against a larger purse), and from Golden Core what a year of someone's work is worth grows sixfold a realm, as prices do: 1,620 at Golden Core, 9,720 at Nascent Soul, 58,320 at Deity Transformation (below Golden Core it is as before). Measured over two worlds of 300 years and 40 more of play: the books still balance to the stone, towns' merchants hold their full share of the stones for the first time (12 to 17%, from 6 to 8%) and none went broke, the median cultivator holds about twice as much, and the median Deity Transformation cultivator 12,000 to 21,000 stones instead of 4,000 to 7,000. More cultivators reach the high realms (at 300 years, about twice as many of Nascent Soul and Deity Transformation): the world's strong are more and better armed against you.

## 0.13.55 (2026-11-22)

- **A Heavenly Foundation or Golden Core asks nothing of your dao.** Pressing past the peak of Qi Condensation asked your main dao's leading concept at 30, and a Heavenly Golden Core (and the Great Perfection before it) asked a whole concept: years of contemplation a short early life could not spare. Now the hidden stage and its seal are all the early realms ask (the Tenth Layer still wants a dao foundation of 70). From Golden Core on nothing changes: a Heavenly Nascent Soul still asks two whole concepts.
- **The world's makers design as you do.** A smith or alchemist of the world threw one or two random materials together, so a master's blade had a twentieth of the edge of yours made of nine ingots. Now each maker sets out for one effect (a breakthrough pill, a blade's edge, an array's gathering), puts in three units just at a grade's skill and up to all nine near its top, picks the materials that give it most and leans on the five phases; the finest hands of a grade work their materials first (ingots, extracts, timber, plates). Shops, sect halls, masterpieces and the world's crafters all make such works now, and the people who carry them fight and break through with them. Over sixty years of history the world's weapons hit about six times harder, and as many people carry one as before: makers still turn out cheap first-grade pieces two times in five, so Qi Condensation cultivators can afford a blade.
- **Secret realms hold works worth the risk.** A realm's heart (and the old delve's core, the heavens' relics, a fallen treasure) is an ancient work made with nine units of worked materials from every craft: a blade, armour, breakthrough, healing or qi pills, striking or warding talismans. The world's wanderers who go into an opening now and then bring one out too, and wear or take it.
- **The world's cultivators take their best breakthrough pill.** They took the first one in their bag, however weak.

## 0.13.54 (2026-11-21)

- **A clan's child can join a sect and stay of the clan.** Entering a sect used to mean leaving your clan for good. Now a clan member who joins a sect becomes a scion of the house: they serve the sect (its ranks, contribution, library and laws) and keep the ancestral hall's blessing, honouring the ancestors, half their allowance and the clan's blood feuds. Leave the sect, or be cast out of it, and you go home to the clan; a scion can also renounce the house (Sect tab). Children of a scion are born into the clan.
- **Clans send their young to the sects, and win influence there.** A clan's head, ancestor or elders can send one of its young to a sect with a gift from the treasury (Sect tab, "Send kin to a sect"): the clan's word opens the gate out of the intake season. The world's clans do the same each year. A house's influence in a sect grows with its scions' ranks (an outer disciple 1, an elder 8, the sect master 20). From 4, the two halls think better of each other each year; from 8, neither will make war on the other; and a candidate for the sect's seat is carried by their house. Scions send stones home, and now and then copy a manual their house lacks; a righteous sect that catches one casts them out. The Sect tab lists the houses with blood in a sect, and a clan's scions abroad.
- **Your house asks things of you, and houses lobby a sect master.** Serving a sect as a scion, every few years a letter comes from home: send stones, copy a manual from the sect's library (you may be caught), or speak for a cousin at the gate. Leading a sect, the houses with blood in it ask you to raise their scions: granting it wins the house and the two halls draw closer, while your other elders grumble.
- **Attribute pills reach as high in beast form as in human form.** A pill's limit counted your bloodline's share of the attribute, which a beast's form doubles, so the same pill took a beast with +6 from its blood 6 points higher when swallowed in human form. The limit now leaves your blood out, as it already left out what training and breakthroughs earned: your blood comes on top in either form. The pill's preview says so.
- **A conquered clan or sect leaves ruins.** When a faction was crushed by another, its estate or mountain gate stayed on the map as if it still stood, empty, and anyone could found a new house there. It is now ruins, held by the conqueror. Older saves are mended on loading.
- **Your intake standard holds.** Branch families sent children to the clan whatever their roots, an academy sent its pupils to your house whatever theirs, and "Recruit disciples" gave up on a rare standard (Heavenly, Variant) and took whoever came. All three now keep to "Intake: X root or better". An academy's pupil who falls short asks to be your own disciple instead, and one who meets it goes to the house's seat. The Members list counts everyone, says how many lie past the first sixty, and shows each member's root.
- **Arts handed to you unasked now suit you.** An ascended master or parent's avatar taught you one of their arts every year, and a month's guidance could hand over their finest, whatever its dao, element or path: the Arts tab filled with arts of every kind. Now a teacher passes on only an art you can use: one of your paths (a method only where you have none), of your root's elements or a dao you have studied, or neutral. You are told when an avatar teaches you.
- **A rival who poisons someone is a real person, and Causality finds them.** When someone in the world died "poisoned by a rival" or "killed in a treasure dispute", nobody had done it, so reading the death through Causality said "no hand but heaven's". Now the culprit is the victim's rival or enemy, or else an unfriendly peer of their realm nearby, and the death names them: the reading follows the thread to them, and a ghost or kin's grudge can find them. Deaths of this kind from before this build find their culprit the first time you read them. Victims of road robbers read as nameless hands, not heaven's.
- **Tending fields teaches once a month.** The button took no time and could be pressed again and again for free herbalism. It now tends each field once a month, and says so when there is nothing left to tend.
- **"Bid it keep its beast shape" stays.** Every new year it flipped back for a beast you cherish, as if you were a master lifting a seal. Only a seal laid on you by a master is lifted that way now.
- **Pills given to others speak of them.** Feeding a dao companion, disciple or beast a pill above their grade said "its force tears at your dantian"; the preview and the result now say "their".
- **Siblings are in the family list.** Brothers, sisters and half-siblings show in the Relations tab's Family (Kin), and a half-sibling is named so.
- **A tamed tribe chief is yours, once.** Taming a chief left it chief of its horde until the year's end: its challenge stayed on the Location tab, and fighting it again listed it twice among your companions (one beast, so whatever touched one touched "both", and killing it lost both). Taming a chief now hands its tribe on at once, the challenge is not offered against your own beast, a beast already yours cannot be tamed again, and a doubled companion is listed once.
- **A ghost lives as a ghost.** A ghost was offered a marriage contract, and could take a dao companion, swallow pills, work for wages, sit in teahouses and keep a festival, because every new way to play had been let do whatever a cultivator of flesh does. Now one table says, for each kind of being (a beast, a ghost, a spirit in its vessel), what it may do, and every such button reads its reason. A ghost cannot wed, keep consorts, have or take in children, swallow pills (in battle or out: it grows by devouring), tend fields, work, trade in town, join a sect or keep the town's company; it may still fight, explore, delve, haunt, craft, teach and take disciples, raze and conquer, and enter the black market. Other ghosts, the demonic and anyone who thinks well of it will still barter with it face to face. A spirit still bound in its artifact answers to the same table, and about a hundred more actions that need hands or presence now ask it too: it could raise and move abodes, set up arrays, refine and bind gear, rear gu, remake its blood, found towns, rule kingdoms, heal, ride, teach in person, play Go and debate. A ghost answers each of those as well (it can do most, but not remake flesh or blood, heal, play Go or debate). A spirit that has manifested a body is a person like any other and may do all of it, children aside.
- **A puppet says what realm it fights at.** Its details in the bag, the shops, the market, the battle bag and the sect's gate, and the line when you finish making one, now name the cultivator it strikes as hard as in your hands ("Late Foundation Building"): its blows on a typical cultivator of that realm land as hard as theirs do on each other. It counts everything the fight counts: its grade, its arms, its role, a war-puppet school, and how much of a puppet above your realm you can work. While you design one in the Professions tab, it says the realm it would strike at, from middling work to flawless.
- **A Mending Gu tires instead of dying at a touch.** Used at rest it was gone after one use. It now closes wounds the same way in battle and out of it: each use tires it by a quarter (four uses from full vigour, fed back up as any gu), and when it is spent one of the brood dies and the rest go on.
- **Blood Essence has no seeds.** Seeds could be drawn from a mortal's Blood Essence and planted, and then never grew or left the field. Seeds come only from living herbs now, and any field can be uprooted (Location tab, your abode's fields) to clear it.
- **Robbers do not reach into your inner world.** Your people living inside it died "ambushed by robbers", "killed in a treasure dispute" or "poisoned by a rival". Inside, only its beasts and wilds can take them.
- **No town inside an inner world.** A town founded beside an abode carried into your inner world had no road to it. Founding one there is refused (its people come by Settle on its lands); one already founded is now a step away from its abode, and back.
- **Immortals stay above the clouds.** Inviting an immortal into a sect you lead below the clouds carried them to its gate at once, at full strength, until the year's end pulled them back up: they now join and serve from where they are. An immortal of the heavens is not carried into an inner world whose keeper stands below, and sworn siblings and lovers may now be brought into your inner world as kin can. Someone let out of your inner world while you stand inside it steps out where you entered, and a companion left at an abode inside your world is left there only when you stand inside it, so no one is shut in where only you inside can speak to them.

## 0.13.53 (2026-11-21)

- **Mystical places.** Ancient springs, magma pits, lightning valleys, moon pools, blood wells of the ancients and frost caverns lie in the wilds, more of them in an age rich in qi. Each gives a great gift as strong as a master's marrow washing and as rare (six months soaking, once a century for each kind): the foundation washed, the body tempered (+5 Strength and Constitution), the meridians struck open, the bloodline purified, the mind made clear. A lesser gift (healing, a little insight in the place's dao) comes again every five years. The strongest faction nearby holds each, as it holds spirit veins: its members pay contribution, outsiders pay spirit stones, and factions go to war over them. The world's cultivators bathe too. A Deity Transformation with insight 90 in a place's dao can shape a new one in the wilds (five years, 100,000 spirit stones) and bathes in it free.
- **The defier forges a dao of their own.** On the defying road each realm now also asks one more concept forged into a dao you name, on top of everything it asked before: one to make the three treasures one, two for Unfettered, three for Limitless, four for Transcendent. Concepts are forged from daos you have perfected (a year each, Character tab) and work half again as strongly in you. The fifth is its Great Completion: your domain becomes the dao's own, the domains of every dao its concepts came from at work at once. The world's defiers forge theirs too.
- **Holdings and businesses.** Set up a spirit stone mine at a vein no faction holds (its grade shown by the vein's richness, a grade more above the clouds), a herb farm or a beast-core farm in the wilds, or buy a stake in a town's trade (it pays 8% of its cost a year). Each mine or farm yields its goods every year, or sells them for spirit stones: your choice, holding by holding. A faction nearby may take one you are away from and too weak to hold. (NPCs already spent spirit stones on their cultivation each year, and factions holding veins already grew richer for it.)
- **A kingdom at work.** The overlord of a kingdom can set its wilds to be farmed (the goods kept, or sold), and drill its army with their arts: its officers rise to Qi Condensation, then Foundation Building, and it fights harder in its wars.
- **Begin as a Young Ruler.** A new origin: raised too young to a small kingdom's throne, with its tribute, its wars, its rebels and its court.
- **The blood-draining array.** A demonic formation laid over a town (formations 40, three months): each year it drinks its mortals' lives and wears at the weaker cultivators there, and you cultivate up to twice as fast while it stands. It darkens karma, brings infamy, and the righteous sects of the region find it in time and put a price on your head. Anyone no more than a realm below its maker can break it. The world's ruthless demonic cultivators lay them too.
- **Craft arts.** A craft scripture you make now carries one art of its own, forged as a method's traits are (rerolls from your insight and comprehension, or set by will): Finer Work, Sure Hand, Quick Study, Frugal Hands for every craft; Abundance for alchemy and talismans; Bountiful Fields, Steady Cross and Deep Seed for herbalism. Only the scripture that counts for the craft acts.
- **Treasure recipes.** Each world hides six lost recipes for great works in its ruins and secret realms; exploring there may turn one up. Making one asks insight 80 in its daos, skill past its grade's mark, and a hand near flawless (80% of a perfect work, or it cracks and the materials are lost). Made, its effects are 1.6 times what its materials would give. They are never sold.
- **Seclusion with focus.** Before a seclusion you may play the Small Heavenly Circuit, guiding qi around the body's circuit while keeping the pressure down. Played well, your next ten years of seclusion go up to 20% faster. It is optional, and can be skipped once mastered.
- **Higher realms already hit far harder and take far less.** Measured before touching it: a Foundation Builder with an ordinary kit wins none of its fights against Golden Core, landing about a tenth of the harm it takes, and nothing at all against higher realms; only a kit stacked far past its realm beats one realm up. So no change to health by realm.

- **Inheritances have real trials.** Claiming a legacy was one roll of comprehension, then you walked out with everything. Now its maker set tests by their own nature: two for a Yellow or Mystic legacy, three for Earth or Heaven, four above. Seven kinds so far: the Race, the Mirror of Desire, the Remnant's Duel, the Starving Village, the Poisoned Cup, the Dao Lecture and the Bottomless Stair. Each offers several answers with their chance from your attributes, realm and insight; some can kill you if they fail (they say so), and one always cannot fail.
  - **The maker's favour** is apart from passing: a ruthless maker likes ruthlessness, a compassionate one mercy, a cautious one care, an arrogant one boldness. At the end you take the legacy in proportion to it, the finest things first: a quarter at least, all of it and the maker's blessing (insight in their deepest dao) at full favour. What you leave stays sealed for the next to try.
  - **End out of favour** and the seal turns you away for ten years a grade. Or **take it all by force**: karma -20, and a fight with the inheritance's spirit.
  - **The world's wanderers face the same trials,** answering by their own nature, and some die in them.

- **A tribe works for its chief.** Tribes gathered and then did nothing. Now a chief sets its tribe one of four orders (Location tab): **Guard the lair** (as before), **Forage** (each year the members at the lair bring in the land's herbs, ores and the cores of what they hunt, into the tribe's hoard), **Train** (each member cultivates six months more a year, never past you) or **Raid** (each year the tribe falls on the nearest town of the region: a tenth of its purse comes to you, but the weaker raiders may fall, you gain infamy, and the region's tamers come again). The chief takes what it needs from the hoard at the lair. The world's tribes forage on their own.
- **A beast's instinct is weaker off its own path.** A beast needs no method on any path, where a person must find one for each, so it could take up the Body and the Soul beside its own for free. Measured, a beast already cultivates its own path at half a person's pace with a method (beasts grow by devouring instead), so its own path is unchanged; on a path taken up besides it, instinct now runs at 60% of that.
- **The world's cultivators take insight pills** when none is working, and contemplate faster for it, as you do.
- **Your sect no longer sends you to die.** The sect's call to arms sent a disciple against anyone of the enemy a realm above or below, and ignoring the order cost 15 standing however hopeless the fight. It now sends you only against a foe at most a quarter stronger than you, tells you how you measure against them, and asks nothing of you for refusing one stronger than you. A champion at your gate far beyond you (half again your strength) may be left to shout without your people thinking less of you.
- **Robbers, grudge-holders and challengers size you up.** Picked by realm alone, they fell on players far stronger than their realm said and died like fools, again and again. When you are three times the strength of all of them together, they now think better of it four times in five.
- **Probe someone with divine sense.** Interact offers a forced look at a sheet closed to you: your divine sense against how well they hide (two in five at equal strength, more the stronger you are). Laid bare, their sheet stays open to you for ten years; caught, their regard falls by 25, the arrogant and the vengeful count you an enemy, and they stay on guard the rest of the year.
- **On a phone:** the battle screen puts its buttons above the log, and the log keeps only the round under way ("Every round" shows them all). The designer's result folds to one line under the materials with a button to open it, and its sort and filter sit above the list again. Inventory items have a line between them. A tap just as a list stops scrolling no longer presses what slid under the finger.
- **The detailed log's wound line adds up.** A blow on a vital part held at the edge of ruin read "71.8 / toughness 1.50 = 1": what came through was about 48, and the head, held by its blood, could take only 1. It now says both: "71.8 / toughness 1.50 = 47.9, of which the head takes 1 (held at the edge of ruin)".
- **Fixes:** a ghost of the world that seized a living body stayed a "ghost" in human flesh, and so did one rebuilt from a caught soul or reborn through a rebirth seed: each load turned the body back into a shade. The blood money paid to a hunting sect and the reparations of a broken attack now go through the world's stone ledger.

## 0.13.52 (2026-11-21)

- **Contemplate the Dao no longer buries you in text.** The list of daos shows one short line each (concepts open, the place's affinity); what a dao and its concepts do is on that dao's own page, and the long explanation at the top is behind "How the dao works".
- **Contemplate until it is whole.** A concept's page says about how long it has left at your pace ("whole in about 70 years"). Besides one year to a hundred, a concept can now be contemplated **until it is whole**, and a dao's open concepts **until every one is whole**: one after another, the deepest first, without stopping each time one is made whole (that goes to the chronicle). **Every open concept, until whole** does the same across every dao. The last few points of a concept that does not lead its dao are slow on purpose; the estimate now says so.
- **Open every concept you can.** One button opens as many concepts as your realm holds, the deepest first, in every dao or in one. A concept you could not open no longer drops you out of the dao's page.
- **Repeat works for contemplation.** Repeat found the dao you picked last time by its whole label, stage included ("Fire (94.0, Dao Heart)"), so after any long contemplation that reached a new stage it fell back to the list of daos. It now finds it by name.
- **Avatars contemplate every open concept.** An avatar can be set to every concept you hold open, in every dao, one after another. What it makes whole goes to the chronicle and no longer stops your own seclusion; it asks what next once none is left.
- **The Hall of Fame sorts.** By newest, highest realm, strength, earliest ascension, youngest inducted, fame, most slain or deepest dao. New entries keep these numbers; older ones keep their realm and age and come last where they lack the rest.
- **The Relations tab narrows your family.** Kin, Descendants, Companions, Spirit beasts, and Master and disciples, each with how many, and a search once the family passes eight.
- **Perfected daos fold away in battle.** With more than two, their powers sit behind one button ("Perfected daos, 3 of 9 ready"), open or closed as you last left it, the ready ones first.
- **The Chronicle can be filtered.** Hide kinds of line (cultivation, fights, people, crafts and spoils, the world, the rest) and filter by a word.
- **Union exams can skip tiers.** You may sit any tier above yours that your skill reaches: a saint alchemist need not sit with beginners. Each tier above yours has its own button on the union page, greyed with the reason when your skill falls short.
- **Choose which parts you are born without.** Besides the named conditions, each arm, leg and eye can be chosen one by one. Points come back by what is missing: an arm or a leg 3 and both 7, an eye 2 and both 8, and 3 more for limbless and blind (so limbless is 14 and limbless and blind still 25).
- **A clan you add to a new world carries your surname.** Its founder and people were given a surname of the world's own. Now they carry the one you type in the new surname box, or the one in the clan's name ("Luo Clan": the Luo).
- **Insight pills.** A pill of Spirit and Longevity quickens contemplating the dao for a while: 15% more a grade, at most twice as much (Primordial), for twice its strength in months. The body tires of them as of every lasting gift, so each one of a grade works less. Nothing but contemplation is hastened. The world's cultivators do not take them yet.
- **Tamers no longer hunt a free beast every five years.** They came 20% of every year from anywhere in the world. Now they come from your region only, 12% a year at first, never for 15 years after you beat one (4 after you outrun one), fewer for each one beaten, for a fearsome name (half at 300 fame) and for a tribe's chief (a quarter).
- **A beast can trade, face to face.** Towns still bar their doors, but a beast tamer (Beast Taming 20), anyone who walks a demonic way, anyone who thinks well of you (regard 30), and once you are wise enough to speak (Nascent Soul) anyone your equal, will barter with a beast. A beast at a spirit vein no faction holds can also **gnaw stones out of it** (a month, two months of the vein's yield, six times a year).
- **A beast's soul in a person's body is a person.** Seizing a human body as a beast left you "a beast" in human flesh: shut out of towns and arts, and human form locked behind a Nascent Soul the soul had lost. You now walk as a person born a beast, and can take your own beast shape again.
- **The art forge says what things mean.** The tempering minigame now says on its screen what each of the three measures does for this kind of move (guard, hold and answer for a guard: what each stops, how long, how hard it answers), that qi still held at the release is lost, and that backlash under 40 is safe. A new move's message says what it does in battle in plain numbers and its qi cost. The form picker says what a form is with its element ("Metal Flame: qi shaped as fire... strikes from afar... its effects: Burning, Sweeping, Withering").
- **Backlash past 40 now costs something.** Releasing a move you tempered by hand with backlash above 40 tears at your meridians (a point for every 4 over) and lowers the chance it takes, as the yellow zone always implied. By instinct, no strain is charged.
- **Proven work makes quick crafting worth it.** A work you make by hand at 50% or better proves you at its difficulty: from then on, quick crafting anything of that craft as hard or easier comes out at 95% of your best by hand there, without rolling (the button shows it: "Quick craft (76%)"). So the minigame is for a harder work than you have made before, or to beat your best. A single quick craft also plays a mastered minigame near your best now, as "Quick craft all" did.

## 0.13.51 (2026-11-21)

- **Begin a life on the Martial Way or the Shaman's Way.** The new-life screen's path list now offers both mortal ways. You start as an Apprentice (or a Listener), a mortal with no root needed, and a path of cultivation suited to your gifts waits beside it should you ever take it up. A shaman's fate is rolled with the Spirit of 30 the spirits answer.
- **You are always asked before your body is remade.** A body asleep in your blood used to wake on its own at a breakthrough, a tribulation, an insight in seclusion or at death's door, and replace whatever body you had, a secret body earned with great deeds included. Now it stirs and asks: wake it, keep your own for now (it stirs again no sooner than ten years on), or keep your own and seal it away for good.
- **An avatar keeping your door no longer trades your body away.** The heavens' offer of a secret body had no cautious answer, so an avatar answering for you (or "let nothing stop me") took its first choice, Accept: a great dao body could become Heaven-Forging Hands while you sat in seclusion. Any offer of a new body now always waits for you, whatever your settings.
- **The battle log is in colour.** A struck part's name takes its state's colour, as on the body diagram beside the log (pale green whole, gold hurt, red badly hurt or destroyed). How bad a wound is runs from pale (barely bruising) through orange (wounding) to red (mangling, destroying, severing). What turned a blow aside (a miss, footwork, a guard, a shield, an afterimage) is blue.
- **The detailed log shows its sums.** With Detailed log on, every blow gets three small lines under it:
  - **To hit:** the chance, term by term (base 80%, speed against speed, divine sense, footwork, aim, mastery, concepts...), and the roll.
  - **Harm:** attack against defence and the realms' tiers, the edge, then every factor that changed it (the roll, the elements, momentum, intent, concepts, a guard...).
  - **Wound:** what the part took through the body's toughness, its integrity before and after, the bleeding and blood left, and the chance the blow reaches the organ behind it.
  The fight goes exactly as it would with the log off.
- **Combat formulas in the Guide.** A new topic spells out speed, the chance to hit, attack, defence, the edge, harm and wounds, with the numbers the game uses.
- **The Dao of the Sword reworked.** Players found four of its five concepts hardly worth holding: Intent rarely mattered, Precision was a mere +10% to hit, and Will's cheaper qi rarely counted. Sheath gave +40% every other round, less than two plain blows. Blade is unchanged.
  - **Intent:** foes not above your realm (not only lower ones) falter as battle begins, 25% of the time each when whole. Your sword blows now also shake the foe 15% of the time.
  - **Precision:** +15% to hit (was +10%), and 20% of sword blows find a vital point and strike 50% harder.
  - **Sheath:** each round you hold the sword back (guarding, stepping, healing, anything but striking) adds +100% to your next sword blow, for up to 2 rounds held (+200%). A "Sheathed" chip shows what is stored.
  - **Will:** sword blows pass 50% of a raised guard or barrier, and sword arts still cost 20% less qi.
- **Chronicle lines begin with a capital.** Lines that opened with a beast's or a place's name ("the Jade Python...") began in lower case. Older saves' lines are mended as they load.
- **An artifact spirit lying unheld meets what befalls a vessel, not a cultivator.** Waiting years in ruins for a hand, a spirit was set on by beasts and robbed by rogues, as if it had a body and a purse.
  - **Passers-by:** someone comes through. Call out to them or let them pass.
  - **Weather:** rain and frost wear at the vessel unless you ward it with a little qi.
  - **A scavenger:** a mortal takes you for scrap and carries you to the nearest town, where more people pass.
  - **A beast's nest:** a beast carries the bright thing off to its nest in the wilds nearby.
  - **How you wait** (a button beside your vessel): quietly; with your aura hidden, so few pass and none of the greedy; or sending out pulses, so passers-by come three times as often, the greedy with them.
- **Held, the road's dangers fall on your holder.** A beast goes for them and a robber blocks their way. Their realm decides what comes, their stones pay the toll, and their legs run.
- **Calling out, you choose whom to call to, and they decide.** "Call out" took up the strongest person there at once. Now you pick from the people where your vessel lies, each with how likely they are to take you up. The greedy and the ambitious reach for a thing that speaks. The cautious, the reclusive and frightened mortals leave it lying, and a vessel far beneath them is hardly worth the bother. One who turns you down is not asked again that year.
- **Your holder, at a glance.** The vessel panel names them with their nature and regard and opens them as anyone on the Relations tab. Their sheet is open to you: you live against their skin.
- **Lending your strength no longer costs regard.** Holding back half or all of it used to make your holder think less of you every year. Now holding back costs nothing, and lending all of it wins 2 regard a year.
- **Urge your holder to do more than travel.** Urge them to spend a month searching the wilds where they stand for materials (food for you, once they share), sitting to cultivate (8% of a stage on top of their own), or resting their wounds. They heed you by their regard, as with travel.
- **Every spirit can resonate in battle.** Staying in the vessel and lending your holder help each round was a jewel's alone. A blade's spirit now lends Forewarning and Pour qi, an armour's Ward and Pour qi, a jewel's all four: something to do in a fight before you know an art of your own.
- **When the world is about to kill your holder, you feel it coming.** A beast tide or a calamity killed holders out of sight while the spirit sat cultivating. Now you are stopped and choose: pour your strength into them (your soul is strained; by your realm and their regard they live, and think the better of you), scream at them to flee (by their regard), or let it come.
- **Fighting beside your holder, blows are really not aimed at you.** A saber's sweep, a volley, a wave art's spill, conducted lightning, a miasma, an array's field and a counter stance all reached the spirit in its vessel while its holder stood. Now they pass it by, as aimed blows did; soul arts still find it.
- **A bound spirit does nothing that needs a body.** Lying in ruins, it could still "give the ruins back to the wild" or raze an empty seat. Now it cannot, and no one nearby comes to see it as a rival, a friend or a foe (only its holder knows it is there).
- **A ghost is a shade, not a body.** A ghost lost limbs, bled, and died at once when its throat or heart was destroyed. Now it wears a shade: it does not bleed, a limb torn away is a wisp that gathers again, and only its heart of yin (the chest) or its sea of consciousness can end it. Ghosts of older saves take the shade as they load.
- **A ghost keeps the arts it knew.** Rising as a ghost took every art but soul arts and wards, though a ghost could learn them all again. Now it keeps them; only its cultivation methods are gone.
- **Forged armour and jewels can raise an attribute.** The only attribute forged gear gave was Agility, from swift materials. Now a jewel's sharp-cut facets, and a herb of long life quenched into a jewel or a piece of armour, give the attribute of the piece's element while worn: Strength from Fire or Metal, Agility from Wind or Lightning, Spirit from Water or Ice, Comprehension from Wood, Constitution from Earth. Pieces stack, each further one counting less.
- **A ghost belongs to the Yellow Springs.** A ghost walks down to the Netherworld and back whenever it will, without the 60 insight the living need. Dying, you can linger where you fell or go down there at once. A ghost also grasps the Dao of Yin a little more every year it lingers (1.5 a year, twice that in the Netherworld).
- **A haunt of your own.** A ghost can make the place it stands its haunt (a month), then soak its yin deeper, one level for each of its realms (three months a level, five levels at most). Each level, it cultivates a tenth faster there and drinks a fifth more of a town's fear, and its haunt draws lesser ghosts to it, food for its keeper.
- **Hunt other ghosts.** "Seek a ghost to devour" lists the ghosts of your world, the nearest first, and takes you to one.
- **Seizing a body is a contest of souls.** It was one roll of the dice. Now the two souls fight round by round: press (to crush), hold (to wall up) or feint (to slip past). Press breaks a feint, a feint slips past a hold, a hold turns a press. Each round goes on the odds the seizing always had, swung far toward whoever read the other right, and the first to win two takes it. Every soul leans one way by its nature (the ruthless and proud press, the careful hold, the sly feint), and you are shown how it leans. This is how a ghost possesses a body and how a living soul seizes another's. Throwing out a soul that seeks your own body can be fought the same way.
- **A seized body fights its new soul.** The ghost's road ended the moment it took a body. Now the flesh rejects you for four years, and half a year more for each realm your soul stands above it. A body given freely does so for a year and a half. A body made for yin souls (ghost-touched, Nine Yin, a nether corpse) does so a quarter as long. Meanwhile you cultivate at ×0.6. Wear its physique down to an ordinary body and it is yours at once.
- **Rise as a ghost.** The new-life screen can begin a life as a ghost, in a ruin among the living or at the Netherworld's Gate of Ghosts.
- **Be born a ghost or a demon.** The beasts of the Netherworld (a hungry ghost, a hopping corpse, a nether raven...) and of the Demon Realm (an imp, a brimstone hound, a heart demon...) can be chosen on the new-life screen. They are born at their plane's gate: a deadly cradle, where a parent of your line helps.
- **Evolutions can lean.** Every evolution gives attributes or a new nature by turns, by the beast's element and new form. Now a beast can be raised to lean toward might (Strength and Constitution; massive, thorned or shelled), swiftness (Agility; elusive or blinking) or spirit (Spirit and Comprehension; soul-warded, mirrored or radiant) instead. Set it on the Relations tab for a tamed beast, or the Character tab played as a beast. The world's beasts keep their blood's own way.
- **Agility, said plainly.** Its tooltip said it decided "who strikes first and how often". In fact speed counts in every exchange: every 25 speed above a foe's is +10% to hit them and to be missed by them. Opening or closing the distance, fleeing and catching a foe who overreaches all compare speeds too. The tooltip now says this, with the numbers.

## 0.13.50 (2026-11-20)

- **Sect wars have generals and champions.** From players' proposals for sects at war.
  - **Generals:** as leader, name up to three of your people on the Sect tab. They stand first in your line in every clash of your wars.
  - **A champion at the gate:** once a year or so while your faction is at war, the enemy's champion comes to your gate and calls you out. Answer it yourself, send a general, or let them shout (approval -5).
  - **Losing heart:** the side whose champion falls fields one fewer fighter in that war's clashes for a year. The winner gains 15 prestige and 5 approval.
- **Send your sect against someone.** As leader, pay from the treasury (500 stones at Qi Condensation, four times as much each realm). Your best then go after the target each year for five years, until they fall; at most three at once. Against the demonic, your sworn enemies, a faction at war with you or the infamous it is just. Against anyone else it is murder by another hand, which darkens your karma and spreads your infamy. Orders can be called off.
- **Every house has its Holy Son.** Each sect and clan names its most gifted young cultivator (a genius at least, under 60) its Holy Son or Holy Maiden. A clan names a Young Master or Young Lady, a demonic sect a Demon Son or Demon Maiden.
  - **Stipend:** 200 stones a year for each of their realms, until they pass 100.
  - **Protector:** one of the house's strongest is set over them. Whoever falls on them in a duel meets the protector first.
  - **Where it shows:** the title appears on sheets and in lists, and the Sect tab's Ranking names them. You can be named one too.
- **The arrogant young master.** An arrogant Holy Son or Young Master of another house, near your strength, may block your way in a town and demand that you kneel. At most once a year:
  - **Bow:** they laugh, and you lose a little fame.
  - **Walk past:** half the time they let it go, the other half they strike anyway.
  - **Teach them manners:** a fight. Win and your name grows (+15 fame). Kill them, and their house thinks far worse of you and their protector becomes your enemy.
- **Secret realms can be walked.** From players' proposals for realms you explore, not just a list of rooms. At an open realm, **Step into the realm** (Location tab) and the Map shows its inside, six chambers you travel between like any land:
  - **The Gate:** the only way out.
  - **The Winding Passages:** explore and hunt there as in the wilds.
  - **The Garden of Spirit Herbs:** gathering finds a grade finer than elsewhere in the realm.
  - **The Trial Hall:** a guardian at your realm (up to the realm's limit) fights you once each opening. Win, and your dao foundation deepens.
  - **The Heart of the Realm:** its greatest treasure, an ancient work a grade above the realm's, with stones and fame. Taking it makes the realm fall: it seals a month later.
  - **A hidden vault:** off the garden, and not on your map until you sense it (30 insight into Space, comprehension 70 or Spirit 70). It holds a treasure and stones, once each opening.
  - **Time runs differently** in some realms: twice as fast, so a month inside is two months of cultivation, or half as fast. The Location tab says which.
  - **Shut in:** a realm that seals with you inside keeps you until its next opening, unless you tear your way out (60 insight into Space, and a wound to the soul). The realm's limit on who may enter holds inside too.
  - **The old delve, room by room, is still there** at the entrance.
- **Puppets have roles, mend themselves and are built from parts.**
  - **Roles** (Inventory, from 10 puppetry): a Striker hits a fifth harder. A Defender guards you unless told otherwise, steps into 60% of the blows meant for you (not 40%) and wears 30% less. A Gatherer finds half again as much on errands and strikes a fifth softer. The world's puppeteers string theirs too.
  - **Mending:** from 20 puppetry, puppets you carry regain a twentieth of your skill in integrity each month (5 at 100).
  - **Parts:** a puppet is its arms (power), frame (durability) and joints (agility). From 30 puppetry, graft one part of another puppet into it (a month; the donor is spent). A lesser-grade part carries less, and only a master keeps all of it.
- **The black market.** Every city now hides one, and it is the only place demonic wares are sold: manuals of demonic arts, soul banners, demonic gu and poisons, at half again their worth. Ordinary shops no longer stock them (a demonic sect's own shop aside). It opens to a mask, infamy 30 or a demonic method, buys demonic wares at 60%, and the world's demonic cultivators buy their arts there.
- **The assassins' guild.** It meets behind the black market.
  - **Hire a blade** against anyone below the clouds: 500 stones for Qi Condensation, four times as much each realm above, never returned. Within the year a blade at least as strong goes after them. If it fails, the target may learn who paid. An undeserved death stains your karma.
  - **Be a blade:** with infamy 20 or a mask, join and take contracts on those no more than a realm above you. Kill the target and the guild pays.
  - **The world hires it too,** against its enemies and against you: the blade comes as a knife in the dark.
- **Immortals reach below the clouds.**
  - **Humbling a sect:** a sect with three times the might of the next and two of Deity Transformation may draw an avatar of the heavens down on it. If the avatar wins, two of its greatest fall and half its treasury burns. A sect you lead is only watched.
  - **Lifting:** an immortal can lift one of their own (a descendant, disciple or dao companion of Deity Transformation) into the Upper Realm without a tribulation. It costs 100,000 stones, once in ten years.
  - **Sending stones down:** an immortal can send stones to the clan or sect they came from.
  - The world's immortals do all three. Above the clouds, the Location tab's **Reach below the clouds...** lets you lift and send.
- **A smaller batch:**
  - **Detailed combat log** (a toggle on the battle screen): every blow says the damage it did, and each round lists everyone's health and qi.
  - **Talisman clusters:** from 40 in the talisman craft, bind three of one talisman into a cluster that burns in one turn for 2.5 times one alone. The world's talisman masters do too.
  - **Spirit wine:** a beast with hands (an ape, any hand-bearing beast, or one in human shape) can brew it in the wilds from three spirit herbs. That gives two cups, drunk like a qi pill.
  - **Gu rarity and breeding:** every gu names its rarity (Common to Legendary, by quality). Two of one brood breed young in three months on one feeding, now and then a rarer one. The world's gu masters breed theirs. Gu were already used from the battle screen's Bag.
  - **Demonic arrays have names:** an array fed souls takes a name from what it does (Ten-Thousand-Souls, Soul-Drinking, Bone-Walled, Ghost-Veiled), and its preview says what it burns.
  - **Secret bodies for the world:** the world's cultivators who earn a secret body are now remade into it. Before, they were only checked when they travelled.

## 0.13.49 (2026-11-20)

- **The mortal ways go past their summit.** From several players' proposals: martial artists who rise by understanding, not by resources.
  - **Three new tiers on each way, above Peak Grandmaster:**
    - Martial: Three Flowers Gather at the Summit, Five Energies Return to the Origin, Ultimate Pinnacle.
    - Shaman: Heaven-Hearing Shaman, Shaman of the Myriad Spirits, Spirit Sovereign.
  - **Rising by understanding:** no one tests you there. Gather the tier's force and understand enough, and you rise on your own, with no bottleneck. A martial artist needs the Dao of the Body or a weapon's dao (Sword, Saber, Spear, Bow), a shaman the Dao of Soul: 30 insight and comprehension 55 for the first, 50 and 65 for the second, 70 and 75 for the last.
  - **Strength:** they fight as Foundation Building begins, as its peak, and as Golden Core begins (a shaman two thirds of that), and can judge the strength of a Golden Core cultivator.
  - **Ageing:** from Five Energies the rebuilt body starts to fail thirty years later; Ultimate Pinnacle adds thirty years of life. The realm stays Mortal throughout.
  - **Drill until the tier is full** (Arts tab) runs the drills month after month in one go, instead of a click a month.
- **A new world option: earned paths.** When forging a world, you can choose that the Body and Soul paths are earned (off unless chosen; a harder start).
  - The Body path opens only to an Early Grandmaster of the Martial way (or a grandmaster of the martial calling), or from the sixth layer of Qi Condensation.
  - The Soul path opens only to an Early Great Shaman, with Spirit 75, or from Golden Core on another path.
  - A path chosen when your life begins is yours as before, and the world's own people are not held to it.

- **A jewel's spirit can resonate with its holder.** The first part of a player's proposal for jewel spirits that guide rather than fight. Besides commanding its holder or fighting beside them, a spirit in a jewel can now stay within its vessel. It strikes no one, and its holder fights by their own judgement. Each round it may lend one resonance, paid from its own qi, with a wait before it can be lent again:
  - **Forewarning:** the holder is harder to hit for two rounds (by 15, and 5 more for each realm of the spirit's soul, at most 45).
  - **Ward:** a shell of soul light over the holder (10 at Qi Condensation, three times as much each realm, at most 400).
  - **Steady the mind:** a stun thrown off, and none takes hold for two rounds.
  - **Pour qi:** 30% of the holder's qi back, once a fight.

  Choose it on the Location tab ("In battle:" now turns through three ways for a jewel).
- **A jewel's spirit keeps a repository, and can become a house's ancestral treasure.** The second and third parts of the same proposal.
  - **The repository:** a jewel keeps every art it knows and every design it remembers: four, plus three for each grade of its vessel and two for each realm of its soul. Once its holder trusts it (regard 20), it can study their arts (as long as learning them takes) and designs (a month each). It teaches what it keeps to whoever it guides, no art more than a grade above their realm, and they think the better of it.
  - **The ancestral treasure:** a holder of a sect or clan who holds the spirit dear (regard 60) can enshrine it in their house's hall, at its seat. There it teaches any of the house who come to the seat, and they cultivate faster there (3% for each realm of its soul, at most 20%). It leaves the hall only in the hands of a bearer it chooses, or of the winner of a trial the house holds.
  - **Thieves:** each year one in a hundred comes for an enshrined treasure, more as its fame grows (at most one in ten). A house whose strongest at the seat do not outmatch the thief may lose it, and the thief carries it off into the wilds. A fallen house's hall goes to its conquerors with its treasury.

  It all sits on the Location tab beside your vessel. The Guide's "Artifact spirits" topic and two Glossary terms explain it. In battle the resonances replace the arts, with Wait a round, Auto-fight (which lends what each moment asks) and the holder's orders. No blow is aimed at the vessel while the holder stands. The Guide's "Artifact spirits" topic and a new Glossary term explain it.

- **A sect's master can draw on its treasury.** A sect or clan you led could pile up millions that no one could touch, while you went without. Now the Sect tab has "Take from the treasury":
  - 2% of the treasury a year is the master's due, and no one remarks on it.
  - Past that, every share you take costs approval of your rule (the whole treasury at once would cost 40).
  - What you took past the due is owed. Each year it is not given back, the faction thinks a little worse of you (up to 10 approval a year), and a fifth of it is forgotten.
  - Giving stones to the treasury repays it first.

  The picker offers the year's due, shares of the treasury or any sum, each with its cost, and asks before anything costly.

- **The world's strong are grown in it, not dropped into it.** Followed for 200 years, a world's Golden Cores needed about 400 years of their own cultivation to reach the bottleneck, with about 300 left to live, so 62% never would. Only two or three of them became Nascent Souls, and none reached Deity Transformation. The top of the world was held by those it began with, while a third of the wanderers it rolled in to keep its numbers up came at Foundation Building or higher. Now:
  - Cultivators sit in seclusion more of the year the higher their realm: 15% more for each realm past Qi Condensation (a Golden Core 85% of the year).
  - A wandering cultivator of Golden Core or above no longer drifts from town to town. They keep to the densest unclaimed ground within a step, and come into town about one year in twenty.
  - Wanderers come only at Qi Condensation or Foundation Building.

  Over 600 years in three worlds, every Golden Core alive at the end had grown in the world, about two thirds of the Nascent Souls had, and a third to half of the Deity Transformations (the rest are the long-lived ones the world began with). Before, no one grew into Deity Transformation at all.

- **The breakthrough warns you before you leave the hidden stage behind.** Pressing Breakthrough with the hidden stage open to you, but not held, broke through at once, and a Heavenly realm was lost to one wrong press. Now it asks first. It says that only the hidden stage leads to a Heavenly realm, what it adds, and how much qi gathered against its seal would be lost. The quick breakthrough asks too.
- **The Thousand-Faces Body hides every demonic art.** It kept infamy off demonic cultivation, but soul banners, raised corpses, giving form to a demonic art, soul-searching and souls bound into arrays still spread your name. None of them do now.
- **Attribute pills no longer swallow what training earned.** A pill's limit was measured against the whole attribute, so a martial calling's +10 Strength, taken before the pills, filled their room and was lost. Ranks of a martial calling and the gifts of a breakthrough pushed past its goal now stand outside what pills reach.
- **Arts of the highest grades give rerolls and locks again.** Rerolls and locks come from insight past what an art's grade needs, but insight stops at 100. A seventh-grade art left two points of room, so a Golden Immortal got one lock in seven. Where a grade leaves less than forty points, each point past its need now counts for more, so full insight is always worth at least forty points past the need: about nine rerolls and four locks. Lower grades are unchanged.
- **Choose which path your devouring feeds.** After a fight, a demonic cultivator with more than one demonic path, or a beast walking several paths, has a "Devouring feeds" button beside Kill or Devour. A path shut at its bottleneck passes the essence on to the next one that can take it.
- **The fields take only the blood essence they need.** Feeding the fields poured in the whole stack, four hundred pieces to ripen herbs a few years short. Now only the pieces that ripen the slowest field are used, and the picker says how many.
- **Storming a sect your own was at war with earns contribution.** You took its vault, but your sect gave you nothing. Now you earn 500 contribution, plus 60 for each of its people and its prestige, as well as standing with your sect and its approval. A righteous sect counts a demonic sect's fall too.
- **A leader risen above the clouds can claim land there.** Land could only be claimed next to land the faction already held, and a sect below had none above. In a world where your faction holds nothing yet, its first claim is the land you stand on, and it grows by its borders from there. The Sect tab offers it.
- **No mortals to render above the clouds.** Every city above counted 30,000 mortals for blood refining and massacres, where no one is below Deity Transformation. They count none now.
- **Puppets by skill.** Everyone worked two puppets at once, a novice and a Golden Immortal alike. Now you work one, plus one for every 30 puppetry (four at 90), plus one with Many Strings. From 50 puppetry your finest puppet is already out when a fight begins, and from 80 your two finest.
- **Gu say what they do, and Stone-Skin is worth its turn.** A battle gu now shows its strength in its description: a Moonlight Gu's blade, a Stone-Skin Gu's shell, a venom's harm per round and its rounds. Stone-Skin's shell is twice as thick (up to 300).
- **Blood Essence, Flesh Iron and Soul Pearls are kinds of their own.** They were filed with the herbs and ores. Now they have their own entry in the bag's material filter and its groups, and in the inventor's.
- **The inner world's pour picker asks the element first.** Pouring an element into a land listed every material without saying its element. With materials of several elements, you now pick the element first (with how many stacks of each). Each line names its element.
- **Places drawn on top of each other can be told apart.** Hovering one names the others there. Clicking again picks the next one, so a sect hidden under your own land can be reached.
- **A beast line's evolutions at character creation.** Each line now lists the forms it can grow into, with the realm and blood purity each asks, and whether it flies.
- **The bag and the arts use the left column on a PC.** As on the Professions page: the bag's kinds, sort, filters and search, and the Arts tab's paths, stand in the left column while the tab is open. Your status comes back on every other tab. Tablets and phones keep their layout.
- **The Guide breathes.** A long paragraph is broken at each of its bold leads, so a topic reads as sections instead of a wall. Searching the Guide finds the smaller sections too.
- **A long message can be closed from the top.** The karma ledger and other long texts show a Close button above the text as well as Continue at the bottom.

## 0.13.48 (2026-11-20)

- **Pills, materials, spirit stones and devouring feed the seal while you press past your peak.** At the bottleneck, everything but your own cultivation was turned away ("break through first"), even while you pressed for the hidden stage. Now, while you press, a qi pill, an absorbed herb or core, a draw of spirit stones, a beast's devouring, a demonic method's devouring, a dao companion's cultivation and an avatar's all go against the seal at half their worth, as your cultivation does. The pill's preview and the absorb message say so. Once the seal's qi is full, or when you stop pressing, the bottleneck is shut as before. The world's cultivators press the same way.
- **A beast witnesses the births and deaths of its own.** "Witness the birth or the death of someone close to you" never counted a beast's mate, its pack or its young. A person's own child never counted either: only news of someone else's child did. Now the bottleneck is met by the birth of your own child or the child of anyone close to you (a cub raised with your mate, a hatched egg), and by the death of your kin, dao companion, master, disciple, or, for a beast, its mate, its pack or its master.
- **A ghost or a seized body no longer dies of old age at once.** A soul that lingered as a ghost kept the age it died at, but began again at Qi Condensation with that realm's span: a soul of four centuries died the month it rose. A ghost now counts its years from the day it rises, as a young cultivator's. A soul that seizes a body cut down to a lower realm now has at least the years its host had left.
- **The World Bearer's seal fills.** On the faith road the seal above your peak fills as your world's four measures are carried a quarter past whole, but the laws can only be woven as many as the world holds: one more than World Emperor asks is a fifth past whole, so from World Emperor on the seal never filled. The laws now count past whole as far as the daos the world holds allow (every one woven and grasped). Pressing now shows the seal at once instead of after the year's turn, and the "Past your peak" window names the measure that holds the seal back and what it waits on.
- **The breakthrough quality names your key treasure.** Its row read "carry the realm's key treasure" without saying which; it now names it (a Calming Lotus, a Foundation Establishment Flower, a beast core of the grade).
- **Rogues at a sect's gate are visitors.** Anyone at a sect's mountain gate was "on sect duty" or "sparring with fellow disciples", rogues and members of other sects alike. Only the sect's own do its duties now; others wait at the gate, seek an audience or trade.

## 0.13.47 (2026-11-19)

- **An ancient's avatar has to get past your clan's defences before it reaches your people.** When an ancient's rage fell on a clan, the avatar simply killed one or two of its people. It ignored the guardian beasts at its home, its formations and any protecting avatar, and you were told only of the dead. Now:
  - It meets the home's Great Guarding Formation first, then any abode's formations and guards there, as any raider does. They may turn it back, and a killing formation may scatter it.
  - Past them, it shatters the guardian array, and the clan's guardian beasts fight it together. Three guardians at the peak of Deity Transformation scattered it in about a third of raids, and now and then left it too hurt to harm anyone. An avatar of an ancient is still far stronger than any one of them.
  - A protecting avatar can snatch a clan member, or a kinsman the ancient comes for, from death.
  - The chronicle tells each step: what held, who fought, who fell, who was saved.
- **Causality can tell you who leads a sect, a clan or a kingdom.** The new reading "Seek who leads" names a faction's master, its ancestor, its founder and its elders. For a kingdom it names the cultivator overlord, their heir and the National Preceptor, beside the mortal on the throne. Read clearly, each is marked on the map where they stand now; the strong veil theirs.
  - A person's sheet, their line in Relations and their row among the people at a place now show any seat they hold ("Sect Master of the Azure Cloud Sect", "Overlord of the Great Jade Kingdom", "Named heir of…").
  - The Sect tab's list of known factions names each one's leader.
- **Sects no longer war on those far above or below them.** A sect left with a few Qi Condensation disciples was warred on again and again, by sects up to Deity Transformation, until it held only mortals. Now no sect or clan goes to war with one of eight times its might, or an eighth of it. Blood feuds, coveted veins and a lord's punishment all follow this rule. A war that comes to that lapses. A sect far too weak to refuse a demand to bow simply bows.
  - A sect or clan left with no one past Qi Condensation seals its mountain gate. Its wars end, no one marches on it, and its overlord's wars pass it by, until one of its own reaches Foundation Building again. Over 150 years of history, about one sect in five sits sealed at any time.
  - A vassal is marched only into its overlord's wars that are fit for it.
  - A sect or clan you lead still makes the wars you choose.
- **The Professions page has room to breathe.** On a PC, the crafts, the parts of the chosen craft and the inventor's sort, filters and draft now stand in the left column while the Professions tab is open, in place of your status. The craft's own page gets the whole centre. The crafts sit two to a line, the chosen one pressed, and each one's skill is in its tooltip. Your status comes back on every other tab. Tablets and phones keep the layout they had. (From a player's mockup.)

## 0.13.46 (2026-11-19)

- **The world's cultivators use what a cultivator has, as far as their talent carries them.** Four in five cultivators of Foundation Building were rogues who could buy only the common Yellow manuals. The rest kept the first method their sect handed them, its lowest, whatever their root. So geniuses mostly practised a method of the wrong element, methods and arts sat a grade or two below their realm, and almost no one walked two paths. Now:
  - A sect teaches the best method it has for a newcomer's root, and the best attack art their rank opens.
  - Cultivators leave a method for one a fifth faster (a whole grade above was not enough).
  - From Foundation Building, they now and then come upon an art of their own grade, the gifted far more often.
  - Heaven's assessment from the creation screen now shapes everyone. The genius often walks two paths, and the rarest talents all three. The gifted devise arts of their own from Foundation Building.
  - Everyone who has built a foundation practises a craft, as far as their talent goes, and is named by it beside their realm once it reaches 30 ("Puppeteer").
  - Measured over two worlds, a Foundation Building genius's method went from about two thirds of a grade below their realm to within a sixth of one. Its element matches their root a third of the time instead of a quarter: a single root has one element, and a better method of another is often worth more. Six in ten geniuses walk two paths. Golden Core geniuses and above went from a grade and a half behind to about two thirds of one.
- **A locked thing stays in your bag when you stock your shop.** Stocking several of a kind took every piece of that name, so a locked tool went onto the shelves with its plain twins. Now locked things are left out of the shop's list, and stocking, consigning to the auction or selling to a city's shops refuses them and says so, as the market always did.
- **Sealing a demon rift brings no infamy.** Its general was judged like anyone you attack first, so killing it counted as murder: infamy, though the button promised fame and good karma. Now the Demon Realm's generals and the Demon Lord can be slain by anyone without a stain or a grudge, and you get the karma and fame promised.
- **Dead disciples no longer fill your places for disciples.** The cap counted every disciple you ever had, so a master with none alive was told they could not guide more than sixteen. Now only living disciples count (the Sect tab already counted so), for you and for the world's masters, and your records still name everyone you taught.

## 0.13.45 (2026-11-19)

- **The road you choose past Golden Immortal is the road you take.** A failed uniting left one treasure short, and the next breakthrough quietly went on as a World Bearer, with the heavens' edict and a tribulation of your world. Now the chosen road is kept and its gate says what it still lacks. The Character tab names your road and lets you choose again, and going on as a World Bearer asks first, as Three as One does. The Arts tab says why Three Treasures United cannot yet be taken up instead of showing a button that never works.
- **An immortal's martial way no longer fades at sixty.** From sixty even a Golden Immortal lost a martial tier one year in ten and fell back to Apprentice. Now a cultivator's martial way gives ground only in the last seventh of their life.
- **A kingdom you proclaimed stays your house's.** Rebels could topple the throne of your own city while you were away and put a made-up dynasty on it, with no one behind it and no way back. Now a rebellion breaks against a living founder or heir and only shakes the throne. A throne of your city that no cultivator holds can be taken back with the same button ("Take back the throne"), and the world's founders do the same.
- **Those you keep in your inner world stay with you.** A clan calling its people home or a sect's errand carried them off between years: a dao companion kept inside showed up at the clan's estate far below and gave birth there while you stood above the clouds, and disciples inside were attacked, the alarm breaking your seclusion again and again. Now they stand wherever you stand, every part of every month. No fight from outside reaches them, a child born to one of them is born inside, and no child comes of a couple the world keeps apart. The same holds for the world's own keepers.
- **A city's bounty board posts only its own world's devils.** Boards below the clouds listed True Immortals and Immortal Souls living above, since a higher realm's bounty is far larger, and taking such a hunt even set the immortal down below. Now each board lists the wanted of its own side of the clouds, and a hunt never carries anyone across.
- **A secret realm's barrier keeps out your companions above its realm too.** A Deity Transformation companion fought beside you in a realm that let in none above Golden Core. Now companions, tamed beasts, sworn kin and spirits above the barrier's realm wait outside, and the battle says so. A disciple's master above it cannot come to their aid there, and a token cannot call you into one your realm is barred from.
- **A beast devours every foe it beats, the leader included, and the fight's end says how much qi it ate.** The leader of a pack or gang was put to death without counting as your kill, so only the beasts slain before it fed you. Now every beast and person you kill or beat in a fight is devoured, with no stain on your karma, and the victory screen offers "Devour them".
- **A design takes the element its feeders feed.** Two Wood herbs with seven Water cores made a Water pill, because the strongest element always won. Now every element also counts the strength of the materials that feed it, as the five phases say, so the pill comes out Wood and in harmony. This holds in every craft and for every element (Wind, Lightning and Ice count as Wood, Fire and Water). The design screen names the element and what fed it, and each pair with what it adds or takes ("Water feeds Wood +12%", "Lightning (as Fire) overcomes Metal -15%"), in the preview under the materials too.
- **What you carry through the void can still be mended.** Crossing to another world dropped how everything carried was made, so a natal zither could never be repaired or reforged again ("No one knows how it was made"). It also forgot who made it, and a body forged in another world was unknown to this one, which could make a save unreadable ("Unknown physique"). Now the designs, your maker's mark and forged bodies cross with you and with visitors from the shared heavens. Older saves get their designs back where one of the same name is known, and a forged body the world lost comes back as a plain forged physique.
- **A world whose last save was lost can be begun in again.** If the computer stopped right after a save, the world's record of how your last journey left it could be left empty, and the world no longer opened ("'0x00' is an invalid start of a value"). The world now opens from the newest whole save of a journey there, or as it was forged if there is none, and says so. Saves and world records are now written all the way to the disk before they replace the old ones.
- **Grandfather Furnace takes your failures.** A pill made at 11% in the game did not count as "made badly", because a work's quality also rises with how far your skill passes the design. Now a flawed work counts too, and so do works of your earlier lives. When you have nothing to offer, he names the worst thing you made and its quality.
- **A beast drops its own core.** A beast of another element's land (a Thorn Flame Lion) carried the first core of that element at its grade (an Ancient Treant Core). Now it drops a Flame Lion Core.
- **Arts you make start at your own element.** The art maker began at Fire for everyone and hid the element for Body, Soul and Martial methods, so a mortal's martial manuals came out "Inferno Canon" and "Sun Mantra", each giving "+2% power for Fire arts". Now it starts at your own element (none if you have no root), path methods have none, and a martial manual gives Constitution, as a body method does. Manuals already made lose their element.
- **Martial artists and shamans can take a beast's measure.** A mortal way gives no divine sense, so every beast read as "Unfathomable" even to a grandmaster. From Expert (a shaman from Spirit Walker) you see the realm of a Qi Condensation cultivator or beast, and from Early Grandmaster (Early Great Shaman) that of Foundation Building.
- **Sect promotion reads your best path.** Qi Condensation 4 on Qi with Soul chosen first was refused as stage 1. Promotion trials and clan ranks now read the highest stage you hold in your highest realm.
- **A breakthrough's journey asks only for lands of the world you live in.** A world with no desert sent you to the Demon Realm's. Journeys and spirit veins now come from your own world, and a journey already asked for is moved to a land that exists.
- **Forging your own physique says it is once in a life.** Breaking through to a new realm never reopened it, and the greyed button did not say so. It does now, and a body too hurt to forge says how hurt.
- **A spirit that has made itself a body may walk Qi and Body too, and keeps them.** Every load stripped an artifact spirit of every path but Soul, so a manifested spirit's Qi path at peak Golden Immortal was gone each time the game was opened. Only a spirit still bound in its vessel, which has never made flesh, walks the Soul path alone. A spirit with a body of its own may take up Qi Refinement or Body Tempering, and keeps them when it rests in its vessel again (it begins a new one only in its body).
- **Fitting a better prosthetic keeps the old one.** Fitting a prosthetic over one already in place destroyed the old one. It now goes back into your pouch.
- **Exchanging, buying or adding an ingredient keeps the list where it was.** On a long list (a sect's treasury, a craft's materials), the page could jump up after an action, or slide by a row, while its rows were still being measured. The row you were looking at now stays where it was, even when the one above it is gone, and the list of crafts beside the page keeps its scroll too.
- **Music at 0% is silent.** The slider at its lowest still left the music playing very quietly, and a track that began while muted stayed faint after unmuting. At 0% the music now stops, and unmuting brings it back at the slider's volume.
- **Numbers read the same on every computer.** On a machine set to a language that writes decimals with a comma, the game wrote "×1,11" and "3.810 spirit stones". It now always writes "×1.11", "+6.9%" and "3,810".
- **Celestial phenomena read cleanly.** The Location tab showed colour codes around the place and date ("[color=#8a8070](Ruins of the He Clan, ...)[/color]"). They are now dimmed, as intended.

## 0.13.44 (2026-11-19)

- **The defying road breaks through its own way.** Past Golden Immortal, one who makes Qi, Body and Soul one played the games of the road that tends a world: bearing a world's burdens, ruling by decree. Each of the four realms on the defying road now has its own game:
  - Three as One: braid Qi, Body and Soul into one, keeping each from running dry or swelling past holding.
  - Unfettered: break heaven's fetters while its jealousy rises. Hiding your light cools heaven, but tightens every fetter left.
  - Limitless: gather force against limits whose true height you only guess (a stronger divine sense narrows the guess). Strike short of a limit and the blow rebounds on you.
  - Transcendent: sever the nine threads of karma that tie you to the wheel. Each costs the treasure it is tied to, and each one left drains that treasure every round.
- **Each can be pushed past its goal, for the same gift as its rung on the other road:** Charm, years of life, dao foundation, every attribute.

## 0.13.43 (2026-11-19)

- **A crossing into another world that fails no longer ends your life.** Crossing the void marked you as gone from the world you left and saved it so before you had arrived anywhere. If anything went wrong on the far side, you were left behind on the death screen, "died ... crossed the void into the world of ...", with that state already saved. Now nothing is saved until you stand in the other world. If the crossing fails, the void casts you back where you stood, with the stones and the qi you set out with, and the error is written to the log.
