- **Every breakthrough can be pushed past its goal for something that lasts.** Once nine pillars stand, anyone may keep raising pillars, not only one who holds the hidden Tenth Layer, and each pillar past the ninth is +2 dao foundation. The foundation's grid is larger to make room. Every realm has its push: surges past the barrier (Agility), layers packed past a full core (Constitution), memories past the sixth (Spirit), accords past full harmony (Comprehension), and one for each realm above. Each one is harder than the last: the tide grows fiercer, the core hotter, the chain of memories longer. Seal whenever you like (S) and keep them all if the breakthrough holds. A push that breaks loses what was past the goal and a fifth of the result's score, but the breakthrough still stands. The hidden Tenth Layer's tenth pillar still adds a fifth to the foundation's score.
- **The realms past Deity Transformation have breakthroughs of their own.** True Immortal, Golden Immortal and the four realms past it all replayed Deity Transformation's merging with the five phases. Each now has its own game, and each can be pushed for its own gift:
  - True Immortal: burn the mortal dross out of the body without scorching clean flesh (Strength).
  - Golden Immortal: hold the five facets of a golden body against heaven's strikes (Luck).
  - World Bearer: set a world's burdens on your shoulders and spine (Charm).
  - World Emperor: rule by decree without losing Heaven, Earth or the People (years of life).
  - World Sovereign: set a world's laws in order (dao foundation).
  - Dao Ancestor: draw the myriad daos back to the source (every attribute).

## 0.13.42 (2026-11-17)

- **Adding an ingredient keeps the list still.** In the design inventor, the first ingredient that made something added a line above the list (having a shop commission it), and every row below moved down, so the page seemed to scroll up under your finger. The materials list now keeps the row at the top of your view where it stood, whatever appears above it.

## 0.13.41 (2026-11-17)

- **Choosing something in the Professions tab no longer throws the page back to the top.** Adding a material to a design, a filter, a shape or any other pick there rebuilt the page at its top, so a long list of materials had to be scrolled down again after every "+". The page now stays where you were, as every other tab already did (on a phone this tab was not affected).
- **Consign several things to the auction at once.** A city's auction house has a new "Consign several..." beside "Consign to the auction...": tick everything to leave with the house, then consign them all in one go, with the bids expected for the lot. Shift-click ticks every box between the last one you clicked and this one; the same works in the refining pot and when stocking your shop.
- **Lists of boxes to tick fit the window.** With a long description above them, their All, None and confirm buttons ran off the bottom of the screen; the list now shortens to keep them in sight, as one-choice lists already did.

## 0.13.40 (2026-11-17)

- **Enlightenment is rare now: about once in five hundred years.** It came about once in forty years, and with high luck and comprehension every ten or fifteen, so a gifted cultivator owed a large share of their speed to it and long seclusions kept stopping for it. Now it is about once in five hundred years at luck and comprehension 50. Luck and comprehension still make it likelier, but only up to 150 each, so even the most gifted wait about two hundred and twenty years. Each one is as strong as before. The world's cultivators have theirs as rarely.
- **A demonic cultivator can reach the hidden Tenth Layer.** The Tenth Layer asks a dao foundation of 70, both to press past the peak and to break its seal. A demonic method wears the foundation down toward 25, so it was all but closed to demonic cultivators. Now what you have seen of the Abyss (a concept of the Dao of Devouring, which practising a demonic method deepens) holds the foundation up: 25, plus 3 for each point of the Abyss, up to 80. A glimpse of the Abyss (15) is the 70 the seal asks. Breakthroughs read this held-up foundation for their chance, their quality and the minigame. Heaven's lightning still reads the worn foundation itself. The Character tab shows both ("Dao foundation 70 (your own 25, held up by the Abyss...)"), and demonic NPCs press for the Tenth Layer too.
- **A beast's foundation is its blood.** A beast cannot buy a foundation pill and has no master to wash its marrow, so its foundation stayed where it was born. Now its blood's purity holds the foundation up (30, plus 0.7 for each point of purity), wherever that stands higher.
- **Beasts eat hard herbs for their foundation.** A mountain root or a blade grass, eaten raw (Inventory, Eat), lays a little dao foundation, less as it nears 80, and none past it.
- **An elder of your line can wash your blood.** A beast of your own line and a higher realm can drive its blood through yours (Relations, Interact: half a year). It gives as much foundation as a master's marrow washing, once a century, and only if it holds you dear. You can wash your own young the same way once you outgrow them, and the world's beast parents wash theirs.

## 0.13.39 (2026-11-17)

- **Your inner world's board is drawn, and you tend it by clicking.** The Character tab has an Inner World section of its own. The lands lie three to a row as tiles: five columns for each land's phases, bars for plants, herds and beasts of prey, its people, its ward arrays and its qi against what it can reach; a land heading for a disaster is edged in red. Click a land and everything under the board is that land: its five phases on a wheel (the generating cycle round the rim, the overcoming star inside, and the arrow to the element you should pour lit up), bars showing where its qi and life are headed, what the land most wants done in plain words, and every deed as a button that acts on it. The same panel shows on the Location tab when you stand in a land. "Show as text" keeps the old lines.
- **Both roads past Golden Immortal explain themselves.** At the peak of Golden Immortal the Cultivation section draws each road's gate as bars. On the faith road, a line under the four measures says what the lowest one is waiting on (which land, which dao, how many years at this pace), and six bars show how ready your world is for each of heaven's tests. On the defying road, Heaven's Jealousy is five pips that each say who comes and how often, with bars for the quiet years and the wait to the next reckoning. The Guide opens both roads with numbered steps and explains how to read the board; "How the board works" and "How the two roads work" open it at the right page.
- **Inside an inner world entered above the clouds, the Breakthrough button no longer reads "Ascend".**

- **Golden Immortal is no longer the end: four realms lie past it.** At the peak of Golden Immortal the road forks, and no single path leads on. One fork is faith: tend the world within you, and your realm stands on it (World Bearer, World Emperor, World Sovereign, Dao Ancestor). The other is defiance: make Qi, Body and Soul one (Three as One, Unfettered, Limitless, Transcendent). A realm on one road ranks with the same realm on the other. If you stand ready for both, the game asks which; you walk one for the rest of that life. Each gives more years (about 80,000 at the first, where three paths in harmony gave a Golden Immortal about 40,000), has its own hidden stage, and can be formed Heavenly like the realms below. Things of these realms are still of the seventh grade.
- **Three as One: Qi, Body and Soul made one (the defying path).** Bring every one of the three your body can walk to the peak of Golden Immortal, full, and the breakthrough makes them one path with one realm and one bar. A body that closes paths, or one born without a root, brings what it has. Your three methods are fused into one scripture that keeps every trait of all three; a beast, which cultivates by instinct, fuses none. What has no body (a ghost, a spirit in its vessel) cannot unite.
  - The harmonies between paths end there, and grow no further than Golden Immortal for everyone. In their place the one path has the best of each (the Body's toughness and blows, Qi's arts, the Soul's soul power and divine sense) and fights at ×2.1, where three paths walked together fought at ×1.45. The first stage of Three as One comes out about 2.4 times as strong as the peak of Golden Immortal on three paths was (a realm's step), with divine sense about 1.3 times as strong.
  - Until you stand ready, the peak of Golden Immortal is only a peak, as before. The Breakthrough button, the Character tab and the road ahead say what is still missing, path by path. Shaping a world is still offered there, and to anyone past it.
  - The game asks before the uniting, since it cannot be undone. Once one, no single treasure can be crippled or undone, and the scripture cannot be forgotten, refined or displaced by a single path's method.
- **It defies heaven, and heaven answers.** Every tribulation from the uniting on is three trials back to back, one for each treasure, each with half again as many bolts and 15% crueler; the second and third fall on the body the one before left. Defying is one more reason for the heavens' disfavour (still four steps at most), so a defier of great talent or dark karma meets the heart demon and the heavenly avatar sooner.
- **Heaven's Jealousy sends the hunt.** A level from 1 to 5 on everyone who made the three one, shown on the Character tab and at the bottleneck. The uniting sets it to 1; each defiant breakthrough after adds one (two under three steps of disfavour or more); slaying a whole hunting party adds one. Three hundred quiet years lower it by one, never below 1. A concealment art or a Heaven-Veiling Formation hides one level from the hunters, Heaven's Deception two; at 5 nothing does.
  - At 2 a lone hunter of your realm comes every 150 to 300 years; at 3 a party of three every 80 to 160; at 4 a party led by one a realm above you every 40 to 80; at 5 four of the strongest every 20 to 40. They come only above the clouds and never into an inner world, but closed-door seclusion does not keep them out. You can fight or try to flee.
  - The hunters are the faithful: those who went past Golden Immortal the other way, and hunt defiers for heaven's merit.
- **The world's own immortals walk the same road.** Every heavens now has two who made the three treasures one, keeping to its wild places, and three of the faithful in its cities; a journey already under way gains them when it loads. Golden Immortals who are battle-minded or proud and lead no one now and then take up the treasures they lack, bring each to the peak and try the uniting. Heaven's hunters come for them as for you, and the chronicle tells of it.
- **Your inner world is a board of lands now.** It has one land for each of its levels (nine at level 9, three by three). Each land has a terrain, the five phases, qi, plants, grazers, hunters, and perhaps a settlement of mortals. A world you already have gains its lands when the save loads, leaning to what it was fed.
  - Each year the phases flow between neighbouring lands and feed one another round the generating cycle; a land's terrain and the element daos woven into the world's laws push theirs up. Plants grow on qi, wood and water, herds on plants, beasts of prey on herds, and people on what the land can feed.
  - Balance deepens the world's era of qi, an era for every fifty wholly balanced years. Each era lets every land hold more qi and feed many more people.
  - A land whose phases stand more than half apart suffers a disaster of its strongest phase sooner or later: frost, overgrowth, flood, drought or a quake. It can be prevented.
  - On the Character tab, under Inner World, you tend it: pour an element into a land (it also wears down the phase that element overcomes), sow herbs and seeds, stock it from beast cores, settle mortals (followers from a town you founded, refugees from a calamity, or townsfolk taken by force), sink protective formation disks as ward arrays, bless a land for a month, scatter its strongest phase or cull its animals, and hunt its beasts.
  - Where a land holds a thousand people or more, a child with a spiritual root is found among them now and then and grows up in your world. No spirit stones come out of an inner world.
- **The faith path: your realm stands on your world.** The gate is a path full at the peak of Golden Immortal, an inner world grown to its fullest (level 10), and a thousand mortals living in it. Past Golden Immortal, cultivation, pills and veins add nothing to that path: its stage is the lowest of the world's four measures, shown as four bars on the Character tab. They are its land (the average qi of its lands), its laws (daos woven in, as deeply as you grasp them), its people and its eras. Whole in all four is the bottleneck; carried a quarter past whole, the hidden stage's seal is ready. Each realm adds two lands to the board and one dao to what its laws hold. A faithful cultivator who walks several paths keeps their harmonies as they stood at Golden Immortal.
- **Heaven's tribulations strike the world, not you.** One falls at every breakthrough on the faith path and one every five hundred years: lightning, a flood or drought or frost, a blight, a beast tide, a heresy or an invasion. Each breaks first on what you have built (ward arrays and laws answer lightning, balance a flood, alchemy a blight, devotion a heresy, wards and your world's own cultivators a tide or an invasion), and each can be answered in the moment: take the lightning on your own body, pour the element that overcomes the flood, give out healing pills, walk among the heretics for a year or put them down, or meet the tide or the invader in battle. Withstood, it deepens the era by a quarter; lost, it takes people, life or wards, and your stage falls with them. Heaven's merit, earned by hunting those who made the three treasures one, shields your world.
  - From World Emperor you set how your world's era turns (even, flourishing or still) and may choose which tribulation tests it next.
- **Keepers among the world's own.** Heads of clans and sects tend their inner worlds: they feed them what they carry, even their lands, sow, stock and settle them. One whose world is whole and peopled takes the faith road, heaven tests their world on the same schedule, and the chronicle tells of it. The faithful above the clouds each stand on a world of their own.
- **An unfolded world becomes up to three places.** A town of its people where any are settled, with you as its founder; a pocket of folded space where Space or Time is woven into its laws; and its richest land as wilds or a spirit vein. A world with no settlement and neither dao still comes out as one place. The world's own unfold theirs too, rarely, and never into a region that already holds three unfolded places. A world a realm stands on cannot be unfolded.
- **The Upper Realm is one living realm for all your journeys.** Its lands, sects and founders were already the same in every journey; now their fates are too. A native of the heavens who dies, rises a realm, makes the three treasures one or takes the faith road in one journey has done so in every other, and those born or arrived there while you played one life are found above the clouds in the next. The heavens keep a calendar of their own that moves with every year you play, so loading another journey, or an older save, does not undo what happened above: the game tells you what passed while you lived another life. Nothing is ever taken back or lowered. A native's belongings, arts and ties to people stay each journey's own. Those who ascended from a world below still belong to that journey and visit the others, as before. Heavens made before 0.13.7, or brought from another machine, stay as they are.
- **The sects above hold land and go to war.** A sect whose leader lives claims free wild places, spirit veins and ruins near its gate (three, and one more for every fifty prestige), and its members cultivate in its immortal qi there as at its gate. When the heavens are at peace two sects now and then go to war, the upright against the fallen where both stand. Each year of a war their strongest meet; the winner's sect gains prestige and takes one of the loser's holdings, and three wins or fifteen years end it. If your own sect is at war you are called to its field now and then: beat the other side's champion for 600 merit and a win in the war. A sect's gate shows its prestige, its holdings and its war.
- **A World Sovereign has anchors.** On the faith road you may shape one more world for each realm past Golden Immortal. From World Sovereign every world you shaped is an anchor: when death comes, an anchor falls in your place and you stand again at the heart of the heavens, and you fall only when none is left. Old age and a shattered soul cannot be held off this way.
- **A Dao Ancestor makes their world one with a dao.** The dao must be woven into the world's laws and made whole in you. Each land can then hold 20 more qi, everyone living in it comes to that dao three times as fast, and those who ascend from the worlds you shaped arrive in your world, not in the Upper Realm. It cannot be undone.
- **Shaping a world has its own button.** It was reached through the Breakthrough button at the very peak. Now "Shape a world..." is on the Character tab under Inner World, greyed with what it still asks, and Breakthrough only breaks through.
- **A people can be shaped from above the clouds.** The faith road asks for a thousand mortals in your world, yet no mortal lives above the clouds and no town can be founded there, so a Golden Immortal who never left the heavens had no way to begin. Now "Settle people" has a fourth way that needs no town: shape a first people out of your own dao. It makes 300 people in a land, once a year, for 5 years of your life; fed and at peace they reach the thousand by themselves within a few years. The gate's own reason names it. Towns above the clouds stay closed: mortals still cannot live under the heavens' qi.
- **Every land of your inner world is a place you can stand in.** Step in and you stand in its first land; "Walk to another land..." (Location and Character tabs) crosses to any other in no time. The Location tab shows the land you stand in, and everything a keeper does to a land (pour, sow, stock, settle, ward, bless, teach, scatter, hunt) is there as a button for that land, greyed with the reason where it cannot be done, along with gathering what grew. Stepping out from any land comes out where you went in. An abode can stand only in the first land. The world's own keepers, whose worlds nobody walks, add nothing to the map.
- **Teach in a land.** A month teaching where people live: everyone named who lives in your world learns its woven daos and half a year of their own road, devotion rises by 3, and a land of a thousand or more may bring a child with a spiritual root forward. The world's own keepers teach theirs each year.
- **The heavens reckon with defiers on a schedule.** Besides the tribulations of your breakthroughs, every 500 years from the uniting the heavens come for you wherever you are: one trial of your own realm's lightning at the strength kept for those who defy heaven. Face it bolt by bolt or bear it as it falls. Come through and your heart is steadier; fail and you are scorched, your foundation cracks by 10 and half of what you had gathered toward the next stage scatters. The Character tab says how many years remain. The world's own defiers bear theirs too, and some die of it.
- **The heavens have calamities of their own.** About every 150 years, one at a time: a beast tide round a beast lord, which stays where it rose, or a devourer, a fallen Golden Immortal who walks from place to place. Its heart stands at the peak of Golden Immortal and falls on the immortals near it each year; now and then the strongest of the heavens goes to face it. It ends slain, or spent after 40 years. Every place above shows where it is, and where it stands you can face it: ending it is 500 fame and 1,500 merit in your immortal sect.
- **Immortals of one name are a house above the clouds.** Three or more of one surname living above are a house, seated where most of them stand, its strongest the ancestor. The ancestor guides those beneath them each year (three months of cultivation each), and now and then two houses quarrel and their strongest beneath the ancestors settle it. You belong to your own surname's house while you live above, and are its ancestor if you are its strongest. A house's seat shows on the Location tab and your own on the Character tab.
- The Guide has a new topic, Past Golden Immortal, and "The inner world" now explains the board. The Glossary knows Three as One, Heaven's Jealousy, the faithful, a world's four measures, its tribulations, its lands and the living heavens.

## 0.13.38 (2026-11-17)

- **Render its mortals and Slaughter its mortals finally appear.** Both need a town or city, but they were only ever offered in the wilds, so neither could be found anywhere, and no one could make Soul Pearls. They now sit in a town's "Sects, thrones and land" actions.
- **Actions you cannot take yet show greyed, with what they need.** About thirty actions stayed hidden until you reached a realm, insight, rank or art, so players could not tell they existed. Now they show greyed out, and hovering (or holding on a touch screen) says what each one asks. Among them:
  - Location: Render its mortals, Raise a Dao Abode, Claim this land, Fold a secret realm, Remake the land, Tear a rift into another world, Walk the Yellow Springs, Become a guest elder, tearing a sealed realm open, Raise a grand formation, and a beast's Found a tribe.
  - Character: Enter the hall of fame, Shape a person, Open your inner world, Forge your own physique, Found your own bloodline.
  - Talking to someone: a token of protection, courting a consort, swaying their mind, debating the dao, sending them on an errand, and taking a junior as your disciple.
  - Elsewhere: absorbing spirit stones, waking a weapon's spirit, asking for the leader's seat, and the union market's reason for being closed.
  - Breakthrough and Quick breakthrough always show; until the bottleneck, Breakthrough says how much qi you have gathered toward it.
  - In battle, a poison with no weapon in hand, or a corpse your art cannot raise now, stays in the bag greyed instead of vanishing, and "Unfold domain" shows greyed until you can.
  - On the death screen, a new row lists the ways of living on this soul could not take (a ghost, an artifact, seizing a body, reincarnation), each with why.
- **"Seize their body" shows for a soul cultivator who cannot do it yet.** It was meant to show greyed from Soul Golden Core, but it checked for wording no reason used, so it vanished.
- Actions that are not yours to take stay out of sight: a beast's actions for a person, the other half of Join and Leave, and things that are not there this month.

## 0.13.37 (2026-11-17)

- **You can write demonic alchemy and forging scriptures.** The Guide said a cultivator with enough Yin insight could write a Blood Refining (alchemy) or Flesh Forging (forging) scripture, but when you composed a craft art, the Demonic box only appeared for puppetry. Now it appears for alchemy and forging too. Its note says what the scripture does: in a town or city, it lets you render mortals into Blood Essence or Flesh Iron.

## 0.13.36 (2026-11-16)

- **No more attacks that cost no qi from jade slips and fallen souls.** A beast's natural attack (its fangs, horn or breath) is part of its body and costs it no qi. It was also kept in the world's list of arts, so it could reach people as if it were an art: in random jade slips (a skeleton's, a buried cache's, a false senior's), from a soul that failed to seize you, from a soul search, in a dead beast's tomb, at auctions, and in the library a beast in human form founded. Whoever learned it then struck for no qi at all. Now no jade slip, memory or library holds a beast's natural attack, and no one born a person can learn it. A beast in human form keeps its own, but cannot write it down or teach it to a person. Anyone born a person who already knows one loses it when the save loads, and it leaves sect libraries and inheritances too.

## 0.13.35 (2026-11-16)

- **Not everyone will humour you.** A Qi Condensation junior could debate the dao with a Deity Transformation elder as with anyone. Now those two realms or more above you refuse to debate with you at all, and three realms or more above refuse to teach you. Nearer than that, whether they agree is a chance shown on the button. Your charm and their regard raise it; each realm they stand above you lowers it; the humble lean toward yes, the arrogant and the reclusive toward no. It is decided once a month, so asking again the same month changes nothing. Kin, your master or disciples, dao companions, sworn siblings and a heart you have won always agree.
- **A devoted heart reaches a realm further.** A dao companion proposal was refused across more than two realms, however much they cared. Now one whose regard, with how well you suit each other, is 90 or more accepts across three.
- **Charm cuts both ways.**
  - From 65 charm, unwed cultivators of about your realm who think well of you court you with gifts now and then: a fifth of years at 100 charm.
  - Anyone who cares for you (regard 60) tends your wounds with a pill or a healing art when they find you hurt.
  - The wicked notice beauty too. From 70 charm, a ruthless or demonic elder two realms above you may come to bind you as their dao companion, as you could bind one far beneath you. Bow your head, refuse and fight, or try to flee.
- **Your companions are not attacked under your nose.**
  - One who would fall on a companion beside you now weighs your whole party first, and thinks better of it if you are the stronger. The arrogant court death anyway.
  - When an attack does come, you are asked: step in and you all fight them, or stand aside. It used to be settled out of sight, with only a message afterwards.
- **Your allies fight on when you fall.** A fight ended as lost the moment you were knocked out, even with a pack of beasts or a party of companions still standing. Now they fight on without you to the end. If they win, the fight is won.
- **An enlightenment fades after ten years.** It used to wait forever, so players poured it into a craft they did not use just to clear the slot. The picker and the button say when it will fade.
- **Fewer guardians on treasures.** Gathering anything but herbs past Heaven grade was a fight for every one or two. Now two finds in five are guarded at Heaven grade (it was three in five), and a little over half above it (it was four in five). A concealment art hides you from the guardian, down to about one in four at its best.
- **Unfolding a domain costs no turn.** A domain took your whole turn, and for most daos it was not worth one. Now you unfold it (V) and still act that round. The world's fighters unfold theirs the same way.
- **Commissions pay for their materials.** Clients bring nothing for a crafted order, but the pay covered only the work. Now it adds what six units of the grade's materials cost at market.
- **Remaking land asks the land's own dao, and its holders answer.**
  - Any biome could be made with Earth or Space alone, anywhere, with no one objecting. Now the new land's own dao is needed at 60 too.
  - On land that is not yours, a year in, the sect or clan that holds it sends its strongest to stop you, or the land's beasts come. Only a victory finishes the work, and the house will not forget it.

- **Less scrolling on the Location, Map, Inventory and Professions tabs.**
  - **Location, Market:** the market's parts each have their own page, with only one shown at a time: Trade, Union halls, Beast pens, Bounties and caravans, and Hire, where the rogues for hire are listed with a Hire button each.
  - **Map:** the side panel has views: Map (the selected place and the legend), Places of interest, Qi and Dao. Qi colours every place by its qi and lists the richest. Dao colours places by how readily the dao you pick is contemplated there, marks that dao's abodes, and lists the best land for it.
  - **Inventory:** every item takes two lines, its name and its main effect; click it to see everything else and its buttons, or open every row at once. Folded groups take one line. The Shops tab's rows are still the long ones.
  - **Professions:** on a PC the crafts stand in a bar down the left, each with its skill, and the craft's page beside it (phones and tablets keep the row on top). Each craft is split into parts: Crafting, Design, Working materials and Union (Herbalism: Fields, Working materials, Cross strains). Only the one you pick is shown, and each craft remembers it. In the design inventor, the sort and filter pickers sit in the bar pinned at the bottom of the page, so they never scroll out of reach.
- **Heart demons linger, and must be faced.** A heart demon in a breakthrough used to cost a little stability and be gone. Now a failed breakthrough into Golden Core or above may leave one behind as a lingering wound. It is likelier the greater the realm and the less steady your dao heart.
  - While it lingers, it takes up to 25% from every breakthrough and holds the hidden stage's seal shut.
  - No pill or physician reaches it. Face it yourself (a month, from the right column): your dao heart decides it, with your spirit and comprehension. If you lose, it grows. A steady dao heart (60 and up) also wears it down slowly in seclusion.
  - The world's cultivators carry and face theirs too.
- **Rebuild any path, not only Qi, and two new pills to do it.** Crippling yourself to lay a perfect foundation again worked only on the Qi path. "Undo a path's cultivation" (Character tab) now does it for Body and Soul too.
  - **The Qi path** can be shattered at once (your dantian is wounded and must mend), or dissolved in seclusion, six months a realm, unhurt.
  - **Body and Soul** cannot be torn down by will: tempered flesh and a built soul unwind only on their pill, in seclusion, six months a realm, unhurt. A **flesh-dissolving pill** (alchemy: Essence with Toxic) unwinds the Body path; a **soul-unbinding pill** (Spirit with Swift) unwinds the Soul path. The pill must be of the realm's grade or finer, and it is spent. It has its own button in the Inventory too.
  - Either way, everything on that path is lost, flaws included.
- **Beasts no longer fill the high realms.** In a 1,200-year save, 97% of the Nascent Souls and 95% of the Deity Transformations were beasts in human form. Every member of a beast tribe took human form at Nascent Soul, and tamed beasts came through every tribulation untouched with +30% to every breakthrough. Now:
  - A tribe's beasts stay beasts.
  - A beast kept by a person takes human form on its own about one year in ten, instead of one in five.
  - A tamed beast's master steadies its breakthroughs by 15%, and from Nascent Soul it faces its tribulations like anyone.
  - In an 800-year test world, beasts in human form among the Nascent Souls fell from 530 to 8, and there are twice as many human Nascent Souls.
- **A token of protection.** From Nascent Soul, give someone close to you a token holding a sliver of your soul (Interact; two at Nascent Soul, one more each realm). When anyone attacks them, the token calls you if you can reach them: anywhere in your region, or anywhere in your world with 60 insight into Space. Answer it and you are there, fighting beside them. Out of reach, the token flares once, drives the attacker off, and is spent.
- **Masters answer for their disciples.** Attack someone's disciple, and their master may come to stand by them: if they are in the same region, stronger than the disciple, no more than a realm beneath you, and not cautious.
- **The Hall of Fame.** An immortal of yours (True Immortal, or ascended) can enter the Hall of Fame from the Character tab. The main menu's new Hall of Fame shows each one's face, name, world and the road they took: their realms and how they were formed, their daos, their sect, their destinies, their fame and their karma. It is kept beside the saves, so it outlives every journey.
- **Keep a design book.** With "Keep a design book" on in Settings, your own designs are kept beside the saves at every save, and every new life starts knowing them. Only designs whose materials exist in the new world come with you; a strain bred elsewhere does not.
- **An open demon rift can become an invasion.** A rift left open a year or more may widen. The Demon Realm's warlord and two captains then pour through it as a calamity. Seal rifts while you can.
- **A great beast tide devours a town.** A tide of ten or more Nascent Soul beasts that breaks into a town now leaves only a sixth of its people, where an ordinary tide leaves three in five, and shakes its kingdom harder. Few below its realm live through it.
- **Towns fall to ruins.** A great tide that leaves a town with no one turns it into the Ruins of its name. Its kingdom loses it (a fallen capital's court moves to its largest town, or the kingdom ends); its shops, stalls and auction close and their wares lie in the ruins to be searched for, tills going back to their keepers; houses, gatherings, requests and orders there end, and what an order's shop still held of your price comes back; a craft union's hall moves to another city, the beast pens break open, a sect seated there moves or scatters, and those who lived there go to the nearest town. Build an abode in the ruins and "Raise it again": the town rises where it stood, under its old name.
- **Treasures fall from above the clouds.** About once in fifty years, a Celestial-grade work lost in the immortals' battles falls into the wilds below. The chronicle tells where it fell, and it is marked among your places of interest for whoever gets there first.
- **A glimpse of an immortal.** Rarely, out in the open, an immortal passes overhead with their dao unhidden. Gaze upon them and you understand your deepest dao a little as they do, more the stronger your soul. The sight burns, and a weak soul may not survive it. Or look away.
- **Strict stand-ins, if you want them.** When a design's material was missing, any material of the same kind and grade or finer stood in, so a Heaven-grade herb could be burnt on a Yellow pill. A switch above the design list, "Substitutes: strict", now takes only one at most a grade finer, of the wanted material's element or the element that feeds it.
- **Charm opens doors with your equals.** With those of your realm or beneath it, half of your charm past 50 (at most 25) now comes off the regard they need before they follow you, become your disciple, learn from you or run your errands. The refusal says what your charm took off. Those above you are not swayed by it.
- **Masters answer by their nature.** A disciple's master came to their side three times in five, whoever they were. Now the ruthless and the proud come readily, the honourable, humble and kind seldom, and the cautious only send a warning from afar. Best a disciple of an honourable, humble or kind master who stayed away, and the master may come to apologise for them, with a twentieth of their stones by way of amends.
- **Remade land fights for its element.** On land remade into another biome (or kept by a house), arts of the land's element now strike 10% harder, and those its element overcomes 10% softer. Wild land of its own nature lends nothing.
- **A natal blessing for your child.** A new Causality rite, Natal Blessing (skill 45), weaves a blessing into the fate of a child of your blood under six standing with you: their luck and comprehension rise by 2 to 8 for life, once for each child. The world's diviners bless their own small children too.
- **The five phases in every craft.** How the materials' elements sit together weighed only in alchemy. Now a blade, a talisman, an array or a puppet is 12% stronger for each pair of elements that feed one another, 15% weaker for each that overcome one another, and 5% stronger for one pure element. An unbroken feeding chain adds more on top: three in a row (Wood feeds Fire feeds Earth) +10%, four +15%, all five +25%, up to ×1.75 in all. The inventor shows it for every craft and names the chain.
- **Fields fed and hastened.** Growing herbs can be rushed: pour blood essence refined from mortals into the beds, and every growing field gains herb-years at once. Soul pearls are worked into the spirit soil instead, worth their souls (three points for each doubling), and can make it richer than your herbalism knows how to. With 40 insight into the Dao of Time, "Hasten the season" (a month, once a year) gives every field a herb-year for each 25 insight. The Dao of Wood's Growth now makes every harvest finer as well (half its growth bonus, up to +25% quality).
- **An elemental world.** Every region of the world below is ruled by an element, from its land (deserts Fire, forests Wood, mountains Metal, plains Earth, coasts and marshes Water), named on the Location tab and the Map. Its beasts are half again as likely to be of that element, and its dao is contemplated a fifth more readily there. A beast born in a region of another element is sometimes a variant of the region's (an Ice wolf, a Flame python), with that element's blood, breath and core. Every newly made world is different from before.
- **Seas.** Open water now lies off the coast: one sea or two, each with an island, a reef and a sea cave, reached by sea routes drawn dashed in blue on the Map. A crossing between the land and the sea goes by ship and takes a month more, unless you fly. Older worlds keep their map.
- **Two mortal ways: the Martial Way and the Shaman's Way.** Ways of life for mortals, not roads to immortality: they need no root, are taken up beside any path from their own tabs in the Arts tab, never lengthen life and never raise a realm. Each has nine tiers.
  - **Martial:** Apprentice to Peak Grandmaster. Drill a month at a time (faster under a master who stands above you, and with a martial manual), learn from every fight (more against the stronger, nothing against one who crushes you), and prove a full tier by winning a bout against the next tier or passing a master's test. A Peak Grandmaster fights as a cultivator in Qi Condensation's second stage, and no more. From sixty a martial artist slowly loses ground.
  - **Shaman:** Listener to Great Shaman. Carve a totem of spirit wood around a beast core, bind the spirits of places to it, and make offerings of herbs, cores or a banner's souls. Each pact adds every year and rides your blows of its element. Rites call the rain on a place's fields, ward a place against beast tides below their realm until the end of next year, or let you speak with the dead (how they died, by whose hand, what they left). Spirit sight finds more when gathering, and beasts trust a shaman. A shaman fights at two thirds of a martial artist's strength.
  - The world's rootless take up both ways; every kingdom keeps two martial generals at its capital; towns come to have shamans who perform rites for a fee (the Location tab).
- **A method's second dao.** A Qi method can now carry a second dao beside its element's: choose it when you make the method (the art forge's new "Second dao", asking as much insight in it as the method's own), or weave one into a method of Earth grade or finer by refining it (the Arts tab, "Carry a second dao..."). While you practise the method, that dao is contemplated 25% faster, and it adds up to 3.3% to every breakthrough's quality (a third of the deepest dao's weight): the breakthrough panel shows it. The main dao a Heavenly realm asks is still the method's element. Some sect library methods carry the dao of the element that feeds theirs, and the world's cultivators give their own methods their next-deepest dao.
- **An artifact spirit's body is its vessel.** A spirit bound in its vessel wore a human body. Now it wears the vessel's: its core, a blade's edge and hilt, armour's plates and bindings, or a jewel's setting and facets, and the spirit seal, which is its soul. The parts are as whole as the vessel; repair the vessel and the spirit mends, and what it devours mends it a little. Manifested, it wears its human body again (kept, not made anew), and withdrawing gives the vessel's back. The Character tab draws the blade, breastplate or ring.
- **Fight beside your holder, if you choose.** A spirit used to command its holder's whole turn. Now it may instead fight beside them from the vessel with its own arts (the Location tab, "In battle: ..."), while the holder fights as an ally run by the game. Tell the holder to press on, hold back or flee. No blow is aimed at the spirit while its holder stands; blows that reach it chip the vessel, and only soul arts reach its soul. Blows on a holder who wears the vessel chip it too. If the holder falls the spirit fights on until the vessel cracks; if the holder flees, it carries the vessel away. The world's spirits fight beside whoever carries them, in every battle. Every companion can also now be told to flee.
- **Armour that grows into a set.** A spirit in a piece of armour can absorb another piece its holder carries (a month; their regard 40). The vessel then covers that place too, up to a whole suit: its effects grow with the share of the suit it covers, the piece's best effect joins them, and worn it fills every place at once. The world's armour spirits do the same.
- **The gu refining pot.** Seal three or more gu of one grade, of any kinds, in a pot (tick them on the Professions tab): they devour one another for the months of rearing the next grade. The kind put in most survives, and rises a grade as often as a brood of that grade would live (a tenth likelier), or stays as it was. It keeps the natures of up to two lesser kinds it ate, at a third of their strength: a Moonlight Gu that ate a Venom Gu poisons where its blade lands, one that ate a Stone-Skin Gu throws up a thin shell. A demonic gu eaten makes it demonic. The Inventory now says what each gu does, where it showed only its kind's code name.
- **Tokens of protection grow with their giver.** From Deity Transformation a flaring token carries a blow of yours that leaves the attacker bleeding. With 80 insight into Space it tears space and brings its bearer to your side, and when you can reach them you may pull them to you instead of fighting. From True Immortal a shade of you lives in the token and flares once more for each realm before it is spent.
- **Rule your kingdom.** The overlord of a mortal kingdom can now set its taxes at its capital (light: half the tribute, a steadier throne; heavy: half again, a shakier one), found an academy (5,000 stones: a steadier throne, and every five years its best student comes to you), and name an heir among their children and disciples. A firm throne gathers national luck, which quickens its overlord's cultivation by up to 10%. When an overlord dies the throne passes to their heir, or their eldest cultivating child, instead of falling away.
- **A body's roar.** A Body cultivator's sound art with no instrument in hand was hummed at reduced force. Now it is a roar, at full force, that shakes the foe's momentum and stuns one time in five.
- **Wake a Celestial blade's spirit.** A weapon of Celestial grade or finer whose spirit has grown to level 3 can now have its spirit called awake (Inventory): it wakes as a person of its own, bound to the blade and devoted to you, and grows on the Soul path as any artifact spirit does.
- **Carry a character into another world.** "Export this character" (Character tab) writes a copy of someone, without their world, beside the saves. In another world, "Bring in a character..." lets the copy step out of the void as one of its people, with their arts, designs and all they carry, and no ties there. An immortal arrives in the Upper Realm, once that world's heavens are open. No one is ever brought into the world they live in.
- **Grand formations in the Formations craft.** The Formations page has a new part, Grand formations: every one you keep, wherever it stands, with a way to its eye on the map, and raising one where you are.
- **The world does it too.** The world's overlords now set taxes by their nature, found academies when rich and name heirs (a disciple when no child cultivates). The world's herbalists hasten their seasons with the Dao of Time and feed their fields refined essence (a demonic sect's from its treasury). Demonic crafters standing in a town at the year's turn now and then render its mortals, with the same karma, infamy and shaken throne. Gu masters rear broods of their own, seal three alike in refining pots, and in battle heal with mending gu and let fighting gu loose.
- **Nothing carried across worlds stays tied to a spirit left behind.** A weapon carried into another world (crossing, visiting from the shared heavens, or a character brought in alone) still named the spirit living in it by its old world's number, which could be someone else entirely in the new one. The spirit now stays a person of the world it lived in.
- **Earth-grade demonic methods feed on Devouring.** Conceiving a demonic method of Earth grade or finer, or refining one up to it, now asks 20 insight into the Dao of Devouring, and 10 more each grade above. The humbler ones need none. The world's people make theirs by the same rule.
- **Charm pills.** A new pill nature, Radiance (Vitality with Longevity), raises Charm for good as attribute pills raise theirs. The world's people take them too.
- **Qi pills work over months.** A qi pill no longer floods you with its qi the moment you swallow it: the same qi now seeps in over four months for a Yellow-grade pill, a month longer each grade (ten at most), whether you cultivate or not. What is still to come waits at a bottleneck. Swallowed in battle, a qi pill still gives its qi back at once. The world's cultivators take theirs the same way.
- **Choose your world's names.** A new world can be named in Chinese-sounding, Japanese-sounding, Korean-sounding or invented names. Its history comes out the same whichever is chosen.
- **Domains come sooner.** Domains now unfold from Golden Core with 45 insight (was Nascent Soul with 50), and stand five rounds instead of four.
- **Catch a dear one's soul.** From Nascent Soul with 30 insight into the Dao of Soul, someone dear to you who dies beside you has their soul caught. Give it a body again (months, and materials of Vitality) and they live once more a major realm lower, with all they knew; or let it go. The world's people catch and rebuild their own.
- **Consorts.** Those who rule (a sect's or clan's leader, a kingdom's overlord) can court up to three consorts, a lesser bond than dao companions that can bear children. Dao companions take it hard, consorts quarrel, and an unhappy one leaves. The world's rulers keep consorts too.
- **More for artifact spirits.** A new life as a spirit can be born in the forge (half again as long-lived, a sixth slower to grow) and choose its vessel's element. A spirit grasps its vessel's dao half again as readily, lends its holder Spirit and Comprehension while worn (all, half or none, as it chooses; holding back costs their regard), and can teach its holder an art of its own.
- **A Golden Immortal shapes a person.** From the Character tab, a Golden Immortal can make a person of the world out of their own dao: name, sex, path, root element, nature and realm (two below their own in the world below; a True Immortal above the clouds), at ten years of life, once in ten years. The world's Golden Immortals shape disciples now and then.
- **Tidier Sect, Shops and Location tabs.** The Sect tab opens on a new Overview section holding what used to fill its top, with the other sections' buttons above it. Shop wares and market stalls are two-line rows that open for the details, with Buy on the name line, and a group of the same ware is one line. The Location tab's top keeps only the place, its qi, its danger and its secret realm; everything else about the place is under "Here".

## 0.13.34 (2026-11-16)

- **Every realm is harder to break into than the one before.** Your first breakthrough was the hardest and every later one easier: comprehension, foundation, insight, luck and the place only ever grew, so a late Deity Transformation cultivator stood at 106% for True Immortal before the 95% cap. Now every help except the realm's own base counts whole into Foundation Building, then 13% less for each realm above: about three quarters into Nascent Soul, about half into True Immortal, 45% into Golden Immortal. The breakthrough's tooltip says so on its first line. That same cultivator now stands at 46%. A cultivator with a good root on a good vein, carrying no key treasure, takes about a sixth longer to be ready to ascend.
- **Each realm asks denser qi of the place.** Any realm drew its full bonus from a place at ×3, so a spirit vein that steadied a foundation steadied an immortal just as well. Now the qi of the place begins to help at ×1 into Foundation Building and helps fully at ×2.5; both rise each realm, to ×2 and ×5 into Golden Immortal. Thinner than the first, a place hurts.
- **A Heavenly realm long ago no longer carries every one after.** The Heaven-Blessed Breakthrough destiny now blesses only the realm right after a Heavenly one. Fine realms of the past add half as much to later breakthroughs' chances as they did.
- **The hidden stage: a stage past the peak of every realm.** At the bottleneck you can now press on instead of breaking through ("Past your peak", on the right). Qi gathers against a seal at half your pace: about as long again as the whole realm took. It is the hidden Tenth Layer of Qi Condensation, and a Great Perfection beyond the Peak of every realm after.
  - It asks a prepared dao. At Qi Condensation that is a dao foundation of 70 and your main dao's leading concept at its half-open limit (30). From Foundation Building it is every concept your realm opens in your main dao, made whole.
  - When the qi is full, break the seal. In a new minigame, turn each of its rings (three, four from Golden Core, five from Deity Transformation) until its gap lies on the line through the seal's heart, and hold it there for a breath until it locks. Meanwhile the seal turns a loose ring back most breaths, and every turn costs breath. A seal that holds scatters some of the qi and bruises the meridians.
  - Held, you fight as one a stage higher, and your breakthrough gains +10% chance and +10% quality, whatever the realm's weight. **It is now the only way to form a realm Heavenly.**
  - A failed breakthrough loses it, but most of its qi is kept. The world's patient and gifted cultivators press past their peaks too.
- **The tenth pillar.** From the hidden Tenth Layer, the Foundation Building minigame no longer ends at nine pillars. Seal the foundation at nine, or raise a tenth against a fiercer tide for a far finer foundation. A tenth that crumbles leaves the nine shaken, but standing.
- **Key treasures are rare.** Players found 30 in a sect's treasury; the saves held up to 659 in one, and thousands in all, because each was as common as any herb of its grade.
  - The herbs among them now grow a quarter as often in the wild.
  - Treasuries, sect gatherers, a market's ordinary lots and shop shelves never stock them.
  - A city's rare-treasure stall sells one only some years, at about ten times its worth instead of two and a half.
  - The world's cultivators buy them at the stall as you do.
  - The Body path's key, any beast core of the realm, is unchanged.
  - Older saves keep at most two of each in every treasury.

## 0.13.33 (2026-11-16)

- **Talk to anyone straight from the Location tab.** Each person in the People list now has a Talk button beside Drink and Interact. Discussing the dao used to be three menus deep, under a dozen options of the moment. The Interact menu now also opens with the everyday choices, in this order: talk, spend time, give a gift, trade, ask for guidance, debate, spar, weiqi.
- **Searching a long list finds the name first.** Typing "Time" in the dao picker listed every dao whose words mention "a round" ahead of the Dao of Time itself. In every searchable picker, a name that begins with what you typed now comes first, then one with a word beginning with it, and last whatever matches only in its description. The contemplation pickers (yours and an avatar's) also list the daos you have gone deepest into first.
- **What you have found stays in place when you contemplate the dao.** A phenomenon the dao showed green, or one your insight revealed, is now filled in for you in every new row, so you only choose the places still unknown. Undo takes back only what you chose. Press L to turn this off, if you want to try a found phenomenon elsewhere.
- **Leave a mark on someone, so you never lose them.** From Interact (where they are), a thread of your spirit they cannot feel. The marked never fall from your Relations tab, which has a new Marked filter, and where they stand shows among the map's places of interest. You can hold one mark, and one more for each 25 insight into Karma or the Soul. From 40 Karma the mark holds to the soul: if they die and are born again, the new life still carries your mark, even if they cared nothing for you either way.
- **Causality can seek someone deep in a dao, and an open demon rift.** "Seek one deep in a dao" (Causality 20) names the people of your world who see further into the dao you choose than you do, nearest first, and where they are: someone to talk it over with, debate or ask for guidance. "Seek a demon rift" (Causality 30) points to an open rift to the Demon Realm and says how long it has left. Open rifts also appear among the map's places of interest, as calamities do.
- **Show the place an epiphany asks for on the map.** An epiphany that sends you to a land of valleys or a spirit vein named the place only in words. A button under it now centres the map on the nearest one.
- **Choose how many to craft.** Beside "Quick craft all", a design now offers "Quick craft how many..." (2, 3, 5, 10 or 20). The run still stops early if materials or skill run out.
- **Let a gu go from the Gu tab.** Unwanted gu could only be discarded from the Inventory, so players kept feeding broods they no longer wanted. Each gu on the Professions tab now has "Let it go...".
- **Put a bred strain out of mind.** Years of breeding filled the design inventor and the recipe lists with old strains. "Forget a strain you bred..." takes one off your lists, with every recipe worked from it. Herbs and seeds you carry are kept; breeding or working it again brings it back.
- **Feed everything to your inner world at once.** The feeding list now opens with "Everything below (your realm's) grade" (your key treasures and finest finds stay in your bag) and "Every raw material you carry". Each asks first.
- **Let the souls in a soul banner go.** A banner's souls could only be pushed out by stronger ones. "Let souls go..." in the Inventory frees one, or all of them, to the wheel. Each person's soul freed lightens your karma a little; your banner arts lose what that soul added.
- **Inheritances teach their maker's understanding.** Claiming an inheritance gave its items, stones and arts, but no insight. Each art it holds now also gives 1.5 insight per grade into that art's dao. The world's wanderers who claim one gain the same.
- **Clan branches settle in ruins.** Every new branch of a clan raised a new estate on the map, so a long world filled with tiles. A branch now takes over the ruins of a fallen house in its new region where there are any, as every new house already did.
- **The battle screen's list view shows only what is hurt.** It used to list every ear and toe at full health. Whole parts are now counted in one line under the wounded ones.
- **The History tab opens quickly.** It rebuilt 400 lines of the annals every time the screen refreshed. It now redraws only when the annals change, shows the newest 150 entries at first, and "Show older entries" reads further back.
- **Join the sect or clan that best fits your root.** When you begin as a clan scion or sect applicant, the picker now offers "The clan (or sect) that best fits your root". It picks the house whose qi methods cover most of your root's elements, among the clans and renowned sects whose teaching is known. Whichever house you pick, its line now also says how well its methods suit your root.
- **Name your world.** Forging a world has a World name field. Leave it empty and the world names itself. A name changes nothing else the seed forges.
- **Bring out a puppet as every fight begins.** If you carry a puppet, the battle screen has a "Puppet at the start" switch beside Advice. When it is on, each fight opens with your first puppet brought out. That costs your first turn, as bringing it out by hand does.

## 0.13.32 (2026-11-16)

- **The Gather menu lists only the kinds of material, and "Where can I find...?" comes first.** Each kind used to carry every material found there, so the list ran long and pushed "Where can I find...?" out of sight at the bottom. The materials are now chosen one step on, the kind's line says how many grow there and of what tier, and the wilds have their own "Where can I find...?" button beside Gather.
- **Gifts come as often as the bond is close.** Every friend at 70 regard had a 15% chance a year to send something, and devoted elders 30%, so the bag filled with gifts. Now a good friend (50 regard) sends a few things about once in twenty-five years, and family, a dao companion, a sworn sibling or one whose heart is wholly yours (100) about once a decade. Elders above you send from their own stores at the same pace.
- **Choose what to keep from the fallen.** Tick "Choose what to keep from the fallen" under the cultivation buttons, and after a fight you win you can look over what you took and leave it on the field: everything, everything below your realm's grade, or one thing at a time. Untick it and you keep everything, as before. A blade holding a spirit is set down on the ground there, not destroyed.
- **Spirit beasts have minds and charm of their own.** Every beast had a comprehension of 20 and a charm of 10, a nine-tailed fox as charming as a rock. A spirit beast has woken to the dao, so it now understands about as well as a person (around 42, from 15 to 85), and its charm is its own (around 38). The kinds known for it charm more: a fox spirit 35 more, the Fortune Cat 25, phoenixes and the Nine-Colour Deer 20, qilin 10 to 15. Mortal beasts stay simpler. Beasts in older saves gain what they would have been born with.
- **An ancient's avatar that bears you no grudge hears you.** Meeting a traced avatar offered only a fight or withdrawing, even to a new life that had never raised a hand against them. Now an avatar of an ancient with no rage at your line speaks for them, as in an audience: their tasks, their lore, or your hand raised against them. Rage at your line still comes to fight, and now says whose deed it answers: your past life's, a forebear's or a clansman's.
- **Make one work from the design inventor without recording it.** Beside "Record this design", "Make one now" (with its minigame) and "Make one (quick)" craft the draft once from the materials you carry. The work is yours, and the design does not join your list.
- **A heart-bending pill can turn your own nature.** Swallowed, it only left you dizzy. Now the Inventory offers "Bend your own heart": choose a nature and what it becomes, and spend a month with the pill. A willing heart turns more easily than another's, and a steady dao heart less. No stain on your karma; a failure costs the pill and shakes your dao heart a little (3). The world's people whose dao heart is failing for a vice do the same with any such pill they carry.
- **Debates and talk of the dao no longer pour out insight for free.** A dao debate took no time and taught 1 plus an eighth of how far the other saw beyond you (11 from someone 80 deeper), once a year from each sage in town. A debate now takes a month and teaches at most 4. A talk of the dao lets slip a sliver of insight once a month, whoever you talk with, where a town of teachers talked to in turn gave many months of contemplation in one.
- **Breed out an aspect you do not want, or draw out one you do.** Strains bred again and again gathered stray aspects until they were good for nothing. From herbalism 60, in your abode's herb garden, half a year and one seed trim an aspect away, and half its strength goes into the strongest aspect left. From 80 you can encourage an aspect instead: a point stronger, taken from the weakest. A tended strain can be crossed and tended again, and named like any strain.
- **Step into your inner world, and keep your abode there.** An inner world was only somewhere to retreat and to keep people. Now it is a place you can stand in: "Step into" it from anywhere in the world of the living or the heavens (Character or Location tab, no time), and step out where you went in. Build an abode inside like anywhere else, or carry the abode you stand in into it (three months; not a sect's or clan's seat): its rooms, storehouse, arrays, hands and fields then go wherever you go, beyond any raider's reach, and you can set it down outside again. Inside, nothing outside reaches you and the qi is your world's. Unfolding the world sets its abode on the new land; if you die, the abode inside is set down where you last went in.
- **No True Immortal lives below the clouds through an inner world.** A tamed beast that rose to True Immortal inside its keeper's inner world above the clouds stayed one when the world was carried below, then took human form and lived on there as a True Immortal. An inner world below the clouds now holds its people at the peak of Deity Transformation, as everyone below is held, and older saves are mended on loading.

## 0.13.31 (2026-11-16)

- **A beast can form a Heavenly realm: its main dao is its blood's element.** A Heavenly realm asks the concepts of your main dao whole, and on the Qi path that dao was your method's element. A beast cultivates by instinct with no method, so it had no main dao, and every breakthrough it made stopped at 79%. Richer qi and a better minigame changed nothing it could see. Now a Fire beast's main dao is Fire (a beast of no element rests on its bloodline's dao, or on Body), and the breakthrough panel names it. The world's beasts follow the same rule.
- **The Guide now names the Soul path's main dao as Soul.** It still said Yin, which has not been true since 0.13.21.

## 0.13.30 (2026-11-16)

- **An enlightenment poured into seclusion now quickens your cultivation instead of granting stages outright.** It gave three minor stages at once; now, as with a dao, cultivating gains three times the qi for the next five years of cultivation. Only months spent cultivating count, it waits at a bottleneck, and the qi bar and its tooltip show what is left.
- **An enlightenment poured into a craft now raises the limit your realm sets on it, not the skill itself.** It lifted a Foundation Building alchemist from 10 straight to 65 skill; now the craft's limit rises to what the realm above holds, and practice takes the skill there. A grandmaster's promised masterpiece is unchanged. The world's cultivators spend theirs the same way.

## 0.13.29 (2026-11-16)

- **One tap acts on phones and tablets, and a drag never presses anything.** "Tap twice to act" was on for everyone, so every button needed two taps; it is now off by default (turn it back on in Settings). A finger that moves more than a few points is a drag: it scrolls the list and never presses the button it began or ended on, even where the list has nothing to scroll or the swipe went sideways. Touching a list still gliding from a flick stops it without pressing.
- **Saving a large late world holds the game about half as long, and a phone no longer writes big saves in one go.** A world thousands of years old held a tablet for 8 to 12 seconds on each save. The save is now compressed on another thread while it is written, so the game waits only for the writing (about 45% less), and only a few megabytes are ever held in memory. Saves leave out every value that is simply zero, so the file is about a third smaller and loads about a quarter faster.
- **Autosaves come less often when each one takes a while.** A save that holds the game for some seconds now waits forty times that long before the next timed one (6 s means at most one every four minutes), instead of every two minutes. Saves when the game is put away, when leaving the game screen and on quitting are unchanged.
- **Each save in the Upper Realm is a fifth of a second quicker.** Every save wrote out each of the world's fallen immortals (some 1,500 in a late world) for the shared heavens, though only the living ever visit other worlds. Now only the living are written.
- **A rare crash in the year's turn is gone.** Someone with no spiritual part left whole who refined a bloodline and rejected it stopped the world.
- **The battle screen keeps up with hundreds of moves.** With 300 arts, each action took over a second to redraw every art and move button; now only what changed is updated (about 25 ms).
- **Auto-fight no longer runs from fights it is winning.** A talisman with an escape in it (a Swift ink among its three effects) carried you, or any fighter, out of the battle when it was burned for its blow or its healing, even at full health. Such talismans are now kept for fleeing, and fighters flee only when badly hurt and their foe is no better off.
- **Auto-fight strikes with your art's harder moves instead of sealing a foe again and again.** A move whose effect cannot take hold (a seal on meridians already sealed, a stun on a foe who cannot be stunned) now counts as a plain blow, so the other moves are used. Arts are weighed by their best move, and a blow aimed at the head, neck or heart favours the hardest-hitting move. Everyone else in the world fights the same way.
- **A soul spike meshed into another art waits for your sea of consciousness to settle.** Its button is greyed out meanwhile, and auto-fight and the advice no longer waste a turn reaching for it.
- **The Inventory and the Professions tab can show only what you made, or only what others made.** After storming a few sects, the bag and the design lists filled with their vaults and recipes, and your own works were lost among them. A new "Any maker / Made by you / Made by others" choice in the Inventory and "Any designer / Devised by you / Devised by others" in the Professions tab sort them apart. Your own designs read "devised by you", and a shop's commission list and your abode workers' list put yours first.
- **The Professions tab no longer slows down as you learn more designs.** A player who knew 191 alchemy designs waited more than a second on a tablet for it to draw, because every design was drawn at once. Each category now shows 10 designs and a "Show more" button, and a list of more than 30 opens with its categories folded; filters and search still look through every design. The design inventor's materials and the known recipes are shown a page at a time too. On a PC it now draws in under a fifth of a second, down from up to 2.5 seconds.
- **A large bag draws twice as fast.** The Inventory opened every kind of item at once; now the first kinds open until about 30 rows are shown and the rest start folded, and it no longer works out what every item would give back when dismantled before drawing anything.
- **The Chronicle no longer grows heavier over a long session.** It keeps the last 300 lines on the page instead of every line since the game was opened.
- **Your party stays with you, and the world's unseen dangers pass them by as they pass you by.** Companions carried off by the world (a clan calling its people home, an errand, the road of a wandering expert) stayed away for the rest of the month or the whole year's turn, and could die there of things you never saw: a death on the road, a secret realm's crowd, a beast tide or calamity. Now they are back at your side after each part of the month and year, spared those unseen deaths while there, and never sent off as a wandering expert. Guarding a caravan brings your companions along. Fights you or they are really in still put them at risk.
- **A journey your bottleneck asks for is met as soon as you stand in that land.** Reaching the valleys or mountains asked for counted only at the end of a journey you walked yourself. Arriving with a caravan, being summoned there, being stopped by an encounter on the last step, or already standing there left the epiphany "sought". Now standing anywhere in a land of that kind meets it, and it stays met after you leave.
- **A heaven-and-earth treasure born a few steps away offers the way there.** If you could reach the site in no time, the phenomenon offered only "Wait for it here", and choosing it let the treasure go to others because you were not on the spot. Now only standing on the spot offers waiting there; a site moments away offers "Set out", and an old event still asking you to wait takes you to it.
- **A Borrowed Rebirth is now chosen on the death screen.** When you die with a karmic seed planted, the death screen offers to send your soul to its vessel, says where it waits and the realm you will wake at, or says plainly why it cannot (the seed lies across the clouds, your soul was shattered, the child you prepared is dead). Before, the crossing happened unseen at the moment of death, and when it failed nothing told you why. The rite's words now say what a vessel can be: a puppet body of tier 6 or higher, which gives a body of twenty with your own root, or a child of your blood under 12 standing with you; that the three years of preparing count only while you stay beside it; and that a soul crosses only once.
- **An avatar you walk below the clouds no longer dies the month you wake in it.** Its body was counted as old as the years since you made it, and the years your main body had burnt counted against it, so an old immortal's avatar woke at the end of its life ("20 of 20") and the first month destroyed it. An avatar's body now begins at twenty, ages only while you live in it, and keeps years of its own. A save made while walking one is mended on loading.
- **An artifact spirit can win its holder's regard without a fight.** A holder too strong or too settled to adventure left a spirit with no way to rise in their eyes: its regard sat near nothing for decades, and fights it led through their hands counted for nothing. Now, on the Location tab beside your vessel, you can spend a month steadying their cultivation as they sit (5% of a stage), counselling them in your deepest dao, or lending your sense to their craft. Each month wins regard: +5 while they hardly know you, less as it grows (20 in about four months). Every fight you win through their hands adds 3.
- **What a spirit is given is its own to devour.** Ores, jades and cores in a spirit's own bag (gifts) could not be devoured while it was bound; only its holder's stores could, and only at regard 20. Now your own can be devoured at any time, and the picker marks each one as yours or your holder's.
- **A voice in a blade has no body.** A bound spirit could work mortal jobs, sit in teahouses, help the mortals, see a physician, adopt, take requests, hire guards, gamble, fight in the pit, treat with sects, serve courts, give gifts and talk with anyone. Until it manifests, each of these is greyed out, saying why, and so are exploring, gathering, hunting and searching the land. Only the hand that holds you hears you: on the Relations tab you can whisper to your holder, keep them company and ask their guidance; everyone else is a sheet to look at.
- **Primordial herbs give up their seeds to a grandmaster.** Drawing seeds from a Primordial herb, or crossing Primordial strains, asked for 108 herbalism, but a craft's own skill stops at 100. Both now ask 100 at most. Reading a Celestial or Primordial mix before making it also asked for more than 100, and now asks 100.
- **The craft unions say plainly that Tiers 6 and 7 are held above the clouds.** The Professions tab named their hall as if it were an ordinary one, and a crafter below could even buy a seat for an exam they could never reach. The union page now says these tiers are examined in the heart of the heavens, with seats held by the sects there. Below the clouds, the seat button explains that the tier opens once you have ascended.
- **Everyone you know is in the Relations tab.** Acquaintances met in encounters and admirers you never spoke to were missing, because the list kept only bonds and strong opinions. They are now listed after your stronger bonds, with an Acquaintances filter.
- **You can look at whoever asks for your hand before answering.** "View their sheet" in a betrothal no longer says they are hidden from you, however little they think of you. A proposal now gives the suitor's cultivation as you can read it, not their true realm.
- **Spirit is a gold link in the pill guide like the other attributes.** The Guide links each term once, and the material aspect "Spirit" at the top of the aspects topic took the link meant for the attribute. The aspect is now left plain.
- **The weiqi board is marked along its edges.** Columns A to G run across the top and bottom and rows 7 to 1 down the sides, so a move named "A5" can be found. How to play says A1 is the bottom left corner.

## 0.13.28 (2026-11-16)

- **An artifact spirit's Road Ahead is a spirit's.** It told a spirit in its vessel to find a key treasure, take pills, learn an attack art and join a sect, none of which it can do. It now names what stands in a spirit's way: cultivating within the vessel, having the vessel remade a grade finer when it holds you no higher (with the refinement devoured so far), winning your holder's regard (20 to feed on their stores, 60 to pay a smith), being taken up when you lie unheld, and at Nascent Soul a body of your own.
- **A spirit walks the Soul path alone.** The Arts tab let a spirit take up Qi Refinement or Body Tempering beside it, and waking in a vessel sometimes gave one Body Tempering, a new age and a purse of stones out of nothing. A spirit now keeps the Soul path, its soul's own years and only its own stones; older saves lose the other paths on loading.
- **Travel is Urge while you are bound.** The Map tab offered "Travel there" to a spirit in its vessel and did nothing. It now offers "Urge your holder there" (and Urge beside quests and calamities), with the odds by their regard; a beast held in pens or tethered at home sees the Travel buttons greyed out, saying why.
- **No betrothals for a voice in a blade.** A spirit in its vessel was met with betrothals, proposals, approaches, sect summons and wanderers like anyone, could be wed, and so came to have a child on the way. Now only heart demons, a remnant soul, and the beasts and robbers that come for its holder find it; no one proposes to, weds or binds a bound spirit (manifest a body first), and a spirit never has children. The world's spirits are not wed either.

## 0.13.27 (2026-11-13)

- **A master's nature decides what becomes of their beast.** About one year in two a master puts a tamed beast to a use, chosen by their natures: anyone may ride a beast that can carry them (a lazy master most of all), a lustful one pairs it with another of their beasts (the young are theirs), a greedy one sells it to a city's beast pens, a ruthless one seals its shape so it cannot take human form or shift (and a ruthless or greedy one bleeds its essence blood: 3 purity and a wound), a diligent one drills it (3 months of cultivation), and a compassionate one, fond of a beast grown nearly as strong as they are, lets it go with a parting gift. The world's tamed beasts are kept this way, and so are you, living as a tamed beast: each use is put to you to bear or resist. **Whether resisting works depends on your master's strength:** a master no stronger than you cannot force you, one a realm above must overpower your will (a contest of fighting strength and spirit), and one two realms above or more forces it anyway (a ruthless one beats you for trying). The Location tab names your master's natures, what they tend to do, and your odds.
- **Every city's beast pens are real.** The pens were a list drawn up afresh each year: no one kept them, and a beast sold there vanished. Each city's pens now have a keeper, one of its cultivators, and hold real beasts. Each year the keeper hires the mounts among them to the caravans, pairs a male and a female for young, draws essence blood (sold at the city's market as the keeper's own goods), and sells beasts to the city's cultivators, a prize beast at the grand auction for half again. Tamers bring young beasts when the pens run low, and sales share the world's limit on kept beasts with taming, so the world does not fill with them. Buy from the keeper, or sell yours into the pens. A dead keeper is succeeded at once, and the beasts stay. Older saves gain their pens on loading.
- **Lease a city's pens and run them.** Pay off the keeper and the pens are yours (Location tab, Market): order them to rent out mounts, breed, farm blood and sell; take a beast out, pen one of your own, or set one loose. A hired hand runs them while you are away for a fifth of what they earn, and the panel shows what they made last year.
- **Penned as a beast.** A beast player sold to a city is held in its pens: the keeper is your master, hires you out, pairs, bleeds or sells you on, and the bars hold you (no travelling) until someone buys you or you **break out** (once a year; a realm above the keeper helps, agility too; caught, you are hurt and watched until the new year).
- **Live on as an artifact spirit.** A soul strong enough to linger as a ghost can, when it dies, flee into a weapon, armour or a jewel within reach (its own, its killer's, or anyone's there), and a killer who knows the forge can instead forge it into a weapon of their make. You become its spirit and go wherever it goes: carried, sold, consigned, looted or left on the ground. You cultivate the Soul path, one realm a grade of your vessel at most; devour ores, jades and cores from your holder's bag (if they hold you dear) and have them remake you a grade finer (a forger does it, anyone else pays a smith if fond enough of you) to rise further. Win your holder's regard and **urge** them where to go; lying unheld, **call out** to the strongest soul nearby. In battle you fight through the hand that holds you, commanding their whole turn, and your own strikes dart from the weapon. At Nascent Soul a tribulation lets you **manifest** a body with your vessel in your hand, and you can withdraw into it again.
