The world has spirits of its own: a weapon spirit grown to its fullest sometimes wakes as a person, and a forger who kills a strong soul now and then forges it into a weapon. A vessel that ever leaves the world turns up again in someone's hands where it was last.
- **Your master's regard decides how you are kept.** Living as a tamed beast, the Location tab shows what your master thinks of you, and it now matters: bear their will, win fights at their side and bring them an offering once a year (a core or a material from your bag) to raise it; resist and it falls. A master who cherishes you (50) never forces you, never sells, seals or bleeds you, lifts any seal on your shape and gives you two gifts a year; one who trusts you (20) uses you harshly half as often and gives a gift every year; one who distrusts you gives nothing, teaches nothing, and is likelier to seal, bleed or sell you, or to keep you tethered at home, where you cannot travel until they trust you again. The world's tamed beasts are kept the same way.
- **Birds, serpents, spiders and dragons lay eggs.** A paired beast that carries its young (four-legged and man-shaped kinds) bears it at once, as before; an egg-layer lays an egg, a real item that hatches at the turn of the year after as the young of whoever holds it. Carry it, or sell it to a city's pens, where it hatches as theirs.
- **Bred beasts carry mutations.** A young is now and then born with a mark, and takes each of its parents' half the time (almost always when both have it): Fleet-footed (a third faster as a mount), Iron-boned, Thunder-limbed, Wind-quick and Spirit-eyed (+8 to an attribute), Qi-drinking (it grows a fifth faster), Heart-linked (its master cultivates a twentieth faster while it is at their side) and Sage-souled (its master grasps the dao a tenth faster). Each raises a beast's price in the pens by half, and the pens show them.
- **Beast Taming is a craft.** It has its own skill (Professions tab), grown by every beast you tame, every pairing that takes, every young you raise, a month of drilling your beasts, and handling beasts at a city's pens. At skill 100 a beaten beast submits 25 points likelier, pairings take half again as often and fail as crosses half as often, young are born with new mutations three times as often, and your beasts at your side grow a quarter more on every core you feed them and a tenth of a stage a year from your care. Schools at Adept (Soft Voice, Swift Breeding) and Master (Blood Shaper, Pack Leader), a Beast-Kin Body and a Beast Heart method trait born for it, and a Beast Lord epithet. The Beast Tamers' Union examines by taming: a beast of the tier's grade to win over before its fury breaks loose (soothe a timid one, cow a proud one). Each tier makes a city's pens sell to you 4% cheaper and pay 5% more, and its market sells cores. The world's tamers practise it too: they tame more often, keep the pens, and sit the exams.
- **Long seclusions pass faster.** The window that shows time passing gave the world 50 ms of each frame and spent the rest redrawing a panel that barely changes, about a quarter of the time. Once a stretch has run for a second and a half, it now gives the world 150 ms a frame, so decades of seclusion end about a fifth sooner; "Emerge now" still answers at once. And while you sit in closed-door seclusion, the world beyond those bound to you (companions and beasts, children, dao companions, master, disciples, nemesis) lives its months a year at a time, as the far-off world always lived them half a year at a time: a secluded year is a sixth to a quarter quicker, and over sixty years the world came out the same within the spread of chance.
- **Insight is no longer lost in a whole concept.** Insight aimed at a concept you have not opened goes to an open concept of the same dao, the least deep one, even when that one was already whole: with your only open concept of a dao whole, a contemplation of another concept of it (or of the dao) gained nothing at all, a century of it included. Insight now goes only where it has room: an open concept not yet whole, else the concept you named, else the closed one with the most room. The same held for the world's cultivators, avatars and arts that deepen a concept. The concept chooser now says where your insight will settle before you begin, the result says where it went when that was another concept, and any contemplation in which a whole year teaches nothing rises and says so.
- **A contemplation rises when it can go no further.** Contemplating a concept that is not open to you went on to the end of the months asked once it had reached all a glimpse (or a half-open concept) holds, each month teaching almost nothing, and only then said why. It now rises the month it gets there and tells you, as it does when a concept is made whole; contemplating a whole dao rises when every concept of it is as deep as it goes, or whole. To press on past the limit anyway (slowly), contemplate it again: one begun there runs its months.
- **Begin a life as an artifact spirit.** On the new-life screen, rolled or chosen point by point, tick "Wake as an artifact spirit" and pick a weapon, a piece of armour or a jewel: you wake as the spirit of a Yellow-grade one (a Mystic one at times), carried by a cultivator of Qi Condensation or Foundation Building who does not yet know you are there, or, unticking "Carried by a cultivator", lying forgotten in ruins until you call someone to pick you up. Your nature and attributes are the ones you rolled or bought; your path is the Soul path.
- **A master's teaching no longer gives a tamed beast more dao than its concepts allow.** Each year a master explained their deepest dao to their beast by raising all five of its concepts alike, up to 9 a year, past the glimpse a concept not yet open allows: a Qi Condensation beast under a master of 80 insight reached their dao in a decade, far beyond what contemplation could ever give it. The master now explains the one concept of it they know best, as insight earned (each layer harder), and only as far as the beast's own concepts are open.

## 0.13.26 (2026-11-12)

- **Soul arts now wear down a regenerating foe.** A regenerating beast (pythons, the nine-headed serpent, the phoenix) closed 5 points of every wound each round, its sea of consciousness included, and mending gear did the same. A soul art tears about 16 from an equal soul and can be used once every three rounds, so the sea healed faster than it could be hurt and a soul cultivator could never bring one down. A soul does not knit like flesh: regeneration, a Wood domain's spring, and the wounds a devouring art or a blood-drinking power heal now mend the sea of consciousness at a fifth of their pace (a regenerating beast's by 1 a round). Healing arts and pills still mend it in full. The beast's battle chip, the mending gear's description and the Guide say so.
- **The annals no longer tell strangers' enlightenments as yours.** Every enlightenment of the world's cultivators went into its history in your words ("You close your eyes, and heaven and earth flow through you...", "The Dao of Yang lies open before you..."), a dozen a year, filling the Chronicle's annals and the world-forging screen. Only your own are told now; a grandmaster's enlightened masterpiece is still news. Any other line written to "you" stays out of the world's history unless it is yours.
- **A rented-out house pays its rent once a year.** Rent reached you every month, and the coin chimed every month. It now gathers in the house through the year and comes as one sum in the year's first month (the Home panel shows what is owed so far); selling the house or taking it back pays what the tenants owe at once. The amount is unchanged.
- **Luck adds at most 10% to a breakthrough's quality.** Luck past 100 read "21% of 10%" on the Character tab and added all of it. Every part of a breakthrough's quality is now capped at its maximum.
- **The old master in the ring teaches a method of your own path.** He always taught a Qi method and made it your method, so a Body cultivator (an Overlord Body most of all, who can walk no other path) was left cultivating at a crawl (tens of thousands of years to the next stage). His method is now made for the path you walk.
- **Companions stay with you.** The world's own comings and goings (a clan calling its people home, a rogue's wandering) carried companions off while they stayed in your party: their portrait stayed in the side column, they could not be spoken to, and they still came back for your battles. They now rejoin you each month. A companion across the clouds or inside your inner world is shown faded, with a tooltip saying where; those in your inner world go where you go, and lists of people say "inside your inner world" rather than the place where they stepped in.
- **An immortal living on in an avatar below the clouds can climb back above.** When your body above the clouds died and your soul fled into an avatar you had sent below, you still counted as being above: you could neither ascend nor break through, stuck at the peak of True Immortal in the world below. The Ascend button now takes you back up without a tribulation; older saves are mended on loading.
- **A clan or sect made to order in the world forge is there to be born into.** It could fall in the centuries of history like any other (a clan made in the forge fell before the end in every world we tried), and was then never offered to a Clan Scion or an applicant. If it falls, the last of its line raises it again, under the same name, when the history ends.
- **A traveller home again keeps their arts, and can travel.** An immortal who crossed into another world and came back to the clouds above their own was taken for a visitor from elsewhere each time the game met the shared heavens (on every save), and brought into their own world a second time. The copy renumbered the traveller's own arts: they seemed to know none of them, and travelling on the map failed on their travel art. The heavens now know where each immortal lives, no one is ever a visitor of the world they live in, and arts brought from another world are copied, never taken over. A save already broken this way is mended when it is loaded.
- **The Tier 7 union seat can be taken.** A union asked for 120 skill for its Primordial-grade seat (the mark for working that grade with the help of workshops and scriptures), but a craft's own skill stops at 100, so even a grandmaster was refused forever. The top tiers now ask 100, a craft mastered; the exam itself is as hard as it was. The world's crafters can reach it too.
- **Journeys no longer vanish from Load a Journey.** A journey whose small summary file was cut short (the game was closed or killed while writing it) disappeared from the list even though its save was whole, and could be reached only by importing it. Every save is listed now: until it is loaded and saved again, its summary is read from its latest save slot, or its name from the file. Summaries are now written so that they can never be left half written.
- **Export every journey and every world at once.** Load a Journey has an Export all button: every save (with its save slots) in one file, or every save and every world you have forged (as each was last left, with what its playthroughs left behind). Choose a World has Export all worlds and Import worlds. Import a Save takes such a pack as well as a single save: whatever in it is not already here comes in, and nothing here is overwritten. Each export is read back and checked, as single exports are.
- **Stock many kinds of things at once.** Your shop's menu has Stock several kinds: tick every kind of thing you want on the shelves (with a search, and All or None) and everything you carry of each goes out in one go, instead of opening the menu again for every kind.
- **Double-click a place on the map to travel there.**
- **Your own pictures as portraits, for anyone.** Character tab: use a picture as your portrait. Relations, Interact: give anyone (kin, companions, enemies, beasts) a picture of your choosing. It shows wherever their face is drawn: the side column, your companions beside it, and in battle. PNG, JPEG, WebP, BMP or TGA pictures are kept beside your saves (not in them); every journey in the same world shows the same person the same way, and "Draw their portrait again" restores the drawn one.
- **The ledger shows your dao heart.** Everything that steadies or shakes your dao heart (seclusion, festivals, tribulations survived, heart demons faced or fallen to, deeds true to or against your nature, losing one close to you, your nature settling it each year) is now written beside karma and infamy, one line a year for each reason.
- **Life Devouring feeds on the years the slain had left.** It took two years of life from a kill of your own realm, whoever it was. It now takes a share of the years the slain still had to live, by the method's grade: a Yellow method 5%, each grade 5% more, a Primordial one 35% (a superb trait or a second copy more, at most half). A young genius with centuries ahead feeds you for decades; an old master at the end of their road, hardly at all. Those below your realm still give a third as much for each realm between you, and a beast half.
- **Those who loved or hated you can be born again.** A soul of Golden Core or above (or deep in a dao) bound to you (an opinion of you of 50 either way, a dao companion, master, disciple, sworn sibling, a heart won, an enemy) may pass through the wheel when it dies: half of them at Golden Core, more the greater the soul. Some sixteen years later it wakes as a youth in a town of the world below, with its old natures, a fragment of its old understanding, half its old feeling for you, and a heart's wish where its old one stood; its arts return as it climbs the realms. One who loved you comes looking for you. One who hated you swears to find you again, and becomes your nemesis if you have none (you feel the thread of karma tighten). Causality's Trace their kin now follows the thread through the wheel, to the next life of the dead and to the past life of a reborn soul. Only souls bound to you are reborn this way; the rest of the world goes on as before.
- **A spared enemy can come back.** Sparing a beaten foe changed only their opinion of you, unless they were arrogant. Now the vengeful, and anyone who has sworn to be your enemy, may go off, grow strong and come back as your nemesis.
- **Ascension is as rare as the era's qi.** Measured over twelve hundred years of history in each era, a world of withering qi saw someone ascend every 38 years, and a declining one every 3. The golden staircase now barely holds where qi has thinned: a world's cultivators come through the Ascension Tribulation a twelfth as often in an era of withering qi (about once in 240 years now), a third as often in decline (every 9 years), and a little more often in flourishing and golden eras; a balanced world is as it was. What a cultivator brings against the bolts (path, physique, armour, daos: the same toughness your own tribulation weighs) now counts toward their odds too, so the strongest in a world fare better. Your own tribulation is unchanged.
- **Blood refining and flesh forging: mortals rendered into materials.** Alchemy and forging scriptures can now be demonic (Blood Refining, Flesh Forging), as puppetry's Corpse Refining was; write one if your insight into Yin can bear it, or find one in a demonic sect's library. With one, a town or city offers Render its mortals: Blood Essence (a herb for the furnace) or Flesh Iron (an ore for the forge), of the aspect you choose, with a little poison always in it. The more mortals crammed into one piece, the stronger its aspect (three fifths of a wild material at ten mortals, twice at 160, nearly three times at a thousand), and the Dao of the Body sets how many a piece can hold (ten, plus ten for each point). Its grade is a realm below yours, never above Earth. A month takes a hundred mortals for each of your realms.
- **Soul Pearls.** With 10 insight into the Dao of the Soul and any demonic art, the souls of the rendered dead can be bound into Soul Pearls (five to a pearl, plus five for every point of the Dao of the Soul), or the souls alone can be harvested. A puppet given souls gains a spirit and strikes a tenth to a quarter harder; talismans burn their souls when used and strike a quarter stronger (Inventory, Bind souls into it); soul-fed arrays take pearls as they take a banner's souls.
- **Mortals are finite.** Every town and city has its people (a founded town its own count, others six thousand or thirty thousand), drawn on by both massacres and the cauldrons, and recovering a tenth of the loss each year. Each batch stains karma, spreads infamy and shakes the kingdom, and demonic sects now empty the towns near them the same way, filling their treasuries with what they render (the annals notice).
- **Faction secret realms.** A master of the Dao of Space (80 insight) can fold a secret realm out of space (Location, a year): for a faction they lead, founded or watch over as its ancestor (paid from its treasury), or for their own disciples and descendants (paid from their own stones). It admits those up to a realm below its maker and opens only when its owner pays (six months), its seats few and fewer the higher the realm (8 of Qi Condensation, 5 of Foundation Building, 3 of Golden Core, 2 of Nascent Soul, 1 of Deity Transformation), given out by an internal tournament. A member without a seat may challenge the weakest holder to a bout, and takes the seat by winning. Only seat holders may enter; those of the world come out stronger when it closes (none die in a realm made to train), some with a gift of the body or an art its maker knew. The world's factions with such a master raise one and open it every ten years or so; the Sect tab shows your faction's.
- **Karma runs from -1000 to +1000.** Every deed weighs ten times what it did, so nothing is easier or harder than before, but the Balanced middle is wider (within 300 either way; Virtuous and Stained past it, Saintly and Blood-soaked past 700). Old saves are converted on loading. Your karma now follows your soul into its next life, whole.
- **A death weighs by who died.** Killing someone of no karma costs 30 (as much as robbing a wounded stranger); a saint of +1000 costs 100; slaying one of -1000 earns 98 (the heavens are fair), and a demonic cultivator counts as -200 at least. Each realm you stand above them costs 10 more. As before, killing those who came for you, or your enemies, stains nothing; slaying the wicked still earns its merit then.
- **Karma shown where it is decided.** A fight's header names your foe's infamy and, if you can read it (10 insight into the Dao of Karma, 30 Causality, or two realms above them), their karma and what killing them would weigh. The mercy buttons, Attack in the Interact menu, massacres and the rendering of mortals all say what they will cost.
- **The farther your karma from the middle, the more plainly you grasp the Dao of Karma**: twice as fast from your deeds at either end.
- **The world answers karma at its ends** (700 either way). The blood-soaked are hunted by bands of the righteous and, rarely, judged by an envoy of heaven a realm above them; saints are stalked by demonic assassins and, rarely, a fiend from below. The upright warm to a virtuous player and recoil from a wicked one, and the demonic the other way. A natal treasure takes on its bearer's karma: a saint's grows a light that mends them (regeneration 3, qi spring 2), a fiend's a hunger that drains their foes (blood-drinking 8%, qi-siphoning 4%). A call to hunt a saint or a fiend of the world may reach you in a town; join, and the kill weighs as any other. The world's people settle into the karma their natures and sects lead them to, so there are saints and fiends to hunt.
- **The ledger's dao heart, the heavens' disfavour and every karma threshold** (secret bodies, a heart's wish, crusades, kin naming blessings, karmic theft) have moved to the new scale. The heavens' disfavour falls at -500, and again at -900 (the second step could never be reached before).
- **Biomes.** Every wild place is a land of its own kind, drawn from its region (a coast's wilds are a spirit sea, a desert's burning dunes, a highland's frozen tundra), and one in eight is a wonder: jade caverns, floating isles, a mountain of swords. Each has a dao and an element, shown on the Location and Map tabs: its dao is contemplated 40% more readily there, and its element's materials and beasts are found half again as often. A Nascent Soul with 60 insight into the Dao of Earth or of Space can remake a wild place into another biome (two years), and a clan or sect founded on wild land keeps its biome. Worlds are drawn as they were: the biome is read from the place itself.
- **Wear another's face.** With 20 insight into the Dao of Illusion (or 40 puppetry), take a strand of someone's hair unseen (Relations, Interact), then shape their face over your own (Character tab, a month). Those close to them take you for them: a year of dual cultivation with their dao companion, their master's guidance, help from their kin and friends, or turning them against the one you imitate. Infamy and the grudges of those whose kin you kill fall on them, not you (karma stays yours). Anyone two realms above sees through it at once, one realm above now and then; deeds against their nature (killing when they are kind, fighting with an element their root lacks) raise suspicion, and when it reaches 100 the face falls, and they and theirs become your enemies. The world's thieves wear famous faces too: someone may be living under yours.
- **Karma deeds rebalanced for the new scale.** Killing someone of no karma now costs 30 (it was 2, lighter than robbing a wounded stranger at 60); a saint still costs 100 and a fiend of -1000 still earns 98. A massacre weighs 150, plus 60 for each tenfold of the dead (about 380 for a town; it used to weigh barely ten murders), and rendering mortals weighs 60, plus 60 for each tenfold (about 210 for 300). Every other deed keeps its weight: ten times its old number.
- **Schools of thought.** Every sect and clan follows Daoism, Buddhism, Confucianism or Legalism, shown on the Sect tab and at its gate. Daoists contemplate the daos a quarter more readily. Buddhists gain half again as much from good deeds and waver a quarter less, and a Buddhist sect's monks take no dao companions: one who takes one breaks their vows and leaves the temple (a Buddhist clan's faithful marry, or the clan would end). Confucians with a master or a parent in the house cultivate a tenth faster, and the house stands behind its head (approval +10). Legalists collect half again under the same tithe, and order holds (approval +15). Clans lean Confucian, righteous sects Daoist or Buddhist, demonic sects Legalist, and some demonic temples keep the Buddha's robes. A head may turn the house to another school once every fifty years, at a cost to their mandate.
- **A tribe's chief can no longer be killed again and again.** When a tribe's chief died, the tribe kept its dead chief until the end of the year, and the challenge stayed on the Location tab: fighting the body won its spoils every time. The strongest of the tribe now takes the lead the moment the chief falls (or the tribe scatters), and only a living chief can be challenged.
- **Pregnancy.** A child now grows in the womb before it is born: ten months in a mortal, six months more for every realm of the greater parent (over two years for a Golden Core, four for Deity Transformation). This holds for the whole world, not only you.
- **Imparting into an unborn child.** While it grows, its parents can lay gifts into it (Relations, Interact with the mother; the Character tab if you carry it), each kind up to three times, each at a price and a longer term: knowledge (comprehension), a dao (born attuned to it), your body (your physique, from Nascent Soul, for 20 years of your life), luck, good karma, or qi (a purer root and firmer foundation). Those who hold either parent dear (90 or more) come to bless the child once a year. The five mightiest houses nurture their elders' children the same way, so their heirs are born far ahead of the common lot.
- **The Netherworld.** A world of its own below the living, open to anyone with 60 insight into the Dao of Yin or of the Soul (a month's walk, from anywhere). Yin, Soul and Karma come 60% more readily there; its beasts are of the dead and its treasures of water and ice. The Ten Courts weigh your karma once in ten years (ten years of life for the virtuous, the Judge of the Dead for the blood-soaked); the Terrace of Looking Homeward shows the souls you knew who wait on the wheel, and lets you hasten one; Meng Po's soup at the Naihe Bridge washes the heart clean, at the cost of a fifth of your deepest dao.
- **The Demon Realm.** Now and then a rift tears open in the wilds for three years, and demons raid the nearest town each year until a righteous sect seals it or you kill the demon general holding it open (good karma +100). Step through, and the abyss holds treasures of fire and lightning, Fire, Poison and Devouring contemplated 60% more readily, and a Demon Lord of Deity Transformation in its citadel with a hoard, who rises again fifty years after falling. Both planes are walled off from the world of the living as the clouds wall off the Upper Realm: no road leads there, and the map shows only the plane you stand in.
- **The Netherworld and the Demon Realm have beings of their own.** Twenty new kinds live there and nowhere else, so the planes no longer hold the qilins and golden crows of the world above. In the Netherworld: hungry and wandering ghosts, hopping corpses, water ghosts, bone serpents, the Ox-Head and Horse-Face guards of the Ten Courts, nether ravens, the truth-hearing Diting and the ghost kings. In the Demon Realm: imps, blood demons, brimstone hounds, asura warriors, abyssal centipedes, heart demons, hellfire wyverns, rakshasas, abyss behemoths and heavenly demons. A demon rift in the world of the living lets the demons out: the beasts met where one stands are demons. Each has its own core, and a tamed one evolves (a Wandering Ghost into a Vengeful Spirit and on to the Ghost Emperor of the Yellow Springs, a Lesser Imp into an Imp Chieftain...).
- **Soul arts rest on the Dao of the Soul.** A Heavenly realm on the Soul path asks for the Soul's concepts, yet soul-nurturing methods were made from the Dao of Yin, soul attacks drew on Yin and soul wards on Ice, so a soul cultivator had to split their years among three daos. Now soul-nurturing methods, soul attacks and soul wards are made from, and grow with, the Dao of the Soul; so do gripping a mind (mesmerism), destroying a soul (20 insight), tearing your soul free into a living body (40) and healing a scarred soul. Soul searching deepens the Soul, not Yin. Soul banners stay with Yin: they command ghosts. Yin's Moon concept still strengthens soul arts.
- **The side columns are split by use.** The right column holds only the buttons; what the next breakthrough needs, epiphanies, "In your abode" and the face you wear have moved to the left, which scrolls.

## 0.13.25 (2026-11-12)

- **Long work no longer freezes the game.** Contemplating a dao for years, studying or refining an art, crafting, rearing gu, building an abode or a den, a year of dual cultivation, working a job, and about a hundred other things that take months all passed their time inside one press: the game stood still until it was over (five years of contemplation is most of a second on a fast PC just after starting, and several seconds on a phone or a weak laptop). They now pass the way seclusion does: at once while that is quick (a quarter of a second), and past that in the window that shows time passing, with the year, your realm and the months gone by. A contemplation can be risen from there ("Emerge now"); a craft or an art's making goes on to its end, since what it took is already spent. What each of them does is unchanged, month for month.
- **Such work is never saved half done.** If the game is closed while one is under way it is taken to its end first. On a phone put away in the middle of one, nothing is written and it goes on when you come back; if the phone ends the game there, your last save stands, with the work not begun.
- **Sects are weighed once a year.** In each year's turn the world asked how strong every sect was again and again (who contests a spirit vein, who is mightiest for a Dao Abode, who marches on whom), weighing every member each time. Each member is now weighed once in the turn. A wound taken in that turn's war no longer lowers a sect's might until the next year; the dead are never counted. Your own strength, and anything you read or do, always goes by how things stand now. A year in an old world is a few milliseconds shorter for it.

## 0.13.24 (2026-11-12)

- **Duels nobody watches are settled by strength, and the year passes faster for it.** When two of the world's people fought far from you (a clash in a sect war, a vendetta, a challenge for a seat, a brawl in town), the game fought the whole duel out blow by blow, twenty rounds of it between equals, to learn who won. It now weighs the two fighters' strength (the same strength the Heaven ranking goes by) and settles it at the odds those full fights gave: measured over eleven thousand of them in six old worlds, then held side by side with the new way. Between equals either may win; half again as strong wins nine times in ten; twice as strong, thirty times in thirty-one; a realm apart is no contest. About half the losers die in the fight, the rest are left senseless, yield or run, and one who means to kill (an enemy, the ruthless) strikes to kill. Winners come away wounded as they did, sometimes with a wound that lingers. What follows is unchanged: mercy or execution, spoils, fame, mourning. A year in an old world is 7 to 11% faster (wars alone took 15 to 22 ms of a 155 ms year, now 3 to 8), and ninety years lived both ways leave the same number of people alive.
- **What is still fought out in full:** every fight of yours, spars and tournaments, and any fight where others stand beside the two (companions, a pack, an avatar). No single duel will match what it would have been, so the world's history from here on differs from what it would have been; its shape does not.

## 0.13.23 (2026-11-12)

- **Craft unions: every world has a union for each trade, and grades its crafters.** The Alchemists', Artifact Refiners', Talisman Masters', Formation Masters', Puppeteers' and Spirit Farmers' Unions each keep a hall in one of the great cities (Causality and gu are not trades, and have none). A union grades in tiers that follow the item grades: a Tier 1 Alchemist refines Yellow-grade pills, a Tier 4 one Heaven-grade. Tiers 6 and 7 are examined above the clouds. The Professions tab shows your union at each craft, and a city's Market lists the halls there.
- **A yearly exam, played as the craft itself.** Each union holds its exam once a year, in a month of its own, at its hall. The examiners name a work of the tier's grade (a design the world knows, drawn at random, or one drawn up for the day) and you make it before them with the union's materials, in the craft's own minigame; a spirit farmer is set a season in the fields with a herb of the grade. Pass at 45% at Tier 1, five points more each tier, and the work is yours to keep. Each tier is passed in turn.
- **Seats are few, and the mighty hold them.** Sitting the exam takes a seat: 12 a year at Tier 1, 8 at Tier 2, then 5, 3, 2, 2 and 1 at the top. At each new year they go to the mightiest factions, the mightier holding more, who fill them with their own crafters and keep about one in four open. Your own sect's open seat is free; another faction sells you one (100 spirit stones at Tier 1, six times as much each tier). A seat unused in its year is lost.
- **A grade is worth something.** Every work a graded crafter makes in that trade carries the union's hallmark (⚒ before its name), worth a tenth more for each tier wherever it is sold. The union's market at its hall opens to its graded members: the trade's materials up to a grade above their tier and its works up to their tier, at nine tenths of the usual price, with new lots each month. The world's crafters sit the same exams: those already in a world when it gains its unions are graded by their skill, and more rise each year, their shops and sect halls selling hallmarked work.

## 0.13.22 (2026-11-12)

- **Years pass faster in an old world, and the world comes out exactly the same.** A long seclusion runs about a tenth faster once the game has been running a while (in a world of year 2645, a year went from 0.17 s to 0.15 s), and the first years after loading, which are the slowest, about a seventh faster (0.44 s to 0.37 s a year there). What was slow, and no longer is:
  - what someone holds or wears was searched for through their whole bag each time it was asked, several times for every art weighed in a fight and every month for everyone alive; where each piece lies is now remembered;
  - gear that raises attributes was added up seven times over, once for each attribute, every month;
  - each sect, looking for its members' children come of age, asked every member about every person alive without a sect;
  - a member looking over the sect's storehouse noted down every thing in it, wanted or not, and did it again for a second pick;
  - the mightiest sects weighed their whole strength again for every Dao Abode they might seize;
  - in a fight nobody watches, each fighter listed every move of every art several times a round to pick one.
  Seven old worlds, lived three to five years both ways, came out the same, person for person.
- **Fewer small pauses while time passes.** The game throws away a quarter to a third less memory each year (80 MB down to 55 MB a year in that world), so it stops less often to clear it. This helps most on a phone.

## 0.13.21 (2026-11-12)

- **Support arts: a new kind of art that strikes no blow.** Outside healing, the arts that were not attacks, guards or footwork did nothing in a fight. A support art's moves are bargains, each a gain with a price, and they take hold before the round's blows as a guard does. There are 37 of them. Borrowed Time holds your wounds back and lands them all at once, a fifth heavier, when it ends. Shared Fate makes a burn, venom, slow, bind, seal or stun that takes you take your foe too. A Drawn Circle fixes the distance where it stands. Tortoise Breath brings qi back three times as fast, under a token guard. Turn the Tide trades momentum with your foe. Scattered Wound spreads a blow over the whole body so it severs nothing; an Offered Limb draws every blow to one arm. Each has an answer, said beside it.
- **Fields fall on everyone.** Thirteen of the moves lay a field over the whole fight, you included: Carnage (every blow lands harder), Heavy Snow (softer, though burning, venom and bleeding are untouched), Measured Ground (no blow takes more than a fifth of a part), Dead Calm (qi stops returning), Calling the Rain (everyone soaked, so element reactions go off), Settled Odds (what is likelier than not happens, what is not does not), Daybreak (nothing hidden: no afterimage, phantom, feint or cloak), Moonless Night (no aiming, no reading), and more. Only one field stands at a time, so a field is also the answer to a field.
- **A support move is tempered on reach, span and ease.** Like any move (Arts tab, Temper a move, Support): reach is how strong the gain is, span how long it lasts (a round longer from 74), ease how light the price is (about three eighths lighter at 100; from 74 some moves open a kinder form, and a field's ease lightens your own half). A support art is of one dao; you choose which, and the tab lists that dao's moves with what each still asks of you. Any path can make and use one.
- **Three new daos: Illusion, the Soul and Devouring**, each with five concepts, a domain and a perfection. Illusion owns deception: its concepts keep an afterimage alive past the blow it fooled, make your feints and illusions surer and others' less so, veil your intent from a divine sense, and leave a foe who missed you off balance. Its moves make a foe believe a limb gone, turn one against their own side, or send them dreaming on their feet. The Soul is the Soul path's own dao now, as the Body is the Body path's: a Heavenly realm on the Soul path asks its concepts, not Yin's (anyone already walking the Soul path knows the Soul as deeply as they knew Yin). Devouring is the demonic way's dao: devouring blows heal and steal more, kills feed more, and a demonic method wears the foundation less.
- **Eleven new fused daos**: Gravity (Earth and Space), Fate (Karma and Time), Light (Yang and Fire), Darkness (Yin and Water), Cosmos (Space and Time), Magma (Fire and Earth), Blizzard (Ice and Wind), Forge (Metal and Fire), Plague (Poison and Yin), Requiem (Sound and Yin) and Dream (Illusion and Yin). Each gives its own gift, and each has support moves that ask insight in both its parts and are up to a fifth stronger for one who has fused them: Heavy Sky drops flying weapons and drains momentum, Thread Uncut refuses one killing blow for years of your life, Whiteout stops anyone far apart from striking, Red-Hot Grip burns the hand that holds a weapon.
- **Everyone else uses them too.** Sect libraries hold support arts of their own dao, cultivators of Golden Core and above compose them, and in a fight each is used for its moment (a foe with a surge of intent has it taken; a sect elder near death refuses the blow). The battle's advice names a support move when its moment is plain, and says why. What a move leaves in force shows as a chip on the fighter, and the field over the fight on everyone.

## 0.13.20 (2026-11-12)

- **The first years after starting the game run two to three times faster (PC).** The game's code only reaches full speed after it has run for a while, so in an old world the first twenty or thirty years after every launch took three times as long as later ones. While the main menu is showing, a copy of your latest journey is now lived for a few years in the background and thrown away, so the code is already fast when you continue. In a world of year 2362 a year right after launch went from 0.46 s to about 0.2 s. Nothing of the copy is kept, and your journey is not touched.
- **The window that shows time passing no longer freezes once a year.** Every January the whole year's turn (about ninety parts: sects, wars, births, markets) ran in one go, a pause of a tenth of a second in an old world and over half a second just after launch. It is now taken a part at a time between frames. The longest pause fell to about a quarter of what it was, and the world comes out exactly the same.
- **Saving interrupts less.** A save used to hold the game still while it was written out, compressed and stored. Now only the writing out holds it (about 0.25 s instead of 0.4 s in an old world on PC); the rest happens in the background. A save that has come due also waits for a moment when you are not pressing anything (with no window open, or while you read a message), so the pause falls while you read rather than on a press. The first save after loading no longer falls on your first action. This matters most on a phone, where every save froze the game for about a second, on a tap, about once a minute of play. A very large world on a phone (a save over 6 MB) is still written in one go, since writing it behind needs a second copy of the save in memory.
- **On a phone, the game saves when it is put away.** Switching to another app, going to the home screen or turning the screen off saved nothing, and a phone may end a game it has put away without a word: whatever you had done since the last autosave was lost (in the report that showed it, a hidden cache dug up seconds before). What is unsaved is now written as the game is put away. With a battle open nothing is written: you come back to it and choose its ending as before, and if the game is ended there the last save stands. Once the game has seen such a save go through on your phone, its timed autosave comes every two minutes instead of every twenty seconds (each one holds a phone still for about a second); the time is yours to change in Settings, and a time you had already chosen is kept.
- **Continue shows that it is loading.** Reading a journey held the main menu frozen with nothing to say why: four seconds on a phone, about one on PC in an old world. The journey is now read in the background behind a turning "Loading" sign, with the menu alive. The same goes for loading from the list of journeys and for opening a world for a new character.
- **A year no longer freezes a slow device.** A stretch of a year or less always passed inside one press, however long that took: a year of gathering held a phone still for nearly two seconds. Time now passes at once only while it is quick (a quarter of a second); past that, the window that shows time passing takes over, with its progress and its "Emerge now". This covers the cultivation and rest buttons, months of gathering and months of seeking with divine sense. On a fast PC a year is over before the window would open, so nothing changes there. Gathering and seeking come out exactly as before, month for month.
- **Quick save takes half as long.** Saving to a slot wrote the whole world out twice, once for the journey and once for the slot (over two seconds on a phone). The slot now takes a copy of the save just written.
- **A seclusion under way is saved when you close the game.** Closing the window (or putting a phone away) in the middle of a long seclusion begun right after a save lost every year of it. The save now keeps the months that have passed.
- **The log says where the time goes.** Its lines starting with `[perf]` give how long each save held the game and how long the rest took in the background, how long a load and each stretch of time took, and every press that held the game still for a tenth of a second or more, by name. A report from a slow phone now shows which presses freeze, and for how long. The report copied from Settings begins with a summary of those timings and is cut to fit a message: a long one arrived cut off at about 20,000 characters, with the earlier runs (a crashed one among them) lost. Most of its room now goes to a run that crashed, or else to this one, and its last line says it has ended.

## 0.13.19 (2026-11-11)

- **Old worlds save and load about twice as fast.** In a world a thousand years old, two thirds of every save was the bags, arts and daos of people centuries dead, written out whole each time the game saved. Fifty years after someone dies, the world now keeps only who they were: name, kin, dates, how they died, realm, fame, sect, root and bloodline. The family tree, a clan's ancestral hall and readings of the dead show what they showed before. In a world of year 2362, saving went from 0.8 s to 0.4 s, the file from 13.6 MB to 6.1 MB, and loading from 2 s to 1 s. The ascended, ghosts, your own past lives, everyone you knew and anyone whose soul was reborn are kept whole. The world runs exactly as it would have: thirty years lived both ways came out the same, person for person. An older save is slimmed the first time it is loaded, which takes about a second longer, once.

## 0.13.18 (2026-11-11)

- **Exporting a save checks the copy.** Export used to write the file and report success without looking at it. A copy cut short (here, 6 MB of a 36 MB save, the rest zeros) was only found out when another machine refused it. The file is now read back and compared with the save. A mismatch says the export failed, so you can try again while the original is still at hand.
- **Importing a cut-off save says what happened.** It used to give a confusing parsing error. It now says the file was cut short and to export it again from the original machine.

## 0.13.17 (2026-11-11)

- **The heavenly avatar fights to the death.** It was a bout, so losing only failed the tribulation. It now wounds and kills like any foe. If it wins and you live, the tribulation is still lost. Its body leaves the world after the fight, and nothing can be devoured from it.
- **The world's people use the new pills.** NPCs now take:
  - stench out in dangerous wilds;
  - fertility with a dao companion and fewer than three children;
  - beast calming when they have no beast of their own (it makes their taming likelier);
  - blood burning in a close fight.

  The ruthless and treacherous slip heart-bending pills to those they hate (-60 or worse), turning an upright nature to a darker one.
- **The Ascension Tribulation feels the heavens' disfavour.** It used to ignore it. It now brings the extra bolts, the heart demon, the avatar and the sealed wards like any other tribulation, and the world's ascenders face lower odds too.

## 0.13.16 (2026-11-11)

- **The heavens' disfavour at a tribulation.** Four things each turn the heavens a step against you, up to four steps: heavy bad karma (-50, and again at -150), a talent beyond the common lot (60), and a talent so great it affronts heaven (72). The tribulation says why before it starts.
  - One step brings two more bolts.
  - Two steps bring a heart demon first, beaten back by your dao heart. If you lose, your foundation cracks and the lightning comes crueler.
  - Three steps bring a heavenly avatar of your own realm and arts, fought in a bout before the lightning. If you lose, the tribulation is lost.
  - Four steps seal your treasures, so no talisman, treasure or formation wards you.

  The world's cultivators face the same odds. Surviving it steadies your dao heart, three for each step.
- **The heavens' will, a choice when a world is made.** Now and then, for decades, the heavens favour or turn against one thing:
  - a dao (its insight 20% easier or harder for each tier of the tide);
  - a path (cultivation 10% a tier);
  - one sect or clan, the righteous or the demonic, every sect or every clan, or the beasts that cultivate (cultivation 5% and breakthroughs 3% a tier).

  Tides come in three tiers, and up to three run at once. The Location tab lists the tides the world can see. A clear reading of Heaven's calendar tells whether each one is the heavens' will or only chance, and until when. Worlds can be made without them.
- **Merchant houses.** A sect or clan of the new Commerce specialty lives by trade, and you can found one. Every town and city of its region, and any it holds, is a trade post that pays it each year as much as that market can spare. Caravans set out under its flag and pay it a tenth of their cargo where they arrive. Rob one, and the house remembers. Greedy founders favour it. The Sect tab shows the house's trade.

## 0.13.15 (2026-11-11)

- **The ages decide how many take the demonic shortcut.** A golden or flourishing age now sees far fewer demonic sects founded and a declining or withered one far more. The age weighs on each by nature: the ruthless, treacherous and greedy feel it most, while the compassionate and honourable resist. Rogue cultivators stuck at their bottleneck may now give up and join a demonic sect, most often in a withered age.
- **Karmic Theft, a new Causality rite.** It draws off someone's good karma to wash out your own stains, 10 to 40 of their merit by how well it is cast. It needs skill 55, 50 insight into Yin and Karma, and a belonging of theirs. The world's stained masters use it on those they hate. (Stealing luck was already Borrowed Fortune.)
- **The Dao of Slaughter from mortals.** In a town or city, "Slaughter its mortals..." spends a month putting it to the sword. The dao deepens a little by the number of the dead (a thousand lives are worth little more than a hundred), and less the higher your realm. Karma darkens greatly, infamy spreads and the kingdom shakes. A demonic host's master grows the same way from the towns it burns.
- **Life Devouring, a demonic method trait.** What its kills devour becomes years of life instead of qi. A kill of your own realm and stage gives two years, a third as many for each realm below you, and three times as many for each realm above, at most 30.
- **Trace their kin, a new reading.** Each reading finds the next of someone's kin: parents first, then dao companions, children and siblings. It shows who they are and where, and gives their name and birth hour as a medium for a rite. The picker puts your enemies first.
- **A puppetry shop no longer sells assistant puppets it does not deal in.** A keeper's puppet assistant counts as a tool, and stayed on puppetry shelves where it could not be bought or sold back. Shops now stock only what they deal in.

## 0.13.14 (2026-11-11)

- **Talismans are worth using.** A talisman cost as much to make as a sword, burned in a single round, and came out one or two at a time. Now a batch is three, and one more for every 25 skill (seven at 100).
- **Talismans carry three effects.** With two, a herb ink's effect crowded out an ore ink's, so the two inks were never mixed. Now a talisman carries three effects, the third at 60% of its strength.
- **Strong talisman ink is no longer wasted past a cap.** One fine herb ink reached every talisman cap, so eight wrote the same talisman as one. Now the surplus does more:
  - a seal past three rounds of binding seals the meridians too;
  - a veil past 50% lasts a round longer for every 25, up to six;
  - a qi talisman past 60% restores divine sense;
  - fury past 100 momentum builds toward the next surge.
- **Five new pills, made from two aspects together.** A pill whose materials carry both of two aspects gains a further effect, as strong as the lesser of the two. The Guide's crafting topic lists the pairs.
  - **Stench (Toxic + Swift):** beasts shun you for months.
  - **Blood burning (Essence + Sharp):** in battle, blood burned for more harm for three rounds.
  - **Fertility (Longevity + Essence):** a child conceived within the year is born with a purer root.
  - **Heart bending (Spirit + Toxic):** slipped to someone (Relations, Interact), it turns one of their natures into another you choose, at a cost to your karma; if it fails, they know your hand.
  - **Beast calming (Spirit + Vitality):** likelier to tame a beast for a year.
- **The Qi path can absorb spirit stones.** Inventory: Absorb spirit stones draws a handful for half a month of your own cultivation: a hundred stones in Qi Condensation, four times as many each realm up. The stones crumble to dust, and each draw counts as one of the month's absorptions.

## 0.13.13 (2026-11-11)

- **You choose a grand formation's disks, and their make counts.** Raising one used the best disks you carried, and only the weakest disk's grade mattered. Now you pick "the best I carry" or each disk yourself. The disks' quality sets the formation's strength: 70% for disks of no quality, 100% at middling, 130% for flawless ones. That strength reaches everything the formation does: its barrier, its veil, the qi it gathers and drains, its sword domain.
- **No more hard cap on an abode's qi, concealment and protection.** Arrays counted in full up to ×2.5 qi and level 5 of concealment and protection, and nothing beyond. Past those, a quarter of what more you add now still counts.
- **Where your abode stands shapes its fields.** Marshes, lakes and coasts keep the beds wet and bring more rain, while deserts and wastes dry them and bring drought. Rich qi (a spirit vein most of all) gives every bed more vigour and more qi tides, and thin qi less. Strong beasts nearby bring a pest more for every realm past Foundation Building, and more locusts. The season's opening line says what the land brings.
- **An abode can be moved whole.** From Nascent Soul, with 50 insight into both the Dao of Earth and the Dao of Space, "Move it..." lifts your abode with its rooms, storehouse, arrays, hands, fields and what grows in them. It sets them down one step of road away, one more step for every 10 Space insight past 50. It takes two months and one more per step.
- **The land itself can be raised.** Vitality-rich materials now make Earth-Nourishing Arrays. Set up at your abode or planted loose, they nourish the land, and every few years it rises a level, up to three. Each level lets what is gathered or dug there (your hands' digging too) be a grade finer. The Location tab shows the land's level and how long until the next.

## 0.13.12 (2026-11-11)

- **Bloated saves shrink when they load.** A player's save carried one dead elder's ten belongings 4,115 times over, the same items again and again, and grew to 36 MB (most of it that one bag). A save now keeps each item in a bag once when it loads, and the world probe counts any repeats so the cause can be caught. Where they came from is not found yet.
- **A demonic host can be met where it stands.** The Approach button only shows when the host is where you are, so a host one step away could be watched but never met. Now a "Go to ..." button takes you there. Its line also says how much the coalition wears it down each month: its strength falling month by month was the coalition at work.
- **Quick save on the main screen.** A Quick save button beside Save slots keeps the moment in the quick slot, as Ctrl+S does.
- **Other people's paths can be seen, and opened.** A person's sheet and the Interact window list every path they walk, each with its realm when your divine sense can gauge them. A path you have built to Foundation Building can be opened in your children and descendants, disciples and dao companions (Interact: Open a path in them, a month together, from age 12). Golden Core elders and parents of the world now and then do the same for theirs.

## 0.13.11 (2026-11-11)

- **High realms are harder to kill.** Every body part had the same 100 to lose at every realm, so once one side was clearly stronger, any blow that landed destroyed what it struck, and a single hit on the heart ended a fight between immortals. From Golden Core, the body is now harder to break, more with each realm: a quarter harder at Golden Core on the Qi path and for beasts, up to more than twice as hard at Golden Immortal. It grows faster on the Body path and slower on the Soul path, and the sea of consciousness hardens with the rest. Fights between equals of Golden Core and Nascent Soul now last about 20 and 25 rounds, where they took about 15.
- **A ruined vital organ can be held together.** From Golden Core, a blow that would destroy the heart, lungs, brain, spine, head or neck is paid off instead, with what you fight with. Body cultivators and beasts (yours, ones you play, and ones you fight) spend 20 blood, so bleeding wears their reserve down. Qi cultivators pour in 25% of their battle qi. Soul cultivators spend half their divine sense to drive the body on without the organ, then a fifth more every round it stays ruined, until the sense gives out and the organ with it. Each hold costs again, and when nothing is left the organ goes. A severed head is beyond holding. The new "Vitals hold" chip in battle says what yours costs and how often it has held. The paths stand against each other, and humans against beasts, about as they did before.
- **Piercing is no longer unstoppable.** It struck 15% harder, passed most of a shield, and reached the organ behind a part up to 95% of the time, so one piercing art could carry a fight on its own. It no longer strikes harder, it reaches organs at most 60% of the time, and a shelled beast turns half of those aside, as a shell turns edges.
- **Herbalism's sections fold.** Spirit soil, Extract seeds (with how many herbs you carry) and Cross strains now fold like the other profession sections.

## 0.13.10 (2026-11-11)

- **A whole concept is 100.** A concept counted as whole from 99.5, so a contemplation ended with "Moon 94.9 → 99.5... is whole", the Character tab rounded it to 100, and it gave 99.5% of what it promised (+14.9% where it said +15%). A concept that reaches 99.5 is now made 100 at once, and one left at 99.5 or above by an older save is made 100 when it loads.

## 0.13.9 (2026-11-11)

- **A Black-and-White Fortune Cat prowls above the clouds.** A new beast of Golden Immortal in the Upper Realm, half shadow and half light: it sinks into its shadow so a blow strikes only that, burns venom and death qi out of itself every round, and dodges single blows unless held. Its Beckoning Paw of Fortune strikes at the soul, and like the great cats it goes for the spine. It carries a core of its own, and its blood is the luckiest of any beast's (+20 Luck at full purity, with Agility and Charm) and quickens insight into Karma.

## 0.13.8 (2026-11-11)

- **Learning an art takes time.** The sect library, a guest elder's library, an immortal sect's pavilion and a manual handed over an art at once, as many as you could pay for. Studying one now takes a month for a Yellow art, two for Mystic, three for Earth, five for Heaven and eight for Immortal. The library and pavilion rows say how long it will take you.
- **Insight, gifts and root make every study faster.** Learning, tempering moves, binding them and making any other art are all shortened by insight into the art's dao beyond what its grade asks, by comprehension with the art's own attribute, and by a root of its element. Together they cut the time to as little as a twentieth: someone with 100 insight, 100 comprehension and a perfect root makes or learns anything up to Heaven grade almost at once.
- **Making your own arts is quicker.** A move takes as many months as its grade (it was one more), binding moves one fewer, and any other art three months a grade and one (it was four and two). A Yellow attack art is about 6 months of work instead of 11, an Earth one 16 instead of 21.
- **A higher realm makes lesser arts with less insight.** Making an art asked the same insight whatever your realm, so a Nascent Soul wanting an Earth art of a new dao still had decades of contemplation ahead. An art a grade below your realm now asks two thirds of the insight, two grades below half, three below two fifths (a Nascent Soul makes an Earth art with 28 instead of 42). Refining and stripping an art follow the same rule.
- **Arts made by another must be made your own.** From Earth grade, an art someone else made is cut for their meridians, not yours: until you attune it, it strikes, guards and heals weaker and costs more qi (Earth 5%, Heaven 10%, Immortal and above 20%). Attune it from the Arts tab once you have 60% mastery (its grade in months, shortened as studies are), or refine it into your own variant. Arts you create always fit. The world's cultivators attune theirs too, one a year. Every art known before this version counts as attuned.

- **A first whole concept fits in a Foundation Building life.** A Heavenly breakthrough at Foundation Building asks one concept of your main dao whole, but the last stretch of a concept crawled: an ordinary contemplator needed about 500 years, against a life of 220. The deepest open concept of each dao now leads it: it deepens twice as fast and never slows below a tenth of its first pace (about 136 years ordinary, 46 good, 21 at the best). The second whole concept, asked at Golden Core, is as long a labour as before.
- **Qi Condensation contemplation counts.** No concept opens before Foundation Building, so everything past 15 came at a crawl. Now the deepest concept of each dao is half open there: it grows freely to 30 and keeps it when you open it, so a dao can reach 18 at Qi Condensation.
- **Roads for your town.** A fifth work beside the market, clinic, walls and academy, up to level 3. Each level makes journeys to and from the town a quarter faster (for anyone on foot; fliers go over them), gives its merchants a larger share of the world's trade (the caravans keep a fuller purse there, and a fuller purse pays its taxes in full), and adds tolls of a tenth more taxes.
- **Granting land no longer overcrowds a town.** Each grant opened room for 400 souls whatever the town's size, while an eighth of the town came for the fields at once, so a large town overflowed with every grant and stayed overfull. A grant now opens room for a fifth of the town (at least 400), and the settlers who come at once never fill more than half of it. A town with more people than room takes in no one, and a quarter of those without room leave each year; the town panel says so in red.
- **The world's founders tend their towns.** A town founded by someone of the world now and then gets land when it fills, or its least-built work, roads among them.
- **Waking from seclusion tells only what is new.** "While You Were Away" listed every dao companion and enemy who had ever ascended (one in year 366, another in 760) each time the doors opened. It now tells only of those who ascended while you were inside, and of those above the clouds who rose a realm.
- **A companion bound by force stays bound.** One bound by the weight of your realm walked out the year you took another companion, "having had enough of sharing you". A bound companion has no say in whom else you take now: they break the bond only once they reach your realm and could best you in a fight.
- **A tamed legend is yours.** A legend of the wilds you tamed kept wandering back to its lairs, still marked as a beast to hunt, and turning it human did not end that. A legend ends when its beast is anyone's, or a person; a beast king's lair the same.
- **No world's heavens allow an immortal among its mortals.** A world's maker, walking below the clouds of the world they shaped, could tear a rift into another world and step out among its mortals as a Golden Immortal. A maker now crosses from the Upper Realm only, an immortal always arrives above the clouds, and one already stranded below another world's clouds is drawn up when the save loads.
- **Battle talent grows in group battles.** Storming a clan or fighting in a sect's war tempered nothing: only the enemy's leader counted, and only for your side's leader. Every foe of your realm or above who falls now counts for you, wherever you fight in the line.
- **Born attributes go to 100.** Choosing a life point by point capped attributes at 90, and a sandbox life at 150. Both stop at 100 now.
- **Seeking a fate picks bodies and blood from a searchable list.** The body and bloodline dropdowns ran past the bottom of the screen, and a phone could not reach the rest. They open the same searchable list as point buy.
- **Eye colour shows.** The iris was a single pixel, and from Golden Core it was painted gold, so the colour chosen never showed. It is two pixels now, with a golden light over it. A man's long, loose hair falls past his shoulders.
- **An enlightenment never lowers a craft.** A craft past what the realm above holds read "Alchemy: 95 → 80"; it now reads "95 (already past 80)", and pouring an enlightenment into a craft can only raise it.
- **Quick craft all, without substitutes.** A second button crafts again and again only while the exact materials last, so a batch never spends a rarer stand-in.
- **All in.** Sealing an inheritance has an All in button (everything carried, every art, every stone) and a None.
- **Find places by qi.** The map's Richest qi button lists places by their qi, richest first; type "qi 2" for ×2 or more.
- **No more nameless pills.** A work with an empty name read "takes a :" and "Spent or lost: 1× ." in the chronicle. A blank name is never given now, and old saves name such works when they load.
- **A Yellow art asks 10 insight.** Creating, refining or stripping a Yellow art needed 14 insight in its dao, nearly all a Qi Condensation cultivator could hold; it needs 10 now (Mystic 28 and on are unchanged).
- **A beast's heir is a beast.** Carrying on a spirit beast's will as its young, the second generation lost every rule of a beast's life: no beast cores came from its kills however many it slew, it cultivated without a beast's instinct and its blood gave half what it should, and it could trade and craft like a person. Every generation of a beast line now lives as a beast, and a journey already in this state is mended when it loads.
- **Carry arts through the wheel.** A soul choosing its next life point by point can spend those points on arts it knew: each costs its grade (a cultivation method one more), and the new life is born knowing it at half the old mastery. Arts not carried still return as memories at the breakthroughs.
- **Be reborn as a spirit beast.** A soul strong enough to reincarnate can be born again as a young beast of any line, shaping its blood with the points its past life earned instead of a beast's usual ten, and carrying the arts of its old life that its new body can strike with (claws, fangs, its breath...). A beast that is left to the wheel without choosing is born again of its own line, and the memories that return to a reborn beast pass over arts it cannot use.

## 0.13.7 (2026-11-11)

- **The Upper Realm is your own.** Every world had the same heavens, made by hand: the same sixteen places and the same four sects. The Upper Realm is now drawn once for you, and every journey you play rises into the same one. At its heart a city receives ascenders, beside a spirit vein; around it lie three to five more domains across the whole sky, each with wilds of True Immortal and Golden Immortal beasts and most with a city, a vein, ruins or a secret realm. Four to six immortal sects hold its heights, one or two of them sects of the fallen, and the ninety immortals who founded it are the same in every journey of yours. What becomes of them afterwards is each journey's own. Another player finds other heavens. A journey that had already gone above the clouds keeps the heavens it found.
- **No mortal lives above the clouds.** A child born in the Upper Realm was a mortal infant, and within the year it was sent down to a town in the world below. A child born there is now an immortal from the first breath, at Deity Transformation, and stays above. At sixteen its elders teach it a method and arts, and from then a sect may take it.
- **The map shows the whole heavens.** Above the clouds, "All" frames every domain, however far apart they lie.

## 0.13.6 (2026-11-10)

- **An enlightenment quickens a dao instead of granting it.** Pouring an enlightenment into a dao lifted its insight at once to what the realm above holds, and at the high realms that was 100: a concept made whole for nothing. Now contemplating that dao gains three times the insight for five years of contemplation. It never takes you past what your realm and your open concepts allow, and the contemplation pickers show how long it has left. Pouring another into the same dao adds five more years; pouring one into another dao moves it there. The world's cultivators work the same way.
- **Sending an avatar down never offers a secret realm.** When a child of yours was inside a secret realm, "Send It Down" listed it, then refused it (a realm's barrier admits no projection) and left the avatar where it was. Such places are no longer listed.

## 0.13.5 (2026-11-10)

- **Dismantle in bulk.** "Dismantle all shown..." at the top of the Inventory takes apart, for their materials, every weapon, armour, accessory, tool, disk or puppet the filters and search leave showing, or only the ones you tick. It says first what you will get back, and keeps what is equipped or locked and your natal treasure. Tick boxes now appear on anything that can be dismantled, anywhere, not only in towns and sects.

## 0.13.4 (2026-11-10)

- **A concept made whole brings you out of seclusion.** Seclusion, cultivating to the bottleneck and resting went on after a concept reached 100, and a contemplation kept pouring months into a concept with nothing left to give. Both now stop the month it is made whole, and say which concept and what it now gives. Crafts, the making of arts and journeys still run their full time, and waiting for a secret realm to open carries on past it.

## 0.13.3 (2026-11-10)

- **Opening a concept keeps you among the dao's concepts.** Opening or closing a concept dropped you back to the main screen, so opening several meant going through Contemplate the Dao each time. The dao's five concepts now show again at once, with the change.

## 0.13.2 (2026-11-10)

- **Several weapons, and flying swords.** Ready a weapon beside the one in hand (Inventory) and it goes in your other hand: each art now strikes with the weapon of its own form, so with a sword in hand and a saber in the other, saber arts strike with the saber (its damage, element, dao and poison) and sword arts with the sword. With divine sense awakened you can ready more: they fly about you, one at first and one more for every 120 of your sense's strength (up to six). Each flying weapon strikes on its own every round at a third of a plain blow and draws a twentieth of your battle sense to stay aloft; when the sense runs dry they fall for the rest of the fight. From divine sense 200 an art can be loosed through a flying weapon of its form, for an eighth of your sense more. A chip on the battle screen shows how many fly and what they cost.
- **Heal others.** A healing art and every pill in the battle bag can be aimed at an ally of flesh and blood ("↳ on ..."), and healers fighting beside you tend the worst-hurt among you. Outside battle, a person's sheet offers "Tend their wounds" (your best healing art, three times a battle heal, once a month each) and "Have them take a pill". Ghosts and avatars of qi cannot be tended.
- **Those dear to you.** When kin, a dao companion, a master, a disciple, a lover or a sworn sibling has five years of life or less left, is attacked where you are, or dies anywhere, the chronicle says so and seclusion (or any long undertaking) stops, with what happened on screen. Each of the three can be set to carry on instead (Settings).
- **Your own interruption rules.** A fourth setting, "my own rules": every encounter window gets "Rule for this", always stop me or always answer it one way. Your rules come first, the rest is handled as "only what matters"; a fight you could lose is still put to you.
- **Houses.** Buy a house in a town (400), a city (1,500) or your own sect's or clan's grounds (800), on the Location tab's Home section. Stay in it for closed-door seclusion, or let it: tenants pay about a fiftieth of its price a month from the town's purse, wherever you are. A sale returns three quarters.
- **Declarations to the world.** From Foundation Building, with 50 fame, declare a giveaway, a tournament with a prize (a thing, stones, or the hand of you or one of your unwed kin) or an auction of your own goods. Word spreads through the region for six months; on the day the cultivators who heard come, and stay. Be there: a host who never comes loses face and the stakes go back.
- **Weiqi.** Play Go with anyone beside you (once a month): they warm to you, most of all after a close game. Your sect holds friendly matches once a year (Sect tab, Missions) for fame and a little contribution. A seven-line board with captures, ko and area counting.
- **Four new gu.** Stone-Skin Gu (a shell of stone in battle), Silk-Binding Gu (roots the foe for two rounds), Wind-Wing Gu (carried: up to a fifth faster travel) and Treasure-Seeking Gu (carried: up to a quarter more found when gathering).
- **Breakthroughs are described.** Every realm on every path has a passage of what it looks like from inside, closed by a line for the grade you formed, Flawed to Heavenly.
- **Rename your own arts** (Arts tab, "Rename...").
- **A gathering art of no element** now lifts every material by 30% instead of favouring none. Arts of travel, gathering, concealment, soul warding and each craft never added up, and still do not: the Arts tab now marks the strongest "in use" and the rest "idle".
- **The gift list is grouped** by kind (pills, weapons, materials...), each item with its grade and the realm it serves.
- **Consignment quotes now match the sale.** The auction quote ignored the city's purse, though the sale did not: a drained city's bidders pay less, and never more than it holds. The quote now reads the city as it stands and shows what reaches you after the house's tenth. The list no longer stops at your 60 dearest things.
- **Thorns no longer hurt you more than their beast could.** A Thornback Pangolin or an Ancient Treant turned a fifth of every blow back on whoever struck it, measured by the striker's own strength, so a Divine Body hitting a Qi Condensation pangolin at full force took 80 back per blow and lost limbs. Hunts then stopped fighting themselves (you were too hurt), and a fight fought for you could kill you. A blow turned back (thorned and mirror-scaled beasts, thorned gear, mirror wards, a glacial beast's chill, the Reflection, Thorn and Cause concepts) is now never more than its bearer could deal you with its own blow: an equal still takes the whole share, and a beast far beneath you barely scratches you.
- **Lock the repeated action.** A button under Repeat keeps it on one action, whatever else you do.
- **Ancients after their avatars.** Tracing an ancient whose standing avatars you already know now leads past them to where their presence dwells, with how many bodies have fallen and when the first reforms; the wisp of a slain ancient names who slew them.
- **A bred strain is a herb of its grade.** A cross that raised a strain's grade kept its parents' pace and potency, so a Yellow Qi Gathering Grass bred up to Heaven grade still grew in a year or two and was no stronger than the grass it came from, while wild Heaven-grade herbs take 80 years and more. Now a strain never grows faster than the quickest wild herb of its grade (Mystic 6 years, Earth 25, Heaven 80, Immortal 300), and its aspects are raised to at least the average wild herb of its grade. Strains you already bred follow the new rule, including those in your fields: they need longer to ripen, and come out stronger.

## 0.13.1 (2026-11-10)

- **A Heavenly realm now asks a perfected main dao.** From Foundation Building on, every concept your realm can open must be whole (100) in your main dao: one at Foundation Building, two at Golden Core, three at Nascent Soul, four at Deity Transformation, all five for an immortal. Your main dao is the one your path rests on: on the Qi path your method's element (a Fire method, Fire; with no method, or one of no element, Heavenly stays closed), the Body dao on the Body path, Yin on the Soul path. Short of that, the finest realm you can form is a 79% Perfect one, however well you prepared. The breakthrough panel and the Character tab say when Heavenly is closed to you and why. The world's cultivators are held to the same rule. The breakthrough into Foundation Building asks nothing, as no concept opens before it.

## 0.13.0 (2026-11-10)

- **Every dao is now five concepts, and you choose which to master.** Fire is Burning, Igniting, Consuming, Extinguishing and Heat; the Sword is Blade, Intent, Precision, Sheath and Will; every one of the twenty daos has five (the Guide lists all hundred). Each concept has insight of its own and a power of its own: Burning makes the burns you set hit harder and last longer, Igniting makes your first Fire blow of a battle land 40% harder, Consuming burns the foe's qi into yours, Extinguishing dulls burns on you, Heat holds the old +10% quality of pills and forged items. A dao's insight is the average of its five, and that is what strengthens its arts and crafts, unfolds its domain and fuses it with others.
- **Your realm opens concepts.** None before Foundation Building, then one of each dao, two at Golden Core, three at Nascent Soul, four at Deity Transformation, all five for an immortal. An open concept can be made whole; the others grow freely only to 15, then a third the pace, halving every 5 further (the old realm limit worked the same way). Open and close them in the contemplation menu, where you also choose the concept to contemplate. Closing one frees its place, and its insight falls back to 15. The world's cultivators open theirs as they grow.
- **A dao takes longer to master.** Each point of insight now goes to one concept, a fifth of the dao, so a whole dao is about five times the work it was. Fighting, crafting, travel, pills, tribulations and deeds now deepen the concepts they fit (a burn you set deepens Burning, a forging Refinement and Heat, a long road Mist, Roaming and Folding); every concept a battle put to use deepens a little when it ends. Gifts of insight from masters, inheritances and past lives still raise the whole dao.
- **A dao is perfected only with all five concepts whole, and Golden Immortal asks one.** The perfection powers need every concept at 100. No breakthrough to Golden Immortal without a perfected dao.
- **Your old insight is kept.** On load, each dao's five concepts all start at the insight the dao had, none of them open, so every number stays as it was until you choose.

- **A reforging is truly a forging: do it badly and the treasure comes out worse.** In 0.12.53 a reforged natal treasure kept whichever temper was better, so there was nothing to lose. Now it comes out as this forging makes it. Forge it better than it was forged and it takes the finer temper, its effects growing with it; forge it worse and its quality and its effects fall; a forging that fails outright leaves it crude. It is never destroyed, and your bond, its grade and its refinements stay. A masterpiece still comes by the same luck as any other craft, for a master who meets all a masterpiece asks.
- **Every form with a dao of its own asks its element's dao too.** The two-dao rule for weapon arts now holds for all such forms: fist, palm, finger and body arts are the Dao of the Body (a frost palm needs the Body and Ice), soul arts and soul banner arts are Yin, mending is Yang, rift arts Space, venom arts Poison, sound arts Sound, each together with the dao of the element the art carries. Creating such an art, or tempering a move of it, needs the insight its grade asks in both, and it comes out as fine as the shallower allows. With no element it needs its form's dao alone. A finger art of an element used to draw on the element's dao only; it is now the Body's and the element's. Flame, wave, thunder, gale, frost, vine and quake arts, and fan arts, stay their element's dao and nothing else. Arts you already know lose nothing: using or learning one takes the deeper of its two daos.
- **Every weapon answers to the dao that suits it.** At 100 insight a weapon's dao adds 30% weapon damage with it: the Sword, Saber, Spear and Bow for their own weapons, the **Body for gloves**, **Sound for a flute, guqin or zither**, **Poison for hidden weapons** (and hidden weapon arts are made from Poison now), **Yin for a soul banner**, and for a **war fan the dao of the element it is attuned to** (the Wind's, with none). The daos are strictly apart: the Dao of the Sword strengthens swords and nothing else, and no weapon borrows another's dao. (Gloves, fans, hidden weapons and instruments used to be the Dao of the Sword's, and in 0.12.53 a saber, spear or bow still drew on it at half strength: both are gone.
