A character who fights with another weapon on the strength of their Sword Dao gets nothing from it until they contemplate that weapon's own dao.) Each dao's line on the Character tab names the weapons it strengthens.
- **Each great weapon has a fused dao, and the Void Sword is the sword's alone.** The Void Sword (Sword + Space) added its 30% weapon damage with every weapon; it now adds it with a sword only. The other three have fused daos of their own, each adding 30% weapon damage with its weapon and a second gift: the **Mountain-Splitting Saber** (Saber + Earth: +10% toughness), the **Thunder Spear** (Spear + Lightning: +15% toughness against tribulation bolts) and the **Sky-Piercing Bow** (Bow + Wind: +25% travel speed). Like every fused dao, each is fused from Nascent Soul with both daos at 60, is as strong as the weaker of its parts, unfolds a domain that does the work of both parts at once, and perfected (both at 100) calls both parts' powers in one breath: The Mountain Falls, Heaven's Lance, The Arrow That Left No Trace. The world's cultivators fuse them too.

## 0.12.53 (2026-11-10)

- **A founder's blood can be given to any dao companion.** Granting your bloodline counted only the first dao companion you took, so a second one (or one whose bond was recorded on her side) was refused with "Only your descendants, dao companion, disciples...". Every dao companion now counts, and so does a disciple of yours who also has another master. The same slip is fixed wherever a dao companion is asked after: who shares your arrays, who may live in your inner world, who you may call into danger without winning their trust first, a soul's welcome into a companion's body, and the "your dao companion" label on a sheet.
- **The saber, the spear and the bow have daos of their own.** They are contemplated like any other dao, each with its own domain and perfection, and each adds 30% weapon damage with its weapon at 100 insight:
  - **Saber:** its domain cuts the foe before you every round for half a plain blow and leaves them bleeding; perfected, Heaven-Cleaving Stroke makes your next blow strike three and a half times as hard (half your qi).
  - **Spear:** its domain pierces every foe every round for about a third of a plain blow; perfected, One Point Through Ten Thousand triples your next blow (40% of your qi).
  - **Bow:** its domain strikes every foe for a quarter of a plain blow and spends their evasive footwork; perfected, Sun-Shooting Arrow triples your next blow (40% of your qi).
  - The Dao of the Sword stays the dao of swords and of every weapon with no dao of its own (gloves, fans, hidden weapons, instruments, soul banners). With a saber, spear or bow it still counts at half strength (the deeper of the two daos is used), so a swordsman who picks up a spear is not left with nothing.
  - This replaces 0.12.52's "give your sword dao to one weapon". A save where you gave it to the saber, spear or bow has that insight moved to the weapon's own dao on load; given to any other weapon, it is simply the Dao of the Sword again.
- **A weapon art of an element asks two daos.** A fire saber art is the Dao of the Saber and the Dao of Fire: creating it, or tempering a move of it, needs the insight its grade asks in both, and it comes out as fine as the shallower of the two allows. The Arts tab shows both. A weapon art of no element needs its weapon's dao alone. Arts you already know lose nothing: using and learning such an art takes the deeper of its two daos. The world's cultivators follow the same rule, and learn their weapon's dao a little each year as they contemplate.
- **Reforging is a forging.** Reforging your natal treasure (Inventory → Reforge...) now plays the craft again, as when it was first made (the minigame, or a quick forging), on three ores, jades or cores of the grade above it. If this forging comes out finer than the treasure is, it takes the finer temper and its effects grow with it (a superior treasure made flawless); if not, it keeps what it had. A master who meets all a masterpiece asks may see it come out a masterpiece by the same luck as any other craft, but that is no longer required to reforge. It needs a bond of 30 (not 60), and takes the craft's own months and two more.
- **More is settled for you when you are sure of it.** While you are busy (in every interruption setting but "everything stops me", and always with an avatar at your door):
  - A challenge you would win in a moment is accepted and fought instead of declined at a cost to your name: a rival's challenge, a challenger after your fame, a bout before a crowd, your next round in a tournament, a foe your sect sends you after, an avenger of a past life. "Sure" is the same line as any fight fought for you: you are hale and they stand a realm below you.
  - A heart demon you would face down nine times in ten is confronted, not buried at a cost to your foundation. Its choice now shows the chance.
  - A bursting furnace you would steady nine times in ten (alchemy 65) is steadied.
  - The Guide's "An avatar at your door" lists everything settled this way.
- **A beast that lost its head in beast shape has lost its face in human shape too.** Wounds carried from one shape to the other skipped the parts only the new shape has, and a save fixed them on load, so such a world came back from a save slightly different.

## 0.12.52 (2026-11-10)

- **The game no longer freezes on seeds of a strain bred from bred strains.** Planting, crossing or even opening the bag with such seeds (a strain five or six crosses deep) hung the game, and it hung again on every load. The game rebuilt a strain from its parents' names by trying every way the name could be split, which took minutes and over a gigabyte for a long name. It now reads a name in one pass. The Professions tab, which took two seconds to open with such strains known, opens at once. Their seeds also no longer read "Qi (bred) (bred) Seeds".
- **Name the strains you breed.** Professions → Herbalism → Name a strain you bred: the herb, its seeds and whatever is worked from it carry your name everywhere.
- **Two recipes never share a name.** A new design whose name is already taken gets a mark in front of it (Greater, Lesser, Refined, Ancient, Twin, Hidden, Later), as the beasts of a pack are told apart.
- **You can give anything to anyone, and stock anything in your shop.** The gift list and the shop's stocking list showed only the twenty (or forty) dearest things you carried, so a Nascent Soul master with a bag of immortal treasures could no longer give a disciple a Mystic pill, and three hundred cheap formation disks never appeared for the shop. Both lists now show everything, and so does a shop's "Sell to this shop". Gifts and stock ask how many (1, 5, 10, 25, 100, half, all, or a number you type), and the shop lists things of one name together, so disks crafted one by one go on the shelves in one go.
- **A demonic move keeps the effect you chose.** Every demonic move you gave form to came out Devouring, whatever you picked (soul attacks too), and an art bound of your moves had its first move turned to Devouring. Your moves now stay as you tempered them; Devouring is one of the effects a demonic move may take.
- **Your nature works on you.** Each trait now has a way of cultivating or living that suits it:
  - Lazy or Greedy: pills and absorbed materials give 20% more cultivation each (40% with both); for the lazy each one also steadies the dao heart a little. The greedy sell 10% dearer from their own shop.
  - Lustful: dual cultivation gives 40% more and steadies the heart. Treacherous and Lustful together: a Cauldron Harvest gives 50% more and steadies the heart greatly. A dao companion bound by force, or two realms below you, cannot refuse to be harvested again (bind one that way with "Bind them by force" on their Interact sheet, as before).
  - Cautious: +15% cultivation at your own abode behind a protective or concealing array. Diligent: +15% cultivation, but everything that steadies the dao heart steadies it half as much. Reclusive: +10% in seclusion. Ambitious: +8% while your sect or clan has a seat above yours. Humble: everything that steadies the heart steadies it half again as much.
  - Compassionate, Honorable, Arrogant, Ruthless, Treacherous, Vengeful: deeds true to the nature (sparing, taking up a challenge, finishing a foe, robbing, killing a sworn enemy) steady the dao heart.
  - **Some natures rebel.** The greedy against paying robbers or giving stones or medicine away to help someone; the arrogant against fleeing, refusing a challenge or paying robbers; the compassionate against robbing the helpless, walking past someone in need, or killing a beaten foe who had not come for you; the honorable against robbing; the vengeful against sparing a sworn enemy. Do it anyway and your dao heart wavers and you cultivate 20% slower for six months.
  - **You are always warned first.** A choice in an encounter, and each button over a beaten foe, says in red when it goes against your nature and in green when it is true to it. What is settled for you while you are busy is never held against your nature. The Character tab lists each trait's gift and price, and the Guide's Natures topic has them all.
- **Vices are at home in a demonic scripture.** While you practise a demonic method, the traits that trouble the dao heart steady it a little instead, each quickens that method (+5% cultivation each, up to +15%), and your demonic arts ripen 10% faster in practice for each.
- **Twelve new encounters.** A young master outbids you at a city auction out of spite (let it go, bid him into ruin, or meet him outside). A shabby stranger sells a "supreme manual": divine sense or Causality 30 reads him first, and one in four is real. A clan arrives with a marriage contract your dead parents signed (honour it, buy your way out, or swear a duel in three years, which then comes due). A falling star leaves star-metal for the first there and a fight for the next. A pill furnace is about to burst in the market (your alchemy steadies it; or run; or empty the stall). A wounded cub of a beast far above you follows you, with its mother and its hunters behind it. A foe you killed leaves a letter for their family, who may thank you or blame you. An inheritance cave turns out to be someone's living seclusion. A village offers everything it has for an afternoon's work (karma, sometimes a follower, now and then a child with a heavenly root). A stranger's tribulation breaks where you stand (flee, watch for insight at a risk, or strike them for heavy karma). Someone recognises your soul from your last life and comes to collect a favour or a grudge. And a sword in a shop insists it chose you: it has a spirit already awake, likes a hard fight, and sulks when you cut down the helpless.
- **Several masters.** You can bow to up to three masters (Relations → Ask to become their disciple too). As with dao companions, those you already have take it as a slight (-30 regard, and sore for as long as they share you), a new master asks more regard of someone who already has one, and the arrogant may cast you out. What you ask of a master goes to the one you attend; "Attend this master" changes that and slights no one.
- **A master and their spirit beast are fought together.** Attack someone and every beast of theirs that is there joins the fight; attack the beast and its master joins it. They used to be fought one after the other.
- **When a pack's leader flees, you keep what fell.** Killing two of three beasts and seeing the last one run gave nothing at all. Whoever holds the field now skins the beasts and strips the dead that the fleeing leader left behind.
- **A body with no legs barely flees.** Fleeing (and a beaten side breaking and running) is now cut by what is left of the legs, wings or coils, down to a tenth with none. One who can fly needs only a whole spine.
- **Battle talent can be earned.** Every victory over a foe of your own realm or above counts once (no spars), and one won while holding back below the foe's realm the whole fight counts three times: at 60 you are Gifted, at 200 a Prodigy, at 500 Heaven-Defying. The Character tab shows how far you are.
- **Reforge your natal treasure into a masterpiece.** Inventory, on your natal treasure: once your bond with it reaches 60 and you meet all a masterpiece of the forge asks (forging 100, an Immortal-grade forging scripture, your own abode's forge fully built), six months and three ores, jades or cores of the grade above it remake it flawless and half again as strong, with a name of its own and your bond whole.
- **Dismantle for materials.** Inventory, on anything made to a design (weapons, armour, accessories, tools, formation disks, puppets): it is destroyed and you keep 30% to 70% of each material that went in (more the higher your skill in its craft, and always at least one of its main material). You choose how many of a stack. "Dismantle to study" is the old button that destroys an item to learn its design faster.
- **The Sword Dao can be given to your weapon.** Character tab → Dao → Give your sword dao to one weapon: it becomes the Dao of the Spear, the Saber, the Bow, the Fist and so on. Given to no weapon it adds 30% weapon damage with all of them at 100 insight, as before; given to one, 45% with that weapon and 15% with any other. The first choice is free; changing it later costs a fifth of the insight.
- **Cast off a bloodline or a physique.** Character tab: burn out your bloodline (six months, from Foundation Building; your meridians are torn as it leaves) or wear your physique down to an ordinary body (a year; your foundation is shaken). Everything it gave goes with it, and another blood or body can be taken afterwards. A founder cannot cast off their own line, nor a beast its blood.
- **A ghost walks the Soul path alone.** A ghost could still take up Body Tempering with no flesh to temper. Until it has a body again, only the Soul path is open to it.
- **An avatar can keep your door.** A new avatar task (the avatars menu → Keep your door): it goes where you go, and while you cultivate, contemplate, craft, gather or travel it answers whatever comes in your stead, the cautious way, whatever your interruption setting. A fight you could lose still reaches you.
- **What is settled for you follows your strength.** A spirit herb on a cliff is now plucked with qi from Foundation Building, or climbed for by one nimble enough never to fall (agility 100), instead of being left; a wounded stranger's wounds are bound when it costs no pill.
- **Calamities are marked on the map.** A red warning triangle stands at each calamity's heart, with smaller ones on land a withering has reached. The map's side panel lists every calamity under way with Show and Travel, and a selected place says what has befallen it.
- **Choosing a physique or a bloodline at birth fits the screen.** In point buy the two dropdowns ran off the edge of the screen (thirty bloodlines, each with every effect on one line), and on a phone the list covered the page. Each is now a button that opens a list you can search, with what each one gives written under its name, cheapest first.
- **Long design lists fold into categories, and ingredients filter by grade.** With more than eight designs in a craft, the Professions tab groups them under headings you can fold (pills by what they do, weapons by form, armour by where it is worn); searching lists everything flat. Designs and the inventor's ingredient list each have a grade filter.

## 0.12.51 (2026-11-09)

- **Grand formations.** From Formations skill 20 you can bind ordinary formation disks into one formation over several places joined by road (Location tab → Raise a grand formation). It takes one disk for each place and one more for the **array eye**, all of one kind, and the weakest disk sets the grade. The eye is set where you stand: it is where the formation is fed (the land, spirit stones, or souls), and where it can be broken. Your skill binds one place and one more for every 20 skill (more with the Dao of Space), and raising it takes a month for each place. Covering another sect's or clan's land is an act of war. The map draws a gold circle round every covered place, and a second one at an eye you can find.
  - **Spirit-Vein Siphon** (qi-gathering disks). It raises the qi of its places, up to double, by drinking the land within two steps around them, down to 40% of what it was. It moves qi and never makes it: what its places gain is exactly what the land around loses. It takes about five years to reach full strength. When it falls, the qi it gathered scatters at once, and the land it drained takes ten to fifteen years to recover (the map marks drained land with a pale ring). Herbs grow worse on drained land, draining mortal towns stains your karma every year, and a sect whose land you drink finds out in time and sends its strongest against the eye.
  - **Great Guarding Formation** (protection disks). A barrier: raids on your abode, robbers, beasts and hunters on the road, beast tides and curses aimed at you and yours there must break it first. Anyone more than one realm above its grade tears straight through, and a blow that empties it leaves it down for six months.
  - **Heaven-Veiling Formation** (concealment disks). What you and yours do on its places stays hidden: demonic cultivation earns no infamy there, killings are not reckoned (no bounty, no kin's grudge; heaven still counts the karma), and readings of you are veiled. At the fourth grade and above its places vanish from strangers' maps. It can still let a deed out: a life lamp shows the killer anyway, a stronger reader sees through, and each year there is a small chance someone talks.
  - **Myriad-Sword Domain** (killing disks). You and yours fight stronger on its places (6% more attack a grade, up to 30%; the battle advice and the damage you see already count it), and your enemies who linger there bleed every month.
  - **Lost-Mist Maze** (escape disks). Hunters sent after you lose your trail while you stay inside, robbers find you half as often there, and a stranger loses a month crossing each of its places.
  - **Breaking one.** Find the eye (Formations skill, or a clear Seek a person reading of its owner), then assault it a month at a time (a standing barrier takes the blows first; the broken eye leaves half its disks), or unravel it quietly through the formation game, which leaves no grudge if you do it well. A formation falls with its owner.
  - **The world raises them too.** Formation sects raise a Great Guarding Formation over their mountain and the land beside it, demonic sects a Heaven-Veiling Formation over their home, and the greedy a siphon (the righteous only where it drinks wild land).
- **Divination is now Causality.** The craft keeps its skill, schools and readings; only its name changes, everywhere it is shown (a Diviner is a Causality Master; divination coins are hexagram coins). It now has three branches on the Professions tab: Reading (the thirteen readings as before), Weaving, and Severing and Preparing. Every rite is cast through the hexagram like a reading, takes months and incense, and can be resisted, felt and traced.
  - **Karmic mediums.** A harmful rite needs a token of its victim. A clear Read a fate gives their name and birth hour (enough for the lighter curses); beating them in a fight and sparing or robbing them gives a belonging, or blood if they were wounded (the Seven Nails need blood). Your feeling for them, love or hate, tightens the thread.
  - **The dark rites need both the Dao of Yin and the Dao of Karma** (40 insight each for curses, 55 for the Calamity Star and Passing the Calamity, 70 for the Seven Nails, 60 for the effigy, 85 for rebirth), and cost karma, more for each realm the victim stands below you. A rite that finds no hold lands on you instead.
  - **Weaving.** Ill Star (their luck falls by 5 to 20 for one to five years), Withering Fate (15% to 40% slower cultivation and worse breakthroughs for years), Calamity Star (beasts and robbers find them, and a heavier tribulation), Borrowed Fortune (their luck becomes yours for three years), Bestowed Fortune (yours becomes a disciple's or child's; no dao needed), Heaven's Deception (a veil over your own fate: readings of you are muddier and curses slip), and Unbinding (breaks a curse on you or someone beside you, and done clearly shows who cast it).
  - **Seven Nails of Severance.** Over two months the victim withers, and then dies of "a sudden qi deviation" with no killer named: no bounty and no grudge, only very heavy karma and years of your life. Each month they may throw the nails off, or feel them and have them unbound, and then the rite turns on you. A Trace a death by a reader more skilled than you still finds the thread and names you; cast under your own Heaven-Veiling Formation it takes 10 more skill for each grade. No one two realms above you can be severed.
  - **Life Lamp, and Passing the Calamity.** A lamp lit at your abode or your sect's home for you or one of yours goes out when they die, and shows their kin (and you) who did it, even under a veil. Sect elders and clan heads of Golden Core and above keep one, so killing them quietly is harder. Passing the Calamity moves a tribulation you have read onto someone else: you bear yours 15% to 30% better, and heaven's lightning finds them.
  - **Two ways back from death, each once in a life.** The **Substitute Effigy** (Golden Core; a spirit wood or jade of tier 5, your heart's blood, a soul of your realm from a soul banner, six months at your abode without leaving, 30 years of life) is a doll that dies in your place: the same body rises. It does not answer old age, a tribulation or a blow that shatters the soul; it crumbles after 20 years, and raiders who break into the abode break it. **Borrowed Rebirth** (Nascent Soul; three years beside a vessel, 100 years of life) plants a karmic seed in a puppet body of grade 6 or a child of your blood under 12 (the demonic way: -60 karma and their kin's undying hatred). When you die, you wake in the vessel one major realm lower, with your memories, arts and insight. Never across the clouds, and the seed withers if you do not visit it every ten years.
  - **The world uses it on you.** Causality masters curse those they hate, you among them: you learn of it by feeling it (Causality skill or divine sense), or from a reading of the road ahead, and what is woven on you is listed on the Character tab. Rare demonic elders keep a rebirth seed: a clear reading of their fate shows where the vessel waits, and you can break it there before you kill them.
- **A richer world.** Players kept running short of spirit stones, and towns and shops were too poor to pay for what was brought to them. There are now half again as many spirit stones in every world (six times what its people hold when history begins, up from four), and merchants hold a larger share of them (a tenth of the world's supply at ease, up from 7%). Measured over 40 years on a fresh world: the usual town's purse is nearly three times as deep (about 2,700 stones, from about 950), towns with an empty purse fell from three or four in eighteen to one at most, the usual cultivator holds about 180 stones instead of 130, and those with none at all fell from about a hundred to under forty. So goods sell nearer their price in more places, wages and commissions are paid in full more often, and the people you rob, trade with or inherit from carry more. **Worlds already begun** get the same on their next load: the new stones lie in the earth, each town's merchants are brought up to at least half their ease at once, and the rest comes up year by year through the veins and the towns. Stones are still conserved: nothing is minted when you sell.
- **The elements react in battle.** An element's touch lingers on a foe: water leaves them soaked, ice frostbitten, fire burning, wood rooted. An art of the right element landing on them next now sets off a reaction, every time, with no luck in it. Plain blows and arts of no element set nothing off, and nothing reacts in a spar.
  - **Conduction** (lightning on the soaked): stunned for a round, a shock worth a quarter of the blow, and the lightning arcs to every other soaked foe for 40% of the blow, stunning them too.
  - **Flash Freeze** (ice on the soaked): stunned for a round, then slowed to 0.6 of their speed for 3 rounds.
  - **Wildfire** (wind on the burning): the flames bite at once and burn 2 rounds longer.
  - **Steam** (fire on the soaked, or water on the burning): a scald worth a quarter of the blow, and you are harder to hit for a round behind the cloud. The soaking or the fire ends.
  - **Kindling** (fire on the rooted): the bind burns away and they burn for 3 rounds. **Overgrowth** (wood on the soaked): bound for 4 rounds. **Mire** (earth on the soaked): bound for 2 rounds and slower still.
  - **Shatter** (metal or earth on the frostbitten): every blow lands 20% harder on them for 2 rounds.
  - **Grounding.** Behind a raised Earth barrier, lightning neither stuns nor conducts.
  - Your foes look for the same openings on you. The battle screen names the state ("Soaked" and "Frostbitten" in place of a plain "Slowed") and its tooltip lists what answers it; an art's button names the reaction it would set off now, and the battle advice counts it. Stuns from reactions still cannot land in the rounds after one was shaken off.
- **Changing your cultivation method costs a minor stage.** Switching methods was free and instant, so there was no reason not to hop to whatever was best that month. The qi one method gathered does not run by another's circulation: leaving a method for a different one now costs a whole minor stage of that path (you keep the share gathered toward the next). A major realm is never lost: at a realm's first stage you lose the progress gathered toward the next stage instead. The game tells you exactly what you will lose and asks before it happens. A path's first method costs nothing, and refining the method you practise is not a change. Forgetting your method and then taking up another costs the same. Others in the world pay it too, and now change only for a clearly better method.
- **A place's qi is shown as you would feel it.** The map and the Sect tab's holdings showed the land's own qi even where arrays or a siphon had changed it. They now show what you would cultivate in.

## 0.12.50 (2026-11-09)

- **Setting a method trait by will keeps the slot's strength.** In the method forge, giving a slot the trait you choose (or another copy of one) used to roll its strength again, so a superb slot could come out weak. Now the slot keeps the strength it had, and only the trait changes. The picker shows each trait at that strength.
- **A bloodline of your own is built around what you choose, and can be reforged.** Founding a line used to take your two strongest attributes and your element, with no say. Now, as with forging a physique, you choose its focus: Might, Endurance, Swiftness, Insight, Fortune, or as you are. You also choose its element (your own, another of your root's, or none), and you can found it by instinct instead of playing the rewriting. A founder can **reforge** the line later (two years, once per realm) around another focus or element. The rewriting sets its strength again, so a rough hand can leave it weaker. The beast blood folded into it and the line it was founded over stay, and every carrier's blood changes with it. The Character tab shows what would change before you begin.
- **Beasts grow wise at Nascent Soul, even if they never take human form.** A beast you play that stays a beast can now create guards, footwork, healing, concealment and soul wards, and attack arts of its element's qi as well as its body's (still no methods or crafts). Beasts of the world that reach Nascent Soul devise such arts of their own and fight with them: they raise guards, step aside, mend wounds and strike with their arts where those beat their claws. Beast kings now cultivate year by year, below the clouds as far as Nascent Soul.
- **Tribes of beasts.** A wise beast of the wilds gathers its kind into a tribe that holds its lair. More of its kind come each year (up to four more than its realm's number), those of Foundation Building and above learn the arts the chief devises, and they fight beside their chief at the lair. When a chief falls, the strongest member leads; with none strong enough, the tribe scatters. A beast king's challenge shows its tribe, and a tribe's chief who is no king can be challenged at its lair. A beast you play can **found a tribe** at Nascent Soul from the Location tab: your pack become its first members, your kind keep coming, and they learn the arts you create.
- **Every guard and every step now does something only it does.** Most defence and movement moves were the same two things, "blows land softer" or "blows land less often", at different lengths and strengths. Counter Stance even beat the Guarding Aura outright, Phantom Steps was an Afterimage with a Blur added, and Fleeing Light did everything Step Away did. Now each move has a job of its own:
  - **Guards.** The **Guarding Aura** stays the guard that lasts. A **Counter Stance** is a light guard with a hard answer: 120% of a plain strike, up from 80%. A **Mirror Ward** is for blows from afar: they land softer and 40% of each turns back on its caster (it no longer counters close blows). A **Flowing Deflection** may turn a close blow aside entirely, leaving the striker off balance, instead of being a weak guard. An **Iron Bastion** is the firmest guard there is, three times the aura's, but only for the round you raise it, and with no footwork while it stands. An **Unshaken Stance** shakes off a stumble or dread, and for three rounds no stun, stumble or dread takes hold; its guard is only a token now.
  - **Steps.** **Blur** stays the step that makes you harder to hit. **Step In** closes the distance and lets you act first next round whatever your speed. **Step Away** opens the distance, and the foe cannot close it again next round. **Flicker Step** slips every bind, slow and pursuit at once, even while bound, and none takes hold for two rounds. **Circling Step** takes you to the foe's flank, so your next blow passes half their raised guard by. **Phantom Steps** leaves a phantom for every foe: each one's first blow strikes empty air. **Hunter's Stride** still makes your next two blows truer, and now the foe cannot step back or flee for two rounds.
  - **Moves that answer each other.** A hunter's stride breaks a step away and stops a fleeing light. A flicker step slips the hunter. A feint draws out a deflection or a phantom. A circling step gets past the guards.
  - **The rest of the game knows them too.** The world's fighters choose each move for its moment: an Iron Bastion against a gathered blow, an Unshaken Stance when they stumble, a Mirror Ward against an archer or a caster, Phantom Steps against a crowd, a Hunter's Stride on a faltering foe, a Flicker Step when bound. The battle advice suggests a Flicker Step when you are bound or hunted, and favours close arts against a mirror ward. The new states have chips on the battle screen. The Guide and Glossary describe the moves as they now work. Arts keep the moves they had; only what the moves do has changed.

## 0.12.49 (2026-11-09)

- **Losing a fight in an avatar no longer ends your run.** When an avatar your soul rode below the clouds was beaten, your soul did snap back to your main body, but the winner then "executed the loser", which by then was your real body, and the game ended on an Ascension screen. Now the avatar is lost and your soul is wounded, as the Avatars topic says, and nothing reaches your main body. The death screen also stopped calling it "Ascension" when someone who once ascended is later killed above the clouds. Such a death now offers the avatars to live on in, a rebirth and a lingering ghost, like any other.
- **An art's moves come out different.** Creating an art by instinct drew each move's effect at random, so moves often repeated (an Ice soul art could hold four Devouring Soul Spikes). Each move now takes a different effect: a demonic art's Devouring first, then the one you chose, then the rest its form and element allow. "By instinct" on a single move now asks which effect (or guard, or step) to release it as. Before, every quick move of one form and element came out the same.
- **A binding talisman can no longer bind you to death.** Its runes stacked and ignored the moment of freedom every other stun leaves, so a foe with a stack of them could keep you bound round after round. Now a bind lasts at most 3 rounds and never adds to one already holding. Against someone just freed, the runes seize their meridians instead (no arts for 2 rounds). The world's fighters no longer throw one at a foe already bound.
- **Avatars can fight beside you.** A new avatar task, "Fight beside you": it walks where you go and joins every fight (not spars) on your side, a realm below you, with your arts, daos and methods but bare hands. If it is struck dead, or left down when your side loses, it is gone. The backlash hits at once, in the fight: your sea of consciousness takes 20 damage, your meridians 12, and you lose a quarter of your battle qi.
- **Focused methods.** In the method forge you can now set a slot by will: 3 rerolls for any trait, or 2 for another copy of a trait the method already holds. Each copy past the first adds half its strength. Copies across all the methods you practise open more for the traits of a way of playing. Myriad Selves: 2 copies, one more avatar; 3, cultivating avatars send back twice as much; 4, an avatar fighting beside you stands at your own realm. Yin-Yang Harmony: 2, your partner shares it in full; 3, dual cultivation takes half a year for a year's gain; 4, +10% breakthrough chance while your dao companion is beside you. The Arts tab shows what your copies have opened.
- **Demonic methods hunger.** Two traits only a demonic method can hold. Endless Hunger: devouring moves heal you and steal more qi (with 2 copies, even through death qi; with 3, every devouring blow also staunches your bleeding). Blood Feast: more essence from each kill. Every demonic method now carries one of them.

## 0.12.48 (2026-11-09)

- **Enlightenment.** Now and then, unbidden, heaven and earth fall into place in your mind: about once in forty years, a little more often with high luck and comprehension (about twice as often with both at 150). A long seclusion stops for it, and a ✶ Enlightenment button appears at the top of the Cultivation panel. It waits until you pour it into one thing. Seclusion gives three minor stages of cultivation at once (never past the bottleneck). A dao's insight rises at once to what the realm above holds (a Foundation Building cultivator's to 50, Major Attainment). A craft's skill rises at once to what the realm above holds. A grandmaster at skill 100 gets a masterpiece instead: the next work of that craft that succeeds is one, without the workshop, the Immortal scripture or the excellent score a masterpiece usually needs. You hold one at a time, and none comes while you hold one. The world's cultivators have them too, and spend them at once. The old "A Moment of Enlightenment" encounter, which gives a little insight into one of three daos, is now called "A Moment of Clarity" so the two aren't confused.
- **Soul moves can each do something different.** A soul move could only be the soul lash, dread or plain, and in battle every one of them struck the same way whatever its effect, so a soul art of four moves was four copies of one. Every soul move still strikes the sea of consciousness, and now its effect decides what else it does: the pure lash at full force, terror (75% force; the foe hesitates and loses momentum), a mind lock (75%; may seal the foe's meridians for 2 rounds), a siphon (80%; draws their qi into you), or a shock (80%; a good chance to stun). Infusion strengthens each. The creator shows each in numbers. Soul arts you already made keep their moves; temper new ones to use these.
- **An inheritance you leave for your next life waits for it.** A wandering cultivator could find and claim any open inheritance after ten years, taking its guardian beast with it, so one you sealed for a later life was often gone by the time you got back to it; and a new life in the same world didn't even know where it lay. When you seal an inheritance there is now a "Keep it for my next life" box (ticked by default). No wanderer ever claims one kept that way, and every later life of yours in this world (a new life, a reincarnation, an heir) starts out knowing where it is and can open it, your blood or not. Inheritances you sealed in older saves that are still unclaimed are kept this way too; the legacies left at your tombs stay open to anyone worthy. One sealed above the clouds still waits there, out of reach of a life below until it ascends.
- **Arrays can be powered by souls.** Besides the land's qi and spirit stones, an array can now burn souls (the demonic way: 20 insight into Yin, a demonic art, or a demonic sect). Feed it a soul from your soul banner (a cultivator's or a beast's), the remnant soul in a beast core, or a tamed beast of yours sacrificed whole. Souls burn hotter than stones, at 135% of the array's strength, and take nothing from the land's qi. A cultivator's soul lasts six months for each realm it reached (a beast's by its weight), a core three months per grade, divided by the array's grade. Every soul stains your karma, and an array with no soul left lies dormant until you feed it another.
- **You can become a calamity.** From Golden Core, Location tab → News → Become a calamity: be the heart of one yourself, its tier set by your realm (Golden Core a minor calamity, Deity Transformation a middle one, its peak a greater). A harvest of cultivators needs the demonic way and an infamous name (100 infamy per tier): each year you take the cultivators of your region, and their qi flows into your cultivation. A demonic coalition needs you to lead a demonic sect; a righteous crusade, to lead a righteous one with karma +20. Either way the sects of your side band under yours and make war on the rest. The world's heroes come for you as for any calamity's heart: face them, or give it up. You can let it go whenever you like.
- **Soul and sound arts reach into minds.** With a soul or sound art mastered to 25%, the Interact menu can now sway someone's mind (their regard for you rises) or command it: follow you, enthralled; hand over a third of their spirit stones; leave this place; or forget a grudge against you. Your hold is your soul's power, your mastery and your insight into the art's dao (Yin for soul arts, Sound for sound arts); their will is their soul, any soul ward and a steady dao heart, and the upright resist half again as hard. It's the demonic way and stains karma. A failed attempt is felt and they loathe you for it, and you can try each person once a year. An enthralled follower may wake each year (likelier the weaker your hold) and swear vengeance.
- **Searching for hidden treasure really is greyed out in towns and a faction's halls.** In 0.12.47 it stayed open wherever a weapon had once been dropped in a fight, which over centuries of history was most sect gates and clan estates. Now it's open there only to dig up a cache you have already found.
- **Arts can mesh moves of different forms.** Attack moves no longer have to share a form to be bound into one art, only a kind and an element, so long as the moves need one weapon at most between them. A sword art can now hold a gale, a palm strike and a soul spike, but not a spear thrust. Each move strikes as its own form (a soul spike in a sword art still strikes the soul, a gale needs no blade) and costs a tenth more qi than the art's own form. You can also add such a move to an art you already bound.

## 0.12.47 (2026-11-09)

- **Calamities.** Every so often a calamity rises in the world below: a minor one about every 25 years (it threatens Qi Condensation and Foundation Building, and its heart stands at low Golden Core, strong for its realm), a middle one about every century (Golden Core and Nascent Soul; its heart at Deity Transformation), a greater one about every two centuries (Deity Transformation; its heart at the very peak of the world below, and stronger still). Each world draws four of eight kinds when it is made: a beast king rising, a demonic prodigy harvesting cultivators, an old monster mad with grief, an invasion through a rift from another world, a demonic coalition, a righteous crusade against everyone not of them, a plague, and a withering of the land. While one rages, people of the realms it threatens die every year, kingdoms totter and the wilds grow wilder. The strongest go to face its heart: the winner rises, and a fallen hegemon leaves their sect without its pillar. Left alone, it burns itself out and leaves the land wrecked. You can keep away or join in. From the Location tab you can face its heart (to the death, with its captains), work on a plague's remedy (alchemy, and herbs of the element that overcomes the plague), or raise nurturing arrays at each withered place (formations, and jade or ore). Ending one brings fame, karma and gifts.
- **Mortal wars are fought by mortal armies.** A kingdom's army is mortal soldiers led by a few low cultivators, so a battle is now a whole squad of them, not one champion. Meet an army marching on the town you stand in and you can defend it: each vanguard you beat thins the army, and under 1,500 soldiers it is routed. As a kingdom's National Preceptor or overlord you can now lead its campaigns from the capital: two months' march, then the assault, and a win takes the town. You can also declare its wars and sue for peace. Sending mortals to die stains karma; defending them clears it a little.
- **Fused daos have domains and perfections.** A domain of any dao you have fused into a greater one now unfolds as the fused dao's domain and does the work of every part at once. Fire and Lightning unfold as Calamity: it scorches every foe and lashes them with lightning. With every part at 100, a fused dao is perfected too: it calls all its parts' powers at once (Taiji's Great Ultimate is the Gate of the Underworld and the Great Sun together) for the highest single price among them, not their sum. The battle buttons, the Character tab and the Guide show them.
- **Gathering arts are the treasure hunter's edge.** Heaven-grade materials and finer are rare and usually guarded. A gathering art now helps with them: wandering turns them up more often, seeking them with divine sense finds them likelier (the cap rises from a half to four in five at best), two come at once more often, and a share of guarded finds (up to three in four, more for treasures of the art's element) is lifted before the guardian beast stirs or a rival arrives. This works for both plain gathering and seeking with divine sense.
- **Choose how many.** Feeding your inner world, selling to a market or a shop, buying from a shop or a trader, donating to your sect, storing at or taking from your abode, and eating as a beast all ask how many now: one, five, ten, half, all, or any number you type.
- **A place on the world's rankings wins the proud and the ambitious.** An arrogant heart (fame or an elder's seat) and an ambitious one (your realm at theirs) now also take a place on the Heaven ranking, the young talents, the famous or a craft's best as proof enough. The same goes for their terms as dao companions.
- **Ascending, you keep your sect or clan.** Before, ascending took you out of it, and when you came back down in an avatar your own clan called you an outsider and attacked you. Now you stay a member, honoured as its ancestor above the clouds. If you led it, an heir leads while you are away, and you can take the seat back. Its people think well of you and never turn on your avatar. Old saves where this already happened give you your clan back. No call to battle reaches you across the clouds.
- **Part ways from afar.** A companion, a tamed beast or a dao companion you are not with (one left in the world below when you ascended, say) can now be dismissed or set free from the Relations tab, wherever they are.
- **A leader's approval.** Leading a sect or clan, the Sect tab now shows how far it stands behind you. Approval is half your elders' regard, plus the record of your rule (battles and wars won raise it; lost ones and land lost lower it; it fades over the years), plus gratitude if you founded it. Under 60, an ambitious elder may challenge you for the seat; under 40, one may scheme against you; under 20, the elders against you rise. Even then you choose what happens: crush them, buy them off, or step down. If you are twice as strong as all of them together, none dares. Before, a scheming elder's coup was fought out without you, and if it won you were thrown out of your own sect, even one you founded.
- **Shape a world's powers.** Forging a new world now has an Advanced section. Choose how the powers stand: one holy land ruling every other sect and clan as vassals, five rivals of equal might vying for supremacy, the righteous against the demonic at war from the first day, or nine demonic sects against a single righteous one (or the reverse). Set how many sects and clans there are, and add hidden powers: strong, aloof sects that the world's lists don't show until you find their gate. You can also make any sect or clan to order, with its alignment, element (an ice sect, a lightning sect), specialty, founder's realm and name. Worlds forged without these options come out exactly as before.
- **The left column can show the chronicle.** A button at the top of the left column switches it between your status and the latest chronicle lines, newest first, each cut to its first ten words so it reads at a glance. It follows the world as time passes. The game remembers your choice.
- **Your companions in the left column.** Under your status, each companion now shows as on the battle screen: face, name and health, with their realm in the tooltip.
- **Reading the secret bodies costs years.** The secret bodies reading now shows one body at a time, the humblest you have not yet seen or earned. Heaven takes 5 years of life for the first in a life, and twice as many for each one after (10, 20, 40...), so by the twelfth it asks over 10,000 years: even a Golden Immortal cannot read them all. A muddled cast shows nothing and takes nothing. Before, a clear reading showed every body at once for incense alone.
- **A master feeds their beast what its realm can take.** Playing a tamed spirit beast, your master's yearly gift of a core was always your line's own core, whatever its grade, so a Qi Condensation pup of a great line could be handed a Heaven-grade core. Now the core is never finer than your realm carries: your line's own if it fits, otherwise the finest common core of your element at your grade.
- **No more chronicle lines about other people's orders.** "The forge takes your order for a weapon..." kept appearing even for a new character in no sect: cultivators of the world order weapons too, and their orders were written to your chronicle. Now only your own are.
- **Searching for hidden treasure is greyed out where nothing hides.** In towns, cities and a living sect's or clan's home the button is now disabled with the reason, unless something really was left there (a weapon dropped in a fight, a ruined faction's vault).
- **The Repeat button keeps its size.** It renames itself after every action you take. It now stays on one line, cut short with the full name in its tooltip, so the side column no longer changes height under your first click.
- **A faction's head and ancestor use what is theirs.** Its leader and its ancestor now draw from its library and treasury freely, at every grade (before, the ancestor paid like any grand elder, with grand-elder access). The cost shown is what they actually pay. Their standing with it never sinks below 50, whatever ruling it cost them.

## 0.12.46 (2026-11-09)

- **Battles no longer freeze and crash on a phone.** On a narrow screen, some fights froze the moment the bag was opened (or the battle began), and the game closed about ten seconds later. The battle's buttons were sized to fit their rows, but the rows wrap taller in a narrower column, and the window's scrollbar came and went with that height, so the two kept resizing each other and never finished. Now the buttons' area only grows until the next action, and the screen settles at once.
- **Crash reports say more.** On touch screens the log now notes every touch and what it landed on, every tooltip opened by holding, a text box taking focus (the keyboard), and Android's back gesture, so a crash that comes before a tap registers still shows what was touched.

## 0.12.45 (2026-11-09)

- **An opponent who flees a bout loses it.** In a sect tournament or clan contest, a promotion trial, a duel for the leader's seat, the pit or any other spar, a foe who ran from the ring used to end the fight with no winner, and you were counted as beaten: knocked out of the tournament, or failing the trial. Now whoever flees concedes the bout, and the one who stays wins it. Fleeing yourself counts as a loss. Outside a bout, a flight still leaves no winner.

## 0.12.44 (2026-11-09)

- **Winning someone's heart wholly takes a quest of their own.** Before, a few gifts and talks carried anyone to +100, and the only check past 80 was a condition you either met or didn't. Now, once regard nears their bar, ask what would win their heart (Interact). They set a task by their nature: four talks of the dao a month apart for the diligent, a treasure they name for the greedy, your realm at theirs for the ambitious, renown or an elder's seat for the arrogant, insight into Yin or Yang for the lustful, a clean karma for the compassionate and honourable, infamy or strength for the ruthless, deep insight into a dao for the reclusive. Do it, then show them. Until then, regard past the bar comes at a quarter of the pace.
- **The bar moves with who they are.** It was always 80. Now it is lower for the humble, the kind and the lustful, higher for the proud, the guarded and the treacherous, and 3 higher for every realm they stand above you (65 to 92).
- **A higher realm asks less.** One realm above them, their heart asks three quarters as much; two above, half; three above, nothing at all.
- **Sect and clan members have duties to see to first.** Only an elder of another faction used to refuse to travel with you, and only below 85 regard. Now anyone with a rank is held: an outer disciple's chores (a month), an inner or core disciple's duties (2 to 3), an elder's charges (4), a head's whole house (6), and 2 more while their faction is at war. Help them with it for those months, or pay their faction to spare them (a head cannot be bought away). Either frees them for five years. A realm above them halves the work; two above, no faction stands in your way. One two realms above you still sends a disciple in their stead.
- **A dao companion sets terms of their own.** Beyond their heart, the arrogant want a high seat (an elder or head of a faction, or fame 500); the ambitious and the diligent want proof the bond helps them (guide their cultivation for three months, a new Interact action, or stand a realm above them); the greedy a betrothal gift of spirit stones; the compassionate and honourable a good name; the lustful 25 insight into Yin or Yang; the ruthless your realm at theirs; the reclusive and cautious their whole heart. The proposal's hint says what they ask and whether you meet it. Two realms above them, they ask nothing more.
- **Strength can take what regard won't give.** Two realms above someone, you can bind them as your dao companion by force, or order them on an errand, whatever they think of you. It earns infamy (and a stain on your karma for a forced bond), and a forced companion flees once you no longer stand two realms above them, unless they have come round to it. The upright (the honourable, and those of a righteous sect) refuse and fight you to the death, as they already did when made to follow you.
- **More ways to be rude.** Interact → Be rude: insult them (as before), mock their sect (their sect thinks less of you too), snub them, slap them before witnesses (they lose fame; one stronger than you strikes back), warn them off (a realm above them: they leave this place), or demand tribute (a realm above them: a quarter of their stones, infamy, and a bounty for robbery). The proud, the vengeful and the upright may answer with a duel or a fight to the death.
- **Offerings and peace with the sects.** At a faction's gate, or speaking with one of its elders, offer spirit stones for standing (up to 30 a year). A grudge (a bounty, standing of -60 or worse, or its people counting you an enemy) is not bought with gifts: offerings stop at -30. To end it, make peace with its head or an elder, once a year. They weigh your strength against the whole faction's: one who could raze them to the ground is forgiven for nothing, one half as strong pays half, and the weak pay reparations in full. Peace lifts their bounty, and their people stop counting you an enemy.
- **An abode on a faction's land can come to terms with it.** Each year they could find it and come to tear it down. Now, from the abode's panel, pay them rent every year, or, strong enough to make them think twice (60% of their might), give your word to keep to yourself: no rent, but no formation there may gather the land's qi. While the terms hold they never search it out. Miss the rent, or set a gathering array there, and the pact ends and they are offended.
- **A talent for every craft.** Eight new bodies, one born for each craft (Cauldron Heart, Anvil Bone, Ink Spirit, Array Vein, Thousand-Thread, Spirit Soil, Heaven-Reading Eyes, Gu Nest): skill in it comes half again as fast, and both its bottlenecks stand 10 higher, so a Qi Condensation alchemist born for it grows freely to 45 where others stop at 35. Methods can also roll a talent for a craft (Furnace Heart, Anvil Soul, Spirit Brush, Array Mind, Thousand Threads, Spirit Soil Hands, Heaven-Reading Eye, Worm Whisperer): 6% faster per grade of the method, and the bottlenecks a quarter as many points higher (about 10 on an Immortal-grade method). The Professions tab shows your talent for the craft you are looking at.
- **Repeat your last action.** A new side bar button (key P) does again exactly what you did last, and names it: "Repeat: Seek a treasure here › Immortal-grade ore". It replays the button you pressed and every pick after it, even if a number in a label changed since. If an option is gone, the picker is shown and the repeat stops there. A confirmation is always asked again, never answered for you.

## 0.12.43 (2026-11-08)

- **Choose your face.** When a life begins, under Who you are, pick your skin, hair colour and style (five for men, five for women), eyes, brows, mouth, a beard and a mark (a scar across the eye, a beauty mark, a vermilion brow mark, a cheek scar), or take another face at random, or the one fate gave. A face you choose stays through rerolls. The Character tab's **Change your face** lets you choose again later. Faces fate gave are drawn as they always were, so no one you know looks different.
- **Choose the dao of your domain.** Any dao you hold to 50 insight at Nascent Soul can be unfolded as your domain, not only your deepest. In battle, pick it beside Unfold domain; the one you chose last is unfolded again by default.
- **Every dao's domain does its own work.** Besides +20% damage and being harder to hit, each domain acts every round it stands, as strong as your insight (half at 50, in full at 100). Fire scorches every foe (about a third of a plain blow), a sword slices them and leaves them bleeding, and lightning lashes and may stun. Ice slows every foe to 70%, metal breaks their guards, yin withers them, and poison envenoms them. Wood mends you and roots your foes, water draws 6% of their qi into you, and earth raises a shield around you. Wind quickens your footwork, space makes you far harder to hit, and the body stops your bleeding. Yang burns ailments out of you, karma returns the harm done to you, sound may make foes hesitate, and time ages away their guards and shields. The battle button, its chip, the Character tab and the Guide say what each does. NPCs unfold theirs too: Nascent Soul cultivators now beat beasts of their realm 39% of the time (was 33%).
- **A perfected dao (100 insight) gives a power of its own, at a price.** Each has its own price, paid in qi, years of life or a wound:
  - **Turn Back the River** (Time): your body, blood and qi return to how they were at the start of the last round. Costs 5 years of life.
  - **Tear the Seal** (Space): at a sealed secret realm's entrance (Location tab), tear it open for 6 months and go in. Its barrier still holds back the strong. Costs a wound to the soul and 3 years of life.
  - **One Sword** (a blow three times as hard), **Ashen Heaven** (every foe burns for 4 rounds), **Heaven's Judgment** (lightning strikes and stuns every foe), **Eternal Winter** (every foe freezes for 2 rounds), **Ten Thousand Edges** (shields shatter, guards break), **Mountain Unmoved** (a great shield), **Without a Trace** (leave the battle at once), **Spring Eternal** (lost limbs grow back), **Undying Flesh** (wounds close each round for 3 rounds), **All Rivers Return** (half of every foe's qi), **Gate of the Underworld**, **Great Sun**, **All Debts Paid**, **Hundred Venoms** and **Silencing Note**.
  - Battle powers are called once per battle, from a button beside the domain. NPCs who reach 100 use them too, but the game never spends your years of life when it fights for you.
- **A slain beast gives only its own core.** Half the time, a beast also carried a common core of its element and realm (a Demon Wolf Core beside a Bloodfang Wolf Core), so a kill often gave two. Now a Nine-Colour Deer gives a Nine-Colour Deer Core and a Golden Crow a Golden Crow Core, and nothing else. The common cores are still sold in city markets, rare-treasure stalls and auctions. "Where can I find" says so instead of sending you hunting.
- **A forbidden art shows what being forbidden gives it.** Before, the Arts tab and battle named only the price. Now every forbidden art's line says what it gains: nearly twice its grade's force for 40% less qi, plus a power of its own by its price. Paid in years, no shield stops its blow. Paid in blood, it drinks back a fifth of the blood it spills and leaves the foe bleeding. Paid in cultivation, it scatters the foe's qi. The Arts tab shows this on a red line, and battle tooltips, libraries, manuals and shops show it in the art's summary. Creating a forbidden art names the power beside each price, and the Guide and Glossary list all three.
- **Creating an art no longer jumps to the top of the page.** Choosing a kind under Other arts (Method, Gathering, Travel...) scrolled the Arts tab all the way up, because the page was rebuilt twice and the second time forgot where it was. It now stays where you are, and so does any tab rebuilt twice at once.
- **Time passes faster.** On three large saves of eleven hundred people, a year of the world takes 35% to 53% less work for the processor (0.58 to 0.68 seconds before, 0.32 to 0.38 now). It also makes 53% to 61% less garbage for the memory to clear, so ten years of seclusion end seconds sooner. Most of the work went into weighing fighters: whether a hand can grip, how hard each art strikes, what a sect's members are worth in battle (wars, champions, storehouses). It is now worked out once per fighter, instead of afresh for every blow and every art. A fighter who carries no spare weapon no longer weighs every art to see whether it would want one. Arts already mastered are no longer weighed for practice every month. A sect member's trip to the storehouse no longer counts their whole bag once per thing on the shelves, or asks twice whether they would use each thing. A general store no longer sorts through every design in the world each time it restocks. Nothing plays out differently: the same save passes the same years exactly as before.
- **A hidden master who seizes a new body is hidden no more.** One who died in their ascension tribulation and fled into a Foundation Building body was still counted a hidden master. Saves already holding one are mended when they load.

## 0.12.42 (2026-11-08)

- **A strain of herb you bred and grew can be drawn into an extract.** Work materials listed your own strain, but then said it took ten of its seeds and refused, even with the herb and its seeds both in your bag. Seeds carry their herb's name, and a strain you breed and plant has its seeds first in your bag, so the panel picked them up instead of the herb. It now takes the herb, and leaves the seeds alone.
- **An extract of a bred strain keeps its nature.** Once made, it read back from its recipe as a "Spoiled worked material" with no aspects, and the known recipes list could fail on it. It is now the Pure (strain) Extract it was made as, in your bag and in the known recipes, and mixes with a bred strain on either side read back too.
- **A herb given to someone to absorb is the herb.** Giving a herb to a companion who carried seeds of the same herb had them try to absorb the seeds, which does nothing.
- **Bred strains count as herbs for herbalists and alchemists in the world.** They were taken for extracts already worked, so others never drew them, sold them, or counted them as herbs in stock.

## 0.12.41 (2026-11-08)

- **Battle strength, rebalanced.** A Foundation Builder walking all three paths, with a top-grade sword art, high attributes and a good sword, cut down Deity Transformation masters three realms above in a blow. Every advantage multiplied every other, and together they outran the realms' gap (each realm is three times the last). Now:
  - **Realm is power; everything else is an edge, with diminishing returns.** Arts, gear, attributes, dao, paths walked together and method traits still all help, but twice the usual edge makes a blow about 1.6 times as hard, and ten times the usual about 4.5 times. Fights between ordinary cultivators of one realm are unchanged (still about even). A raised guard or barrier counts as before.
  - **Arts beyond your realm are held back.** An art more than a grade above your realm (a manual found above your station) strikes only as hard as one a grade above it, as a weapon beyond your realm already did.
  - **A second or third path lends half of what it does better.** Walking all three paths took each one's best at once: the Body path's toughness, the Soul path's soul power and the Qi path's arts. A path beside your first now gives half of what it does better than usual (the Body path's toughness counts ×1.25 for a Qi cultivator, not ×1.5). The harmonies and the bonuses for walking several paths are unchanged, but "three paths as one" is an edge like the rest. A born talent for battle (Gifted, Prodigy, Heaven-Defying) still counts in full, as the realm does.
  - **The paths, evened.** The Body path's toughness is ×1.5 (was ×1.7) and its blows ×1.3 (was ×1.4); the Qi path's arts are ×1.3 (was ×1.2). Tempered flesh turns blades and fists, but a qi art's fire or frost gets through it more easily: against qi arts, a path's toughness counts half. A Qi cultivator against a Body cultivator of their realm won 1–6% of duels and now wins 11–18%, so the Body path still has the edge.
  - Measured on Foundation Builders with three paths, a seventh-grade tempered sword art, attributes of 120, a sword a grade above them and 80 insight in the sword: they beat Golden Cores nearly every time (as before), Nascent Souls about a third of the time (was 98%) and Deity Transformation never (was 43%). The rankings, the sect contests and the check that lets the game fight a sure win for you weigh strength the same way.
- **Cores and herbs are months of your own cultivation, not years.** Absorbing a material gave a share of your current stage whoever you were, so a Golden Core's core was worth some 26 years of sitting for anyone, and roots, attributes and method counted for nothing. Now:
  - A material gives months of **your own** cultivation, so your roots (or flesh, or spirit), physique and method all count. A core of your realm's grade is worth about 4 to 8 months, and one a grade or two above a third more for each grade.
  - **Your meridians fill.** Each month the first two materials you absorb count in full, the next four a quarter, and then nothing more enters until the month turns. Absorb all stops there.
  - A material two grades or more above your realm's reach is refused, because it would burst your meridians. One a grade beyond it still tears at them, twice as hard as before.
  - Absorbing needs a method of the path running in you. The Absorb button shows what a material would give, in qi and in months, and how many you have absorbed this month.
- **Pills raise an attribute by the attribute itself.** A pill's grade decides how high it can raise an attribute: 55 for a first-grade pill and 15 more for each grade, up to 145. The gift tapers over the last 15 points and stops there, so a first-grade pill does nothing for an Agility of 90 while a fourth-grade one still does. Attribute pills no longer build tolerance (lifespan and foundation pills still do), so no pill feels wasted. Your body takes the whole gift of a pill only up to a grade above your realm: by pills, a Foundation Builder's attributes rise to 85, a Golden Core's to 100, and so on. NPCs judge pills by the same rule.
- **Physiques move attribute caps.** A body that gives +5 Spirit raises the Spirit cap to 155; a sickly body with -15 Constitution caps it at 135. Pills' reach moves with it.
- **Travel, gathering, concealment and craft arts are listed once, under the Qi path.** They showed on all three path tabs, so each was counted three times. They are listed and made on the Qi path's tab, and still work whatever path you walk.
- **Unshaken Stance, Fleeing Light and Hunter's Stride say what they do.** The three newest moves had no effect line on their battle button or in the Arts tab, and what they left on a fighter never showed. Their buttons now give the numbers (how firm the guard and for how long, how much likelier fleeing is, how much truer the next two blows), the battle screen shows **Unshaken**, **Fleeing light** and **Hunter's stride** chips, and the guide lists them with the mastery they open at. A fighter who just shook off a stun now shows it too, so you know a stun won't hold on them yet.
- **Every material picker shows what a material brings to crafting.** Seeking a treasure with divine sense, seeking one through divination, asking your master for a material and posting a sect request now show each material's kind, tier, element and aspects (and any special nature), as gathering already did. Seeking with divine sense where there is nothing to find now says so, instead of opening an empty list.
- **Gu show how long your food lasts.** Each gu in the Gu Rearing panel shows how many years what you carry will feed it (shared with the other gu that eat the same), in red when two years or fewer are left.
- **Protection now covers deaths with no killer named.** A protecting avatar stepped in only when someone named came to kill: robbers on the road, beast tides, a passing host, kidnappers and a junior never rescued went around it. These threats now carry a realm, and an avatar meets them as it meets a named killer. It also watches over consorts and your whole line (grandchildren and on), not only your children.
- **Calling your spirit beasts.** In the Relations tab, beasts of yours that are elsewhere are listed as "Spirit beast (away)", with **Call it to your side**: within a month it comes to you, down through the clouds or up through them (if it is of Deity Transformation or above). A sect's gate guardian must be called back from its post first.
- **Beasts above the clouds can break past Deity Transformation.** A beast that went up at its master's side was never marked as living above, so it could not break through to True Immortal there (and an older build sent such beasts below). Standing above the clouds now counts.
- **Rewards by level.** A stranger's thanks, and what a waylaid traveller carries, now come from their own realm: stones of about 40 for Qi Condensation, three times as much for each realm above (give or take a fifth), or the finest thing they carry of their realm's grade. Before, they swung from a few stones to a treasure. A cave's manual is now of your grade or one below.
- **Waylaying is worth the karma.** A traveller now carries a purse of their realm (a merchant three times as much), drawn from the earth and not tripled out of nothing as before, and goods of their grade (a merchant three lots). One who hands over their valuables without a fight gives up all of it, at a lighter stain: -2 karma and +2 infamy (a robbery by the sword is still -3 and +3).
- **Breaking a ruin's array depends on your realm and formations skill.** Each fallen sect's or clan's array remembers the realm it was raised at. Each realm you stand above it adds about a third to your chance each month, and each realm below takes away more. Your formations skill against what an array of that grade asks counts too (a tenth for every 20 points), and so do the years that have worn it. A Deity Transformation breaks a Golden Core's array by force alone; a Foundation Builder needs a master's formations to break one. The Location tab says the realm it was raised at.
- **A hidden master's ruins are no longer built over.** A new sect or clan could rise in the old ruins a hidden master had made their retreat, leaving the master sitting in a sect's gate. Houses now leave such ruins alone.
- **The stranger you save gets your humblest healing pill**, not the first one in your bag (a masterpiece among them). The choice names the pill, and a locked pill is never given.
- **Crossing to another world keeps your saves, and you are never stuck there.** Going through a portal started a new save slot, so the old saves seemed gone; now the crossing saves into the same slot. On arrival, whatever the last journey left hanging in that world (an event, a fight held for an answer, a secret realm it was in the middle of, an avatar it was riding) is cleared. Before, it could leave you unable to move. One arriving from above the clouds knows that world's gate back. You can't cross while your soul rides an avatar.

## 0.12.40 (2026-11-08)

- **Tearing down your abode.** In your own abode (Location tab), **Tear it down...** takes it apart in a month, after showing what comes back and what is lost. Everything in its storehouse, its formation disks and its puppets come back to you, and its retainers and apprentices are let go. About half the materials worked into its rooms are prised back out (of the humblest kind that would do); the stones it cost, the stone-built chamber and training ground among them, are spent for good, and whatever still grows in its fields is lost. A beast can **abandon its den** the same way, getting back half the cores worked into it. A clan's seat cannot be torn down.
- **A hidden abode found while you are away no longer brings the fight to you.** When a faction found an abode hidden in its land, its searcher was brought to you wherever you were, even years of travel away, and "Abandon it" never gave the abode up, so they came back every year.
  - Away, the searcher now meets the abode's own defences, as raiders do, and you hear of it in the chronicle. Only when you are home is it put to you.
  - **Abandon it** now gives the abode up for good: they take what was stored there and tear it down, you keep your formation disks and puppets, and no one comes looking again.
  - Under "Interruptions: only risky fights", such a search is no longer answered for you with its first choice (to fight it out): it is put to you with all its choices. No fight event is ever answered with its first choice for you any more.
- **The bottleneck to Foundation no longer asks you to hold back.** At the peak of Qi Condensation, a bottleneck could ask for a victory while holding back, but Qi Condensation is the lowest realm, so there was nothing to hold back to and no Hold back choice in battle. Only bottlenecks from Foundation on ask it now. A save already stuck with it is asked instead to **defeat a foe of your own realm or above** (any Qi Condensation foe counts) when it loads.
- **Working materials shows each material's element and aspects** (Professions tab, Work materials). The material lists give every material's element and aspects beside its name, a line under them gives each chosen material's in full (the element in its colour, and any special nature a worked material already carries), and known recipes show their element too.
- **The clouds are whole again.** Several leaks let the two worlds run into each other:
  - A clan or sect founded above the clouds took the two nearest places as neighbours, and the Upper Realm is drawn over the world below, so its estate could get a road to a town below. Immortals walked down it: still counted above the clouds, they were on neither map, yet they claimed tombs, fought and killed below, and ascended family members vanished from reach. New places now link only to their own side; on loading, any road across the clouds is cut and whoever lives above but stands below goes back up.
  - Beast tamers of Deity Transformation bound beasts a realm above their own (True Immortals) below the clouds, and those beasts later took human form as immortals stuck below for good. A long world could hold a dozen of them, sitting on a fifth of all the spirit stones. A tamer now binds nothing above their own realm, and anyone below the clouds past Deity Transformation is pressed down to its peak (as an avatar is), free to ascend from there.
  - Beast kings born in an immortal lair were sent down to a town below every year. They stay above now.
  - A wandering expert who ascended on the road no longer keeps walking it below.
- **The economy's long run.** Over a thousand years the stones piled up with the high realms and every town ran short of them (merchants paid about half, and stopped hiring and buying from the world's cultivators). Now:
  - An NPC keeps by no more than a Nascent Soul's savings (1,620 stones) before spending on cultivation: past Deity Transformation the savings floor kept rising (4,860, then 14,580) and locked up a third of all stones.
  - Towns pay the cultivators around them by doubling per realm, not tripling, so the few at the top no longer take most of every town's pay.
  - Caravans carry stones each year from towns holding far more than their share to towns short of theirs (nothing is made or lost).
  - The merchants' usual share of the world's stones is 7% (it was 12%, a level no town ever reached), and they hire and buy from NPCs only down to 80% of it (it was 50%), so a town keeps enough to pay you.
  - A shop's commissions step down to lesser work when no one in a poor land can pay for the grade you are known for, instead of none coming at all.
  - On a 1,050-year world, after 20 years: short towns 15 → 4, the median town's purse 25% → 47% of its usual, inequality (Gini) 0.86 → 0.79, cultivators with no stones 195 → 80.
- **Agility (or any attribute) no longer drops to 1.** Raising an attribute while gear added a lot to it clamped the total, gear included, to 150, so with +253 from gear a +1 set the attribute beneath to 150 - 253, and it showed as 1 once the gear changed. Gains now raise the attribute itself (gear and methods come on top). A save already hit has the attribute given back on loading (an ordinary 50 plus what pills gave).
- **Twin paths: the Breakthrough button follows the path in focus.** With one path at the peak of Deity Transformation and the other at a lesser bottleneck (the peak of Golden Blood), the button only offered to ascend, leaving the quick breakthrough as the only way through. It now breaks through the focused path; focus the path at the peak to ascend.
- **Long cultivation no longer stops without a word.** A warning you asked for in the Calendar (a merchant, a festival or an auction a month away) stopped seclusion with only a line in the chronicle, and a lifespan running short did the same. Both now say why on screen, naming what is coming (and where to untick the warning).
- **Friends no longer turn on you while you cultivate.** A friend, master or companion asking for help, a loan or an oath of brotherhood while you were busy was refused for you, at a loss of 8 to 15 regard each time, until over long seclusions they hated you (a master turned on one at -100). Now they leave word and go. Also:
  - Several dao companions sour from sharing you only down to 30 (the proud lower, and may still leave), not without end.
  - A soured bond without enmity (master, disciple, parent, child, dao companion, sworn kin) mends a few points each year on its own.
  - Past 80, regard comes at a quarter of the pace until their **heart's wish** is met, by their nature (the ambitious want someone of their realm, the proud someone of standing, the compassionate a clean karma, and so on; Relations shows it). Two gifts and a talk no longer win anyone's devotion.
- **A schemer after your seat can be threatened or paid off** (Relations): your realm against their nerve, or stones (more for each conspirator). Before, only expelling or fighting them was offered, beside "expose" and "join" a plot against yourself.
- **Enemies of your naming.** Someone who killed one of yours (or hates you, or counts you their enemy) can be **counted your enemy** in Relations, alone or with their house. A soul that crushes a host for its body is now a murderer to the host's kin: a fleeing soul once took a player's wife and was no one's enemy for it.
- **An ascended master still answers.** Send word with a jade slip (Relations): they find materials (from above, finer ones), and an avatar of theirs prepares your breakthrough at three quarters of their own help. Marrow is washed only hand to bone.
- **The Inner World protects.** Those kept inside are no longer counted at the place outside, so rampages, tides, raids and fights there no longer find and kill them, and no one outside can kill them.
- **Your own designs stay yours.** Pills, arrays and puppets of your own invention turned up for sale at the grand auction, in markets and shops, though no one else knew how to make them. They stay out of trade until someone outside your line knows them (a disciple you taught, a sect that holds them).
- **The phone fight screen** (Android):
  - Aiming opens as buttons in the panel, grouped (outer, bones, organs, meridians), not a pop-up of thirty parts that ran off the screen. A beast's fight froze on it and crashed: the menu was freed with the panel while still closing. Holding back steps through the realms with a button too.
