  - With both bodies shown as lists, the arts were drawn over Auto-fight, Flee and Surrender (which could no longer be pressed): the wrapping rows are now measured again once they know their width.
  - Your companions and every foe, as tokens, sit under your own fighter in the room the body leaves there, so the log between the columns gets the height.
- **Gu and the schools of a craft no longer squeeze their names into a letter-wide column** beside empty buttons; Gu no longer asks you to choose a school it does not have.
- **Map names**: a place whose name would cover another's tries above it and to either side, and at full zoom every name is drawn.
- **Items:**
  - **Lock** an item (Inventory) and it is never sold or donated, one at a time or with "Sell all shown" and "Donate all shown".
  - **Absorb all** takes in everything you carry that feeds the path in focus, the humblest first, until a bottleneck stops it (locked items, and those more than a grade above your realm, are left). Absorbing a material that far above your realm now tears at your meridians.
  - Gear given to one who holds you dear (60) is worn at once for your sake, even over what they had.
  - City markets have 32 stalls (they had 14) with deeper stacks of materials, shops more shelves, and cities three to seven shops.
  - **Buy what is missing here**: a known design lacking materials can buy them from the stalls where you stand (a smith sells blades and buys ore in one place).
- **The land:**
  - **Raze it to ruins**: the seat of a house that is gone (or one you hold) can be brought down to ruins, held by no one. **Give it back to the wild**: ruins grow over into wild land where herbs, ores and beasts are found again, and land you hold can be let go. New houses rise in old ruins instead of on new ground, so a world swept clean of its houses no longer fills with twice as many places.
  - Companions (a beast's kin and young, tamed beasts, friends) can be **left at your abode or den** to wait, instead of parted with; your den is named on the map.
  - A founder's town academy sends its pupils to join the founder's sect or clan (they can still be taken as personal disciples).
  - **Puppets can guard a sect's gate** (Sect tab, Guardians): up to three, never aging, ascending or dying, for a yearly upkeep from the treasury (unpaid, one wears down and breaks). Beast guardians no longer take human form at their post and walk off.
  - **Herbalism**: the soil of your beds is never poorer than your herbalism, and a master works the fields with up to twelve hands (it was nine).
- **Divination**: **Heaven's treasures** (skill 50) foresees when and where the next heaven-and-earth treasure will be born, and it is then born as foreseen. **The secret bodies** (skill 100) reveals what deed earns each one.
- **Fights:**
  - Any number of foes two realms or more below you is fought for you (it was at most two, a realm below), and beasts beaten in a fight fought for you can have their essence drained instead (Settings).
  - Lesser cultivators who come at you may **flee** at the sight of you (the cautious always, the arrogant and vengeful never): cut them down or let them run.
  - New moves: **Fleeing Light** (movement) carries you out of reach at once and makes your next flight far likelier; **Hunter's Stride** (movement) makes your next two blows far truer; **Unshaken Stance** (guard) lets nothing stun you or throw you off balance for three rounds.
  - Forbidden arts do what no other art can: a blow paid in years passes through any shield, one paid in blood drinks a fifth of the blood it spills, one paid in cultivation scatters the foe's own qi.
- **People:**
  - One two realms or more above you sends a disciple to travel with you rather than come themselves.
  - Two realms above someone, you can **make them follow you** whatever they think of you (infamy); the upright would rather fight you to the death.
  - **Insult them**: rudeness short of murder. The proud may demand a duel there and then.
- **Sects:**
  - **Entrance trials**: the testing stone, the ladder of heart demons and the elders' eye. A fine showing enters as an inner disciple, a brilliant one as a core disciple claimed by an elder, with the sect's regard and a name already made. A core disciple who leaves is a deserter, with a bounty on them.
  - Missions are posted by people (a member, an allied sect's elder, someone of the nearest town) whose regard you win with the work, and who add a reward of their own. You can **post requests for materials** on the board, for a reward the board holds until a member brings them.
- **Quick save**: Ctrl+S keeps the moment in a quick slot, Ctrl+L goes back to it (also in Save slots, and on the death screen).
- **Sandbox character creation**: in point buy, "Sandbox: unlimited points" lets you buy anything, attributes up to 150.

## 0.12.39 (2026-11-06)

- **Aspects in every craft (guide):** a new guide topic maps what each material aspect becomes in every kind of work: pills, weapon poisons, weapons, armour, accessories, tools, talismans, battle and workshop arrays, fighting and assistant puppets, and gu. For each effect it gives the aspects that make it, how much each point gives, and what the effect does in play (caps included). It is read from the crafting rules themselves, so it stays true as they change.
- **No fight is ever lost for you.** A fight the game settles while you cultivate, rest, travel or gather is now fought only when you are sure to win it (you are hale, and face at most two foes, all a realm below you), whatever your interruption setting and whatever you have muted.
  - "Don't stop me for this again" on bandits, beasts, raiders and hunters no longer fights out a foe you could lose to. Before, a muted robber of your own realm, met while you were still hurt, was fought blind and could kill you; now such an encounter stops you with all its choices (fight, pay, flee).
  - A fight fought for you that turns (a heavy blow, a wound, your health falling below 65, or it drags past 20 rounds) is handed to you on the fight screen where it stands, instead of being played out to the end.
  - "Interruptions: nothing (risky)" is now "only risky fights": everything else is still handled the cautious way, but a fight you could lose is put to you.
- **Closing the game on a battle's result screen no longer loses the fight.** Its result, loot and chronicle entry used to be taken only when you pressed a button there. Now, if you close the window on that screen, the fight is settled first (beasts beaten are finished, people beaten are spared) and then saved.
- **Feeding gu by hand.** Each gu in the Gu panel now has a **Feed...** button: pick a material of its aspect, and it gets back 25% vigour (handy after a battle tires it). The yearly meal now takes the humblest matching material you carry, not whichever came first in the bag.
- **A beast that took human form can always shift shape again.** One that had already evolved by then (a Heavenly or Moon-Devouring Wolf, a Storm King Ape, a Jade Flood Dragon...) lost the "Return to your beast shape" button and could not change shape in battle either: only the base kinds were recognised. Such saves work again as they are.
  - The beast shape also keeps the form it grew into: an evolution won as a beast is no longer undone by walking as a person and shifting back.

## 0.12.38 (2026-11-06)

- **The ghost's road.** A soul that dies at Golden Core on the Soul path, Nascent Soul on the Qi path or Deity Transformation on the Body path can now linger as a ghost instead of seizing a body.
  - A ghost keeps only its soul arts and soul wards, and starts again at Qi Condensation on a demonic method.
  - It grows fastest by devouring the ghosts it defeats and by feeding on a town's fear.
  - It can try to possess a living body at any time; if the body's soul wins, the ghost is obliterated.
  - At Deity Transformation, with three fifth-grade treasures, it can spend ten years reforming a body of its own.
  - Plain blows mostly pass through a ghost, while fire and lightning burn it. Restless ghosts appear in ruins, and the vengeful slain sometimes rise as ghosts.
- **Gu, a new profession.** Rear gu from two materials of their aspect, and feed them every year or they starve.
  - Moonlight Gu strike in battle, Thousand-Venom Gu poison, and Mending Gu heal.
  - A carried Qi-Gathering Gu speeds cultivation.
  - The demonic Heart-Eating Gu kills its host unseen within a few years.
- **Breeding spirit beasts.** Pair two of your tamed beasts to raise hatchlings that mix both parents' roots, stats and blood.
  - Crossing kinds gives named crossbreeds, which are sometimes stronger than either parent.
  - Crossing body plans risks a chimeric failure.
- **Beasts:**
  - They live 40% longer than a person of their realm.
  - Their breakthroughs rest on bloodline purity more than on comprehension.
  - A played beast can be born with a parent of its line in its pack, or start as a lost hatchling.
- **Masters and disciples:**
  - A master can prepare a disciple's breakthrough, which lastingly improves the quality of what it forms.
  - Anyone of a higher realm can wash someone's marrow once a century, for a cleaner dao foundation.
  - A disciple can ask their master to find a material, prepare their breakthrough, or wash their marrow.
  - Methods you teach are now actually taken up by the student.
- **Family:**
  - You can name your children, your tamed beasts, and yourself as a beast.
  - Naming a newborn with divination and clean karma can add up to +5 luck.
  - You choose which orphan to adopt.
  - Your infamy, or fame, shields your family from robbers and rivals.
  - Friends and dao companions ask for help only against foes they couldn't beat alone.
- **Battle:**
  - **Hold back** to strike no harder than a lower realm. A bottleneck can now ask you to win while holding back, instead of surviving at death's door.
  - **Ink formations:** inscribe killing, barrier or swift arrays mid-fight with ink and divine sense; the ink sets the element.
  - **Drifting Cloud Arrays** (swift disks) can be crafted from Swift materials.
  - **Arrays set up where you fight** (at your abode, or planted there) can be roused from the battle bag once a fight, as if planted, at the strength the place gives them. They aren't worn or used up.
- **Bodies and blood of your own:**
  - Founding a bloodline is now a rewriting minigame. An ordinary result is a little weaker than heaven's own lines, and a near-perfect one is stronger.
  - You can now forge a physique of your own once, around a focus and an element.
- **Crafting omens:** fine work can draw signs that strengthen it (pill clouds, a pill tribulation, a blade's cry, glowing ink…), and very poor work can backfire (a bursting furnace, shattering ore…).
- **Calendar warnings:** tick what you want to hear of a month ahead, and time passing stops for it.
- **Shaping a world** now needs a fully grown inner world. The world's maker can walk down into its lower world freely, and back up.
- **Fixes:**
  - Killing arrays couldn't be planted: the choice of form for their killing qi never appeared.
  - Puppets and formation disks can now be repaired with a beast core of their grade or better, not only their design's material.
  - A fullscreen game moved to another monitor now refits itself to that screen instead of sitting in a corner.

## 0.12.37 (2026-11-06)

- **Arts are made of moves now.** Attack, defense and movement arts are built one move at a time. The Arts tab's Create section has three buttons: **Temper a move**, **Bind moves into an art**, and **Other arts** (methods, crafts, healing and the rest).
- **Tempering is a new minigame**, played like alchemy and forging, with nothing left to chance. It replaces the old meridian-tracing composer.
  - Circulate qi, press it into the move, sharpen it, infuse its effect, then release it.
  - Qi held past the golden band builds backlash. At 100 the qi turns on you and the move is lost.
  - The rounds are few, so no move can be everything: make a heavy blow, a sure one, or a long poison.
- **What sets what is spelled out** in the creator, the minigame and the Guide:
  - **Realm** sets the qi you can hold steady: 40 + 8 per realm.
  - **Insight** beyond what the move needs adds rounds: 10, plus 1 for every 5 insight beyond, up to 16.
  - **Comprehension** sets where accuracy starts.
  - **Path** gives its own strength: Qi circulates more, Body presses harder, Soul sharpens and steadies better.
- **Every move has three measures.** An attack has power, accuracy and effect; a guard has strength, hold and answer; a step has speed, evasion and trick. 40 on a measure is an art's usual strength.
- **Effects are open to everyone, as long as they make sense for the move.** An attack's effect can be anything its element and form allow: a water move cannot burn, a fire one cannot poison, a blade severs whatever its element, and piercing, sweeping, shattering, sundering, dread and echoing suit any move. Effects no longer cost extra insight.
- **Each effect's exact numbers are shown.** The Arts tab's "What each effect does" lists every effect at no infusion, the usual, and full infusion. For example, a sealing move has a 21%, 35% or 56% chance to seal. Hovering a move shows its own numbers.
- **Moves are pieces to build with.** A move can be used in battle on its own. It is listed compactly and is never practised, refined, taught or written down.
- **Binding four or more moves makes an art.** The moves must share one kind, form and element, and they are used up and become the art, which you then practise and refine.
  - The art's 15% bonus is placed three times, 5% each, on power, accuracy or effect. The split is fixed for good.
  - Each move past four shrinks the bonus, keeping your split in proportion: 9% at five moves, 5% at six, 2% at seven, nothing from eight.
  - You can add a move to an art you bound later. The move is used up and the bonus shrinks.
- **NPCs temper their own arts the same way**, leaning as they are: the arrogant toward force, the cautious toward a sure blow, the vengeful toward a cruel effect.
- **Full Force is gone**, for cultivators and beasts alike. Older arts otherwise fight exactly as before.
- **Battle aiming:** the aim menu says its odds are for a plain strike. Hovering an art or a move shows its own chance to hit the part you aim at, with its mastery and the move's accuracy included.
- **Balance check** (`SimRunner duels`): at the same grade, a four-move tempered art beats an older art about 54% of the time, a lone move about 43%, and a seven-move art about 52%.
- **Fixes and smaller changes:**
  - **A tamed beast king leaves its lair.** Taming a lair's king didn't free the lair, so the beast stayed "in the jungle" while also guarding your sect, and killing it there killed your guardian. Older saves are fixed too.
  - **Coming home from another world restores you.** Crossing back to a world you left used to make a stranger copy of you. Now you are yourself again, with your kin, sect, friends, disciples and abodes, and everything you carried.
  - **Nearby travel takes no time once you are fast enough.** When you can cross your whole world in a month, places within three road steps take no time. You get one more step for every further span of the world your speed covers, up to eight, so travel arts and speed still matter.
  - **Travel, gathering, concealment and craft arts belong to every path:** they show, and can be made, on the Body and Soul tabs too.
  - **Seek a treasure asks for a grade first.** It used to list only the highest-grade materials, so a mortal saw only Primordial ones.
  - **Inner world feeding:** feed all of a stack or just one, from the Character and Inventory tabs.
  - **Character creation fits landscape screens** at larger interface scales: the columns squeeze to fit, or stack when they can't.
  - **Beasts eat herbs.** As a beast, Eat a herb of qi to feed your cultivation, or one of vitality to close your wounds (and feed it a little). It's weaker than a pill, and your belly takes five a month at full effect, then a quarter.

## 0.12.36 (2026-11-06)

- **Gathering shows what a material is good for:** the Gather menu (every kind, and each material to favour) and Where can I find...? list each material's crafting aspects (Hard, Sharp, Spirit, Qi, Swift...) and any special nature, as the Professions tab does.

## 0.12.35 (2026-11-06)

- **Seeking a secret body:** the secret bodies your past lives unlocked now show in Seek a fate's physique list, so you can reroll until one comes up (they were already rollable, but could not be picked to stop on).

## 0.12.34 (2026-11-06)

- **The Wealth ranking** (Records tab → Rankings → Wealth): the richest cultivators, sects and clans, and towns. Wealth is spirit stones and what one's goods would fetch (a town's, its merchants' purse); the board never shows the sums, only a grade beside the middling one of the same kind: destitute, poor, modest, comfortable, prosperous, wealthy or very wealthy. It tells you your own grade and place among the world's cultivators, and your faction's.
- **Trading with cultivators** (Relations → Trade): buy what someone where you are carries, or sell them what they can use (gear they could wield, pills, talismans, manuals they lack, the materials of their crafts). A stranger asks about half again an item's worth and pays under half for yours; one who thinks well of you deals near its worth; the greedy drive a harder bargain; no one spends more than half of what they hold on one deal, and what they buy they may put to use at once. Prices for stacks: one, some or all.
- **Trading with sects and clans** (Location tab, at their gate): buy from the treasury (up to Earth grade, finer with standing and as a guest elder) or sell to it, at prices by your standing. Not your own faction (it trades in contribution), not one at war with yours, and a righteous one turns away the notorious.
- **Seeking someone from a ranking:** every board of people has a button to divine where one of them is (a reading of a person). Strangers can be sought now, but their thread of karma is thin: only a clear cast shows where they are.
- The Guide (Reputation and rankings) describes all of this.
- **Better crash reports:** Settings → **Copy the logs** copies the last four runs at once, each headed with how it ended (in the background, closed, or stopped while open: a crash). The log now notes every button pressed, window opened, tab shown and fight begun, so a crash shows what you had just done; each run starts by saying how the one before it ended, and on Android, when that run stopped while open, what the system log kept of its last moments (even when Android's own record of the exit is missing or unreadable, which it now also says).

## 0.12.33 (2026-11-06)

- **Destroying a soul:** with 20 insight into the Dao of Yin, or any soul art (a soul-striking attack art or a soul ward), you can grip a great soul as it leaves its body. After beating a Nascent Soul (or a Soul cultivator of Golden Core), choose **Kill and destroy their soul**, and it will seize no one's body, yours or anyone else's. A great soul you cut down in the fight itself is caught and ground away as it flees. The Guide (Seizing a body) describes it.
- **Fixed: commissions that paid 0 spirit stones.** The world's cultivators who took paid work (since 0.12.28), sold their spare gear and herbs, and the clans' enterprises were emptying the towns' merchant purses, which pay your wages, sales and commissions: in some worlds 16 of 18 towns were broke. Now the merchants hire the world's cultivators and buy their wares only while their purse is at least half full, and a clan's enterprises take at most a fifth of its town's purse a year. Measured over 300 years of history and 20 of play on three worlds: at most one town broke at a time (was up to 16 of 18), and the books still balance to the stone.
- **Commissions are paid in trust:** the client leaves the pay with your shop when they place the order, so a filled order always pays in full, whatever the town holds by then; an order that lapses or is turned away takes its pay back. Clients are now also the region's rich cultivators (by name) and its sects and clans, who can pay for finer works than a small town's merchants; an order no one can pay for is not placed. Orders from older saves are paid as before, and are not handed over if the town cannot pay.
- **Choosing disciples:** from Elder up, the Sect tab has a Disciples section listing the juniors without a master who could walk your road: a path you walk, and on the Qi path a root sharing an element with yours, best suited first. It shows whether your Qi method would run whole in them or at half strength, their talent and what they think of you, and you can take them as disciples from there.
- **Raw materials only:** processing (smelting, extracts, timber, plates, paper, ink) already refused worked materials, but drawing extracts could also use up the seeds of the same herb in your bag; it no longer does, and seeds are never taken as herbs. A spirit farmer's commission now wants living herbs, not extracts.

## 0.12.32 (2026-11-06)

- **Changing shape in battle:** a beast that has taken human form can change shape mid-fight (it costs the turn, and the wounds come along, part for part where the shapes share one). In beast shape it fights with its kind's natural attack and its nature (shell, warded soul, speed...), in human shape with its arts and weapons. The world's tamed beasts that took human form do it too when a fight turns against them, and fold their beast shape away when the fight ends.
- **The Ice Phoenix:** a legendary ice beast of Deity Transformation, with its own core, Ice Phoenix Blood (a cold no fire can thaw, steeped in the Dao of Ice) and an evolution into the Eternal Frost Phoenix. It is regenerating, elusive and **glacial**, a new nature: whoever strikes it with fists, palms or a close blade is frozen slow and keeps the cold in their meridians.
- **Crippling a cultivation:** shatter the dantian and the Qi path falls to a mortal's (Body and Soul paths untouched). Cripple a beaten foe instead of killing them, cripple outright anyone two realms beneath you (Relations), or cripple yourself to rebuild on a clean foundation (Character tab). A victor about to kill you may let you walk away if you shatter your own dantian: the honourable most often, the ruthless and demonic seldom.
- **People bow, or blanch:** fame, infamy and realm decide how townsfolk, sect gate disciples and the people you face carry themselves before you, from whispers and bows to barred doors and kneeling juniors. Told on arrival, on the Location tab, and when you face someone. Flavour only.
- **Inheritance Pavilion:** a new sect and clan hall; each level lets every master of the faction guide 2 more personal disciples than the usual 6 (and the world's masters take a few more juniors). Disciple Dwellings still raise how many members the faction supports.
- The Guide (Spirit beasts; Combat; Sects and clans; Crippling a cultivation) describes all of this.
- **The Overlord Body** (Legendary): meridians and sea of consciousness sealed, so it walks only the Body path for life and swallows any spiritual root; ×1.8 cultivation, +15 Strength, +20 Constitution, +5 Agility, +30 years, +10% tribulation. Its one path counts as two walked together from Foundation Building and as three from Nascent Soul. Soul strikes lose half their force against it and no soul can seize it, but it holds no soul art and no Qi or Soul method, its divine sense is dim, and qi arts cost it 1.5× the qi. Only birth gives it (no treasure or dormant awakening), and a chosen Qi or Soul path never rolls it.
- **Ascenders stay above the clouds:** an NPC who ascended kept their sect or clan, and its yearly gathering pulled them back to its gate below as a True Immortal, where they led it and contested treasures. Now they leave it as the player does (a clan keeps them as its ancestor above), and reach below only through an avatar sent to their clan's home or their kin, which teaches their children and disciples or guards them. Older saves send their strays back above. (True Immortals still met below are great beasts that took human form.)
- **Extracts hold no seeds:** seeds can no longer be drawn from an extract, nor such seeds planted or crossed. In older saves they crumble, and a field sown with them grows the herb the extract was drawn from.

## 0.12.31 (2026-11-05)

- **Demonic cultivation, reworked:** a demonic method no longer cultivates faster than any other while you sit; it grows by killing, and now devours far more: a quarter of a stage of qi from a person of your own realm and stage (was 15%), an eighth from a beast (was 7.5%). Its wear on the dao foundation now settles toward 25 instead of ruining it: 0.8 a year from 50 as before, slowing as it nears 25 and never going below, so demonic cultivators are no longer walled off from later breakthroughs, and foundation pills hold. Demonic methods in older saves lose their old speed boost.
- **Barrier is now Guarding Aura:** the defence art's first move is renamed, so it is no longer confused with the Qi Shield (a pool of qi that soaks damage). The battle log, status chip, advice, glossary and guide say "guarding aura".
- **Secret bodies at birth:** the secret bodies your past lives earned can now come up when you roll a fate, not only in point buy, as rarely as a Mythic body (the greater ones rarer still).
- **Fixed:** the Guide's Every physique list showed only the secret bodies earned by the character being played; it now lists every one earned in any life, as point buy does.
- **The Guide brought up to date:** the town founding cost (8,000), the closed economy of spirit stones, pill tolerance by gift and pills and gear above your realm, gear worn piece by piece, every defence and movement move, feints, disarming, guards in time and battle advice, divine sense in battle, the daos' current names (Yang and Yin, not Life and Death) and the crafts they feed, tribulation bolts, Disciple Dwellings, a sect's change of focus, city shops, commissions to your own design, apprenticeships, waylaying, beasts guarding an inheritance, and ascension no longer called the end of the road.

## 0.12.30 (2026-11-05)

- **The world's great souls refuse death:** when a Nascent Soul (or a Soul cultivator of Golden Core) dies with its soul whole, it may flee the corpse, as you can (the ruthless and the ambitious most often), and seize the most gifted body in reach that is no stronger than it, crushing the soul that lived there, or be destroyed trying. The world's history tells of it.
- **A soul seeking a body (very rare):** if you stand where such a soul died, or a step away, and are no stronger than it, it may fly at you. Hold your sea of consciousness (soul wards on your gear, soul-warding arts and divine sense help, and a soul at home fights harder than one without a body), or burn the qi you have gathered toward your next stage to scald it out. Throw it out and it is destroyed for good, leaving you stronger in spirit, with a trace of its dao and perhaps one of its arts; win only at the last and your soul is scarred; lose, and it walks away in your body (your crushed soul cannot flee).
- The Guide (A world in motion; Seizing a body) describes what the world's cultivators now do, and the fleeing souls.

## 0.12.29 (2026-11-05)

- **The great undertake what you can:** Nascent Souls split off avatars, which cultivate beside them; Deity Transformations deep in the Dao of Space fold an inner world into themselves, weave their dao into it and bring their kin and disciples inside; ambitious Nascent Souls and the heads of clans rewrite their blood into a line of their own and give it to their descendants and disciples; and a rich cultivator in their own abode founds a town beside it (a few at a time in all the world), and, once it has grown into a city, may make it the seat of a kingdom.
- An apprentice comes away knowing one of their master's designs; a crafter below 20 skill follows designs rather than devising new ones.
- What the world's cultivators carry beyond a dozen things (besides what they wear) is used up, given away or worn out each year, so their bags do not fill without end.

## 0.12.28 (2026-11-05)

- **The world's cultivators work and apprentice:** those who answer to no sect earn their keep as you can: the young and unskilled apprentice themselves to a city's craft shop for a stipend (their craft grows quickly, up to the master's), and the short of stones take the best safe work where they stand for half a year (porters and scribes, guards, tutors, miners, crafters taking commissions), paid from what the place can pay, as you are.

## 0.12.27 (2026-11-05)

- **The world's cultivators keep abodes:** those who answer to no sect carve out cave abodes on the richest unclaimed wild land at hand (at most three places in ten, so land is left for you), and keep them as you do: they stay home in seclusion much of the time (their arrays then really speed their cultivation), fit out the room of their craft with its skill and materials, build a breakthrough chamber, set up the formation disks they carry, and grow herbs, harvesting what ripens, keeping some for their cauldrons and selling the rest. An abode whose owner dies with no heir falls empty, and its land is free again.

## 0.12.26 (2026-11-05)

- **The world's crafters work like you:** they gather the raw materials of their craft and work them (smelting ingots, drawing extracts, seasoning timber, carving jade plates), and what they make with worked materials comes out finer. A skilled hand whose designs (and whose sect's) all lie below what it can now make devises one at its grade.
- **They commission and consign:** in a city, a cultivator without a weapon has the best smithy make one to order (the finest of its master's designs they can afford twice over), and those with spare gear send it to the grand auction.

## 0.12.25 (2026-11-05)

- **The world's cultivators contemplate the dao:** every year they spend months on it as you do (more for the greater realms, the diligent and the reclusive), mostly on their own dao, where they stand mattering, a Dao Abode that serves them deepening it for its price, and epiphanies now and then. Over a world's history some now become true masters (in a 300-year world: over a hundred past 50 insight, a few past 80), and sects raise Dao Abodes for them. Nascent Souls past 60 reach for a dao to fuse with theirs, and some succeed.
- **They create and refine their own arts:** masters of Golden Core and above now and then create an art of their own dao at the grade their insight allows (it can fail, as yours can), and refine the arts they made; half go into their sect's library. A few arts a year in all the world.
- Hidden masters are no longer hired as shop keepers in cities: they stay in their wilds.

## 0.12.24 (2026-11-05)

- **Apprentice yourself to a shop:** every craft shop (Shops tab: Apprentice yourself here..., or Find work in a city) takes apprentices in its craft: alchemy, forging, talismans, formations or puppetry. No skill is needed to start; you earn a small stipend, and the craft grows about half as fast as making works yourself, without spending materials, until you know what its master knows.
- **Commissioning made clearer:** every design you know has a **Commission...** button (in a city with a shop of its craft): pick how many and the shop makes it as it is. A craft shop's **Commission a work...** offers your known designs, or a new design of your own (the inventor, where a **Commission** button now also sits in the pinned bar beside Record). What you have ordered is listed at the top of the Professions tab.
- **Fullscreen:** a Fullscreen option in Settings (desktop), remembered between runs.
- **Forget a place of interest:** each place in the Map tab's Places of interest has a **Forget** button; Remember forgotten places brings them back. A place comes back on its own when there is a new reason to go there.
- **Raise a Dao Abode** now sits by your abode's name (Location tab, Home).
- **The protective formation is no longer an upgrade:** an abode is guarded only by formation disks set up in it (protection and killing arrays), which hold off raiders and herb thieves; the Home view shows its protection beside its qi and concealment. A protective formation built in an older save still counts.
- **One clan at a time:** while you still belong to a clan you raised (or watch over as its ancestor), you cannot raise another in another abode; leave it first (Sect tab).
- **Fixed:** the name you type for a new art was ignored if you typed it after choosing its kind, element or form; the art now takes the name in the box when you create it.

## 0.12.22 (2026-11-05)

- **Dao Abodes:** halls that lay one dao bare. Contemplating that dao in one comes ×1.5 as readily or more (the Contemplate the Dao menu shows it, and the result says what it added and cost).
  - **Raising one:** a master of the dao (80+ insight) raises one in their own abode, or at their faction's home if it is among the five mightiest (Location tab: Raise a Dao Abode here...). It takes a year and 6 materials of Heaven grade or finer of each of the five elements (30 in all); you choose your finest materials or the plainest that will do. The deeper your insight and the finer the materials, the stronger it: ×1.5 at 80 insight with Heaven-grade materials, up to ×2.75 at perfection with Primordial ones. Raising one for your faction earns 1,500 contribution.
  - **Using one:** one in an abode serves whoever may use the abode, and burns spirit stones for each month it is used (30 a month at Heaven grade, doubling each grade finer). A faction's serves its members at its hall for contribution each month (10 at Heaven grade, doubling); its leader and ancestor use it free. A month you cannot pay, it lies dark. NPC members at home deepen their insight in it each year.
  - **The mighty:** the three mightiest sects keep one for their own dao from the dawn of history (in a world saved before, they find theirs in its first year). The five mightiest raise one for their focus when a master of it is among them, and the two mightiest more for other daos (up to three). A faction half again as mighty as an enemy at war can seize its Dao Abodes (as its leader, standing there: Location tab); a fallen faction's pass to whoever holds the land, or lie abandoned, open to anyone at half strength, until a mighty faction claims them. The Sect tab lists your faction's.

## 0.12.21 (2026-11-05)

- **Abode rooms are fitted out by those who work the craft:** the alchemy room, the forge, the talisman study, the puppet workshop, the library and formation hall, and the herb garden are no longer bought with spirit stones. Each level needs your skill in its craft (10 for the first, 15 more for each after; formations or divination for the library) and that craft's own materials (herbs, ores, talisman paper or ink, spirit wood, jade): 4 Yellow-grade or better for the first, and more, finer ones after. The breakthrough chamber, the training ground and the protective formation are still built with stones.
- **An abode gathers qi and hides only through formations:** the spirit gathering array and the concealment array are no longer upgrades. Set up formation disks in your abode instead (Inventory: Set up here): disks that gather qi speed your cultivation there, and disks that conceal hide it from raiders, from the faction whose land it is on and from heart demons in seclusion (every 20 concealment hides it a level more, up to 5; the strongest disk counts in full, each further one half as much). Make them, buy them at a formation shop, or commission one. The abode's Home view shows the qi and concealment its formations give. Levels of either array built in an older save still count. A beast's den still grows its spring and hidden entrance with cores.

## 0.12.20 (2026-11-05)

- **Commission a shop to make it to your design:** no need to take up a craft to get exactly what you want. In a city, draft a design in the Professions tab's inventor (the materials, the shape, the piece, the element, as when inventing one) and press **Commission it at...**: the most skilled shop of that craft where you stand makes it from its own stock, at its master's skill (they must be able to work its grade; their skill sets its quality), and sends it to you when it is done, wherever you are. It costs the work's market price and 60% more for working to your design, paid when you order; pills, talismans and poisons can be ordered 3, 5 or 10 at a time. Every craft's shop (Shops tab) has a **Commission a work to your design...** button that opens the inventor at its craft, and what you have ordered is listed beneath, with when it is ready.

## 0.12.19 (2026-11-05)

- **Beasts that guard an inheritance:** when you seal an inheritance (Inventory), you can set one of your tamed beasts to guard it. Bound by your pact, it stays at the place, grows there through the years (breaking through and evolving as tamed beasts do), and strangers of a lesser realm do not dare the trial while it watches. Whoever passes the trial (a later life of yours, say) finds it waiting (Location tab): **keep it**, and the pact passes to you, with no stain (it was its master's will), or **release it**, its duty done, for good karma. A stranger who claims it decides by their nature.

## 0.12.18 (2026-11-05)

- **Change a sect's focus:** the leader of a sect can turn it to another element (Sect tab, Leadership: Change the sect's focus), from Metal to Water, say. It costs the treasury (more for a larger, prouder sect) and a tenth of its prestige, and a sect can turn only once every ten years. Members whose roots follow the old element take it hard; those of the new one take heart. Its library then lacks arts of the new element: year by year, its cultivators of Foundation Building and above go into seclusion and compose a method, an attack art, a defence art and a movement art of it (each comes to the library and its composer knows it; arts of the new element you give the library count too). The Leadership section says what is still lacking; the world's history notes when the turn is complete.

## 0.12.17 (2026-11-05)

- **Every crafted thing at a glance:** the Inventory's bars now show what any crafted thing would do for you, not only pills.
  - **Weapons, armour and accessories** are weighed against what you wear: weapon force, qi conduction, protection, soul ward, each special property (lifesteal, armour break, qi siphon, thorns, regeneration, qi spring, cultivation speed) and each attribute, from your gear now to your gear with it in its place ("For you, in place of your Black Iron Chest Piece"), or, for what you wear, without it to with it. Worked out as battle works them out: weighed by grade against your realm, stacked, capped.
  - **Talismans, formation disks, puppets and prosthetics** show each effect as much as you can draw on at your realm out of its full strength, with a disk's strength where you stand, and a puppet's or prosthetic's frame.
  - The element, a grade above your realm and uses left sit beneath as chips. **Show as text** switches every item back to sentences.

## 0.12.16 (2026-11-05)

- **Eras:** the world's age of qi is now its era: the Era of Balance, the Era of Flourishing, the Golden Era of Qi... (world creation says "Era").
- **Qi carries over:** pills, cores and herbs you absorb, a mentor's guidance, devouring, dual cultivation and the like no longer stop at a full stage: what fills the stage climbs into the next at once, through as many stages as it fills. Only the realm's last stage holds it, at the bottleneck. (Before, a full stage waited for the next month's cultivation to climb.)
- **Guide until the bottleneck:** in Your Circle's Cultivation, beside a month's guidance, guide someone month after month until they reach their bottleneck. A long run passes behind the progress panel (Emerge stops it); it also ends if something needs you, one of you leaves, or your own years run short.
- **Every beast its own core:** each kind of beast now carries a core named for it (a Thunder Ape Core, a Frost Spider Core, a Ghostfire Fox Core...), of its realm's grade and its element, instead of a few shared ones (the Demon Wolf Core and the like are now the common cores of their element and realm, carried now and then besides a beast's own). A core of a beast's own line still purifies a tamed beast twice as much.
- **The Body path's key treasure** is now any beast's core of the realm it breaks into or stronger (a Mystic-grade core for Foundation Building, an Earth-grade one for Golden Core...): the same grades as before, from any beast of that strength.
- **Evolutions give gifts:** besides a stronger body and arts, each evolution gives something of its own, by turns along each line: attributes in the line's element (wind quickens, earth hardens, metal and fire strengthen, water and ice sharpen the spirit; more at each step), or a new nature its form suggests (the Moon-Devouring Wolf sinks into shadow, a void form steps through space, a sun form blazes, the Black Tortoise is massive, a dragon regenerates...). What the next evolution gives shows on the beast's page and, playing a beast, on the Character tab.
- **Smaller place cards:** the Location tab's cards are smaller (24-pixel icons, a smaller hand), so a row holds more of them. Beast cards show only the beast's name; the rest is in their tooltip.

## 0.12.15 (2026-11-05)

- **Places as boards of cards:** the Location tab's actions are now cards, each with its own pixel icon (a mountain to explore, a herb to gather, a paw to hunt, a coin for the merchant and the auction, crossed swords for the fighting pit and the tournament, a sect gate, a crown for the throne, a scroll for lectures and the request board, a portal to delve...), gathered under their kind of business (Seek and gather, In town, Trade, Contests, Sects, thrones and land, Ancients and other worlds), each kind with its icon. A card you cannot use now is dimmed, and says why when you hover (or hold) it. The parts of the place below (Here, Market, Home, People, News) carry icons too.
- **What lives and grows here:** in the wilds, the cards show every beast that roams there (its icon in the colour of its element, the realm it starts at; a card asks before two months on its trail) and every kind of material that grows or lies there (herbs, ore, wood, jade...; a card opens the choice of which to seek).
- **Show as a list** above the cards switches back to the rows of buttons, and the game remembers it.

## 0.12.14 (2026-11-05)

- **Your character, drawn:** the Character tab now shows its numbers as pictures.
  - **Attributes** are bars out of 100, coloured as before (gold fine, jade good, red poor), with a line at 50 (ordinary); what your methods add shows in violet and what your gear adds in gold, each its own stretch.
  - **Your road through the realms:** each path has a track of its realms, in its own names (Skin Tempering on the Body path): jade behind you, gold where you stand, and the stretch to the next realm cut into this realm's stages and filled with the qi you have gathered. Hover a realm for where you stand in it.
  - **The next breakthrough:** the quality you would form sits on a band of the four grades (Flawed, Common, Perfect, Heavenly) with a pointer where you stand, and each thing that shapes it (dao foundation, the place's qi, the chamber, the key treasure, the minigame, your deepest dao, luck, dao heart...) is a bar of how much of it you have.
  - **Path harmonies** show their level as five pips, with what they give.
- **Show as text** beside the Character tab's sections switches the Overview, Cultivation and Health sections back to words alone (the body diagram too), and the game remembers it.

## 0.12.13 (2026-11-05)

- **Pills at a glance:** in the Inventory, what a pill would do for you is now drawn as bars: one for each thing it moves (qi through this stage, health, breakthrough aid out of 30%, lifespan, an attribute, dao foundation, the toxins you carry with the line where they begin to bite, and the meridians a too-strong pill would tear), each from where it stands now to where it would stand, gains in jade and harm in red. What shapes the pill (its grade against your realm, its element against your root, tolerance, old toxins) sits beneath as chips. Hover (or hold) any bar or chip for the full sentence. **Show as text** beside "For you" switches every pill back to the sentences, and the game remembers it.
- **Fixed:** the preview of a breakthrough pill above your realm promised its full aid, though the body takes in only part of a too-strong pill (as it does for every other effect).

## 0.12.12 (2026-11-05)

- **Body diagrams:** in battle each fighter's body is now drawn instead of listed, in two views: the outer flesh (what can be struck) and the inner body (organs, bones, meridians, the dantian and the sea of consciousness, inside a faint outline of the flesh). Every part is coloured by its state as the list was: jade whole, gold hurt, red badly hurt, bright red destroyed, grey and crossed out when lost, bronze where a prosthetic stands in; a broken bone shows its crack. People are drawn from the front (their left on the left), beasts from the side, spiders from above and birds with their wings spread: every body plan has its own picture.
- **Aim by clicking the foe's body:** click a part of your foe's diagram to aim there (a reticle marks it, and the aim list beside Strike follows), and click it again to aim anywhere. Hover a part (or hold it on a touch screen) for its state, what it does, whether it is vital, and how often a blow aimed there lands. A known weak spot is starred.
- **Show as a list:** the old list is one click away under each fighter's chips, and the game remembers your choice.
- **A beast's nature as chips:** shelled, elusive and the other beast natures now sit with the status chips (hover for what they mean) instead of filling the column with text.
- **Your body on the Character tab:** the Health section opens with the same diagram of your own body.

## 0.12.11 (2026-11-05)

- **Tooltips within tooltips:** every tooltip now links the terms it mentions (momentum, barrier, bottleneck, dao foundation, path harmonies, the five phases, grades...), in gold. Rest the mouse on one and its own tooltip opens beside the first, with gold words of its own, as deep as you care to go; the tooltips stay while the mouse is in them and close when it leaves (or with a click elsewhere). A tooltip with nothing to link closes as soon as the mouse leaves, as before. On a touch screen, press and hold for a tooltip as before, then tap a gold word in it.
- **A glossary:** 85 terms of battle, arts, the body, cultivation, attributes and items, each explained once with its numbers (how much softer a guard makes blows, how momentum builds, what each art trait does, what each path harmony gives per level). The Guide has a Glossary topic listing them all, and every Guide topic links the terms it mentions: rest on one (or tap it) for what it means.

## 0.12.10 (2026-11-05)

- **Guards take hold in time:** guards, barriers, shields and evasive footwork (blur, afterimage, flicker...) now take hold before any blow of the round, then everyone acts, the quickest first. Before, a slower fighter's guard went up after the faster foe had already struck, so it seemed to do nothing; the plain Guard button did nothing at all against a faster foe, since it lasted only the round it was raised in. Stepping in or away still moves in its turn.
- **Defence says what it did:** raising a barrier or a guard says how much softer blows will land and for how long ("blows land 31% softer for 3 rounds"); footwork says how much less likely blows are to land. A blow that lands on a raised guard says how much it took ("the earth barrier blunts the blow, about 31% softer"), a shield says what share it soaked, and a miss the blur alone caused says so.
- **Rounds in the log:** each round starts under a heading of its own with who acts first ("Round 3: Li Wei is quicker and acts first"; in a crowd, the quickest few in order).
- **Status chips:** what each fighter is under (barrier, blur, afterimage, burning, venom, echo, momentum, sundered, withered, sealed, bleeding...) shows as coloured chips with the rounds left, under each fighter. Hover one (or hold it on a touch screen) for what it does in numbers: how much a burn eats each round, how much harder momentum makes blows, where an echo will strike again. Sundered, withered, dread, echo and soul settling were not shown at all before.
- **The battle fills the window:** the battle screen grows with the window instead of staying a fixed box, and the fighters' body lists take the full height.
- **Advice:** turn on Advice [H] in battle and each round it suggests an action and says why (slip a gathered blow, raise a barrier against a harder hitter, bait out an afterimage with a feint, the art whose element overcomes theirs...), and stars its button. Do it takes it.
- **Auto-fight, your way:** Auto: one round [D] lets your fighter take a single round; Auto-fight now stops and hands the fight back when a heavy blow lands on you, when you are maimed (a part lost or destroyed, a bone broken, an organ struck) or when your health falls below a line (30, 50 or 70%), each chosen under Stopping rules.

## 0.12.7 (2026-11-05)

- **Items above your realm give little:** gear, talismans, puppets and formation disks work in full up to your realm's grade, and only 45% for each grade beyond it. Gear one grade above is still a little better than gear of your own grade, but an Immortal-grade spear in a Qi Condensation hand gives about a tenth of its might. (Before, gear above your realm was up to two and a half times stronger, and talismans, puppets and formations ignored your realm entirely.)
- **Pills above your realm hurt:** a pill of a higher grade than your realm can bear is only partly taken in (55% one grade over, about a third of that two grades over) and tears at your meridians as you take it. A hardy body bears it better: high constitution, a tough physique and the Body path. The pill for the next realm's breakthrough is safe to take. The pill's details warn you first, and the world's cultivators no longer swallow such pills.
- **Gear's attributes count:** equipment that says "+ agility" (wind and swift materials) or "+ Strength" and the like (fire and lightning worked into it) now adds to your attributes, shown on the Character page as "(+N gear)", and used everywhere the attribute is. Before, agility from gear counted only for speed in battle, and attribute boosts on gear did nothing at all. Like other worn properties, many pieces stack with diminishing returns, and gear above your realm gives less.
- **Fixed: refining a worked material broke the Professions tab** (since 0.12.3), and the same bad material could blank the whole inventory, so new crafts seemed to vanish. Smelting an ingot again (or extracting an extract) made a material whose recipe the game could not read back. Those materials now load again, a material that still cannot be read shows as a spoiled lump instead of breaking the page, and a worked material can no longer be worked again (it only piled concentration on concentration). Extracts of bred herbs, which hit the same bug, work again.

## 0.12.6 (2026-11-05)

- **Sect ranks are earned:** in a sect you no longer rise by realm alone. You join as an outer disciple (a Foundation Building cultivator as an inner disciple, a Golden Core or greater as a core disciple), and to rise you take the promotion trial in the Hall of Trials (Sect tab, at the sect's home). It asks a realm, standing with the sect and an offering of contribution, then sends examiners of the rank you seek: win every bout (spars) and you rise. Inner disciple: Qi Condensation stage 4, standing 10, 100 contribution, 3 bouts; core disciple: Foundation Building, 30, 400, 3; elder: Golden Core, 50, 1,200, 2; grand elder: Nascent Soul, 70, 3,000, 1. Lose and you may try again next year. The grand tournament's champion rises a rank without a trial. Clans keep their old way (seniority by realm and blood). Ranks you already hold are kept.
- **The library and the treasury open rank by rank:** each rank reads arts and draws treasures up to its grade (outer Yellow, inner Mystic, core Earth, elder Heaven, grand elder Immortal, the leader everything). Locked entries say "Higher rank".
- **Ask for the leader's seat:** an elder with standing 50 can ask the sect master for the sect. One who thinks well of you and is old, or outmatched by you, hands you the seal and steps aside as the sect's ancestor; otherwise they make you take it in a duel before the elders. Lose and you wait three years.
- **Guest elders:** a Golden Core cultivator or greater can become a guest elder of a sect without joining it (at its gate, Location tab), and of several sects at once. A guest elder draws a yearly stipend and may study the library (up to Earth grade at Golden Core, Heaven at Nascent Soul, Immortal beyond) for spirit stones. Take the same seat with a sect's enemy (at war, or bitterly hostile), or join its enemy, and it strikes you from its guest elders and despises you; the button warns you first.
- **A spirit beast's den:** a beast can make its den in the wilds (Location tab, Home): a year of digging, no stones. Its spirit spring, hunting grounds, lair defences, deep hollow for breakthroughs and hidden entrance grow with beast cores instead of stones (2 cores at level 0, one more and a grade higher for each level), a month each.
- **A beast played now evolves:** only tamed beasts evolved before. Playing as a beast, you take the next form of your line (Bloodfang Wolf → Heavenly Wolf → Moon-Devouring Wolf...) when your realm and bloodline purity are enough, at a breakthrough, after purifying your blood or after devouring your own kind. An evolved beast stays of its line: its kin, mates, traits and blood still know it. The Character tab says what you can evolve into next and what it needs.
- **Only breakthrough pills steady a breakthrough:** during the breakthrough minigame you can swallow only pills made for it (breakthrough aid is their chief effect), not a qi or healing pill that happens to carry a little breakthrough aid. The finest grade is swallowed first.
- **Heaven-grade treasures are hard to come by:** materials of Heaven grade and above (three grades higher above the clouds) are found far less often by divine sense (at most half the time at Heaven grade, a third above it), one at a time (rarely two), and most are guarded: a spirit beast that has slept beside it for centuries, at the peak of its realm, or a rival cultivator who sensed it at the same moment. Fight for it or leave it.
- **Armour pieces no longer stack without limit:** a full suit and six accessories of hard ore made a fighter 11 to 40 times harder to hurt. The pieces now stack with diminishing returns (the strongest whole, each further one three quarters of the last), and armour and accessories add together instead of multiplying: a full set guards about one and a half times as well as a chest piece alone. Speed, technique power and soul resistance from many pieces stack the same way. A chest piece and pendant guard about a fifth less than before.
- **Defence and movement arts can be composed by hand,** and have new moves. Defence: Iron Bastion (a guard twice as firm, for two rounds), Mirror Ward (a thin shield and a counterstrike), Flowing Deflection (a light guard for four rounds and more). Movement: Flicker Step (far harder to hit, two rounds), Circling Step (harder to hit, and your next blow is truer), Phantom Steps (an afterimage, and a blur behind it). In the composition minigame they chain by height, momentum and yin and yang like attack traits, and a perfect form is possible (Barrier → Mirror Ward → Gather → Iron Bastion; Blur → Step Away → Gather → Afterimage). The moves you compose open early (mastery 20, 40, 60); arts not composed by hand get the new moves late.
- **Reincarnate by points:** a soul that can be reborn can now choose its next life point by point, with points its past life earned: 6 for any soul strong enough, its realm (Golden Core 6, four more each realm beyond), fame, destinies, karma (bad karma takes points away), its deepest dao and any sect or clan it founded. A strong life earns more than the 20 a new soul gets. Rolling the new life is still there.
- **Export and import saves:** Load a Journey has Export beside each journey and an Import a Save button, using the system's file picker (on Android too), to move a journey between machines. An imported save is a journey of its own; nothing is overwritten.
- **Hide the dead in your family:** the Family tab's tree and the Relations tab's family list each have a Hide the dead toggle (the living below a dead ancestor stay).
- **Android: tap twice, screen by screen:** with Tap twice to act on, Settings now lists each screen (each tab, the side bar, battle, minigames, other windows, the menus) to turn it off for just that one.
- **Android: the fate screens scroll and fit a phone held upright:** the choice of how fate is decided (Roll, Choose, Spirit beast) was cut off at the side and could not scroll, and the creation screen's three columns did not fit. Both now scroll, and on a phone held upright the cards and columns stack.

## 0.12.5 (2026-11-05)

- **Faster on phones:** the Android builds are now optimized release builds (they were debug builds: unoptimized code on a slower engine). They install over the previous version as before.
- **Pages draw faster:** every tab is built off-screen and put in place at once, instead of row by row. A full bag drew about five times faster to build on a test PC, the Professions, Arts and Records tabs three to six times faster.
- **A full bag shows ten of a kind at a time:** each section (pills, armour, formation disks...) shows its first ten, with "Show more" for the rest. A search now finds things in every section, folded or not, and shows only what matches.
- **Less work each month:** checking whether something is equipped no longer searches the whole bag once per gear slot (a bag of a thousand things made every page and every sale slow), and people far away gather their method's traits only when their months are lived.
- **The log notes slow pages,** so a laggy phone's log (Settings) says where the time goes.

## 0.12.4 (2026-11-05)

- **The people of the world trade their spoils:** gear won and never worn no longer piles up (one peak Deity Transformation cultivator carried 50 weapons, 60 armours and 70 accessories). Once a year NPCs keep what they wear and a few of the best spares, and part with the rest. Sect members send the finer pieces home to their sect's storehouse for contribution (until it is full; the greedy keep the price for themselves). The rest is sold where they are: first to a city shop that deals in it, where it goes on the shelves for you and others to buy, then to the town's merchants, as far as their purse goes. Every stone comes out of a till or a purse, so the economy stays balanced; what no one can pay for waits for a richer town. Hoards in older saves sell off over the next few years.
- **Old accessories are worn as their names say:** hairpins, rings and bracelets made before accessories had slots all counted as waist pendants. They (and the designs they came from) now take the slot their name says.
- **Pill tolerance, gift by gift:** a pill's lasting gifts (each attribute, foundation, lifespan) were all dulled by whichever of them you had taken most, so foundation pills dulled the strength of a strength-and-foundation pill. Each gift now answers only to its own tolerance, and the pill's details list each one.
- **Attributes from pills come slowly past 10 a realm:** pills give an attribute in full up to 10 points for each major realm (20 at Foundation Building), then at a third, halving again every 5 points, like dao insight and crafts. Breaking through opens the way again. A pill's details say when it is slowed. Attributes you were born with do not count.
- **The battle bag folds and searches:** its weapons, pills, talismans, formation disks, puppets and poisons fold into sections (a small bag starts open, a full one folded), and a search box finds anything in it.
- **Seek a harder fate:** Seek a fate can ask for no spiritual root (Body or Soul path), a Pseudo root, an ordinary body, no beast blood, no battle talent, and low attributes (40, 30 or 20 or less, for any or every attribute).
- **The game keeps a log on phones too,** and Settings can copy it ("Copy the last run's log" after a crash) to paste into a message. It notes the device, the screen, and each battle's foes, rounds and screen turns.
- **Android for older phones:** a separate 32-bit Android download, for phones that cannot run the regular one.

## 0.12.3 (2026-11-04)

- **Waylay travellers:** a new action in the wilds and on the roads (Location tab, beside Hunt beasts). Spend a month lying in wait, and usually someone passes: a lone cultivator or a merchant with a heavy pouch, most a little weaker than you, but now and then stronger than their aura let on. Demand their valuables (the stronger you look, the likelier they give in; merchants and those who know your dreaded name give in more easily) and take half their stones and their two best things, or strike from hiding and fight. Travellers are strangers new to the world, so robbing them does not use up its people. It costs karma and infamy, a victim who lives puts a price on your head and may come back for revenge, and killing one is murder. More travellers pass near towns than in the deep wilds.
- **The bag's material filter** lists worked kinds too (ingots, extracts, timber, plates), each right after the raw kind it is made from.
- **Worked materials in the crafting designer:** ingots, extracts, timber, jade plates, paper and ink you carry or know the recipe for now appear among the materials you can design with, marked with what they are and the special nature they draw out. A new filter shows raw and worked materials, raw only, or worked only, alongside the kind, element, aspect and owned filters. (Designs calling for a raw material already took a worked one of the same kind and grade or better.)
- **Worked materials are their own type:** ingots, extracts, timber and plates are called that everywhere (in the bag, the designer's kind filter and its labels) instead of ore, herb, wood and jade. They still count as their raw kind in recipes: a design needing ore takes an ingot ("needs at least: Ore or Ingot").
- **The bag folds its materials away:** the sections of herbs, ores, ingots, inks and the rest start folded and open when you click them (a search still finds everything). Ingots, extracts and other worked materials get sections of their own.
- **Weapon spirit bond progress:** every weapon shows its bond and what it needs next ("bond 12/30 to awaken a spirit (needs 60% nurture, now 41%)", "bond 95/150 to level 4", "fully grown"), each victory tells you how much the bond grew, and feeding the spirit shows where it stands.

## 0.12.2 (2026-11-04)

- **Arts grow in battle:** every art you use in a fight you survive (attacks, barriers, footwork, healing) gains mastery until it is perfected: half a point a use, up to 3 a battle, slower as it nears perfection and faster for arts that suit you, like practice. Milestones reached in battle are announced. The people of the world learn from their fights the same way.

## 0.12.1 (2026-11-04)

**Worlds remember everything**
- **A new character begins in a world as your latest journey there left it.** Begin here (in Choose a World) no longer starts the world fresh: the sects your earlier characters founded, the towns they raised, the people they killed, their feuds and where every spirit stone went all stand. The character you played before lives on in the world as one of its people, with all they had. The world list shows how many lives have been lived in each world.
- **Begin as forged** still starts a world fresh, with only relics of your earlier lives (inheritances, tombs, those who ascended, legends), as before.
- **Travelling to another world** arrives in it as its own latest journey left it.

**The ancients' vengeance**
- **Their rage falls on your line, not on you in every playthrough.** Raising a hand against an ancient angers them at you, your descendants, your close kin and your clan, and it stays in the world where it happened (they no longer remember you across playthroughs or worlds; the Settings button to make them forget is gone).
- **Below the clouds, they come for your clan:** an avatar falls on its estate, killing some of its people, burning a quarter of its treasury and breaking a hall. With no clan, they strike your kin; with neither, they curse you as before. If you are at your clan's home when they come, you stand between them.
- **Above the clouds, they come in their true body:** anyone of your line who ascends (you, your child, a clansman) is hunted by the ancient itself, at the peak of Golden Immortal and far stronger than any avatar. Only a peak Golden Immortal of great strength wins about one fight in three. Drive it off and their rage is spent; at rage 3 or more it fights to kill. One of your line living on in the world fights it alone, and most do not survive.
- Rage at someone else's line is not your affair: the ancient still sets you tasks. A line with no one left is forgotten. Appeasing them works as before, and older saves' rage falls on the line being played.

## 0.12.0 (2026-11-04)

**Spirit stones pass from hand to hand**
- **The world's stones are truly finite now.** Below the clouds no stone is made from nothing or lost to nothing: every stone someone gains, someone else has lost. Before, the "fixed supply" was only bookkeeping: shop customers, wages, bounties and market sales made stones from thin air, and within twenty years of play the earth ran dry, so sect, clan, tax and vein incomes fell to a tenth for the rest of the game.
- **Every town has a merchants' purse.** The Location tab shows how flush a city or town's merchants are. They pay for what you sell, your wages, commissions, auctions, bounties and pit purses; they take in what you and everyone else spend on wares, services and building; they pay taxes and tribute to their lords. A drained town pays less (down to a third), then nothing: **go and sell somewhere else.** A town grows rich again as cultivators spend there and the townsfolk dig stones from the hills.
- **Selling is no longer bottomless.** The market stall, the beast pens, trade goods and the grand auctions buy only what the town's purse can pay for. **Your own shop** sells only to a town with stones to spend, and dear wares find buyers slowly (the chance of a sale falls with grade).
- **Manuals:** writing one now takes a piece of jade of the art's grade or better (a lesser jade cannot hold the art), and copies of the same art lose a fifth of their price for each one already sold in that town this year.
- **Shops:** the townsfolk buy only yellow and green wares, out of their town's purse. A new shop's till is put up by its sect or keeper, and its stock is bought from the town's merchants.
- **Spirit veins hold real deposits** that their holders draw on; a vein run dry yields nothing until the earth refills it. What cultivation absorbs, arrays burn and seals consume sinks back into the earth, and each year part of the earth rises again into the veins and the hills.
- **The dead pass on what they had:** to their children (or companion), else to their sect, else to whoever finds the body. A great cultivator who dies alone is laid in a **tomb** with their stones and best art, for someone to find.
- **Incomes come from somewhere:** sect offerings, clan enterprises, kingdom tribute, town taxes and holdings are paid by the towns they serve, and shrink when those towns are poor. Mission rewards are paid by the townsfolk the sect serves, tournament prizes by the treasury, a request's reward by the one who asked, a debt by the debtor, a patron's gift from their own purse. The merchants also buy what the cultivators around them hunt and gather, so wealth spreads further: in a test world the typical cultivator holds about three times what they did, and the richest tenth less of the whole.
- The Upper Realm's markets are still bound by nothing (above the clouds, prices run past what the whole world below holds).
- Older saves balance their books on loading: the merchants and veins are filled from the earth, and stones left lying with the long dead return to it.

**Fixes**
- **Soul and body methods have no element:** a soul art of ice no longer clashes with a soul method that carried a hidden fire element. Only the method of the qi path counts for clashes and affinity.

## 0.11.4 (2026-11-04)

**The shops of the cities**
- **A new Shops tab.** Every city has two to six shops: general stores and shops of one craft (pills, artifacts, talismans, arrays, puppets or herbs). Each is kept by a person of the world, with a name over the door and real wares on its shelves.
- **Their wares are made, not conjured:** a shop of a craft is stocked by its keeper's own work, at the grade their skill reaches, from the designs they know. A general store buys in a little of everything. Shelves refill month by month.
- **Sell to them too:** a general store buys most things at a market's price; a shop of a craft pays 25% more for the works of its craft, as far as its till allows.
- **Sect shops:** about a third of the shops belong to sects, kept by a disciple the sect sends down the mountain. Their takings go to the sect's treasury, and the sect's own disciples get 15% off.
- **Your sect's shops:** as a sect leader, order a shop opened in any city (2,000 stones from the treasury). You choose the kind of shop and the disciple who keeps it, and can later send another disciple or close it. Its takings above a working reserve come to the treasury each month (the keeper keeps a tenth). Keepers stay at their counters and take no other duty.
- **Shops change hands:** when a keeper dies or leaves, a grown child takes the shop on or the sect sends another disciple; otherwise it closes. New shops open in cities that are short of them.

**The world's people buy and use**
- Cultivators in a city come by its shops every month. They buy what they would put to use (a better piece of armour, a pill, a manual) and what serves their fights and crafts (a few talismans, a puppet, a poison for their blade, an array, a tool for their craft), and they use it.
- **The world's smiths forge every piece** of armour and accessory (helms, greaves, rings...), not only chest pieces and pendants, and NPCs wear them.
- **Members spend their contribution:** each year of service earns a sect member contribution (more for higher ranks, a duty that serves the sect, or keeping one of its shops), and works they give the storehouse earn it too, as a donation would. They spend it at the storehouse as you do: on what they would use (a better weapon or piece of armour first, then pills and manuals) and on what serves their fights and crafts, up to two things a year, of a grade their rank opens. Every sect works this way, a sect you lead included.
- Like wares in a shop (one pill at several qualities) fold under one row, as in your bag.

**Secret bodies, offered at once**
- Earning a secret body in any life now brings an event: the heavens shall bestow a new physique upon you. It shows your body now beside the one earned (what each is and does, and any upkeep the new one asks), and you choose to take it or keep your own. Either way, the body stays unlocked for lives to come in point buy.
- A body earned while another event is open is offered as soon as that one is settled.

## 0.11.3 (2026-11-04)

**Armour and accessories, piece by piece**
- **Choose the piece at the forge**, as you choose a weapon's form. Armour: helm, gorget, pauldrons, chest piece, war belt, vambraces, bracers, finger guards or greaves. Accessories: hairpin, earrings, necklace, bracelet, ring or waist pendant.
- **Each piece is worn in its own place:** a new helm replaces your old helm and leaves your chest piece where it is. The Inventory lists every slot and what fills it, and the Equip button says what a piece would replace.
- **The pieces add up.** The chest piece is a whole suit's worth of protection and the waist pendant a whole accessory's, as before. Each other piece is forged at a share of that (a helm 30%, greaves 30%, a war belt 25%, finger guards 10%, a ring 40%...), so the numbers it shows are what it gives. A full suit of nine pieces guards about two and a half times as well as a chest piece alone. Smaller pieces are quicker to make and sell for less.
- **Attuned pieces resist elements by their share:** a full Water suit resists Fire better than a Water robe alone, up to a limit.
- **A piece needs its part of the body:** no greaves without legs, no ring without fingers. A puppet limb will do.
- Armour and accessories from older saves are worn as the chest piece and the waist pendant, at full strength.

**The beast's road ahead**
- A beast's Road Ahead no longer asks it to find a sect, refine a natal treasure, buy at auction or build a breakthrough chamber, all of which a beast cannot do. It asks it to purify its blood (Stirring by Foundation Building, Awakened by Nascent Soul), make a den, lead a pack and find a mate. Its hints speak of hunting and devouring, and before Nascent Soul it warns of the crossroads between beast and human form.
- A beast that has earned human form is still shown a person's goals, each marked to be done in its human shape.

**Fixes**
- The scholar calling now says what it always did: its ranks give no more Comprehension once yours reaches 90.
- The Settings button that makes the ancients forget showed as an empty sliver. It now spans the window and its text shows.

## 0.11.2 (2026-11-04)

- **Everything in the bag folds like puppets:** two or more of one thing (one herb, ore, pill, talisman, weapon or anything else, of one grade) gather under one row with its total and how many of each quality, opening to each one by quality. Before, only things that cannot stack folded, so the same herb at three qualities took three rows.

## 0.11.1 (2026-11-04)

Soul arts reined in. They ended fights against an equal in four or five rounds and won nearly every one; now a soul art beside a body art wins about as often as any other pair of arts, and the Soul path keeps a modest edge.

- **Smaller bites:** a soul strike tears about 16 from an equal's sea of consciousness (it was about 24), and never more than 36 against one. Only a far greater soul still breaks a mind in one or two strikes.
- **Soul strikes can miss:** about 7 in 10 land against an equal. A stronger spirit, an awakened divine sense and a greater realm land more often; dodging footwork, a concealing art or an afterimage help to slip them. A stunned mind cannot slip one.
- **A settling between soul arts:** after each soul art (landed or not), the sea of consciousness needs two rounds before any soul art again. Other arts are free to use meanwhile; the battle screen shows the rounds left.
- **The soul alone:** soul arts no longer bruise the brain as well; they strike the sea of consciousness and nothing else.
- **Defenders' fair share:** the art itself counts for half what it did in a soul strike; the soul behind it does the rest.
- **Fewer stuns:** a soul strike stuns at most one time in four, and never twice in a row.
- **Qi shields** take up to half of a soul strike.
- Fighters of the world use a soul art as soon as it is ready (unless the foe's soul is clearly stronger), instead of almost never reaching for it at higher realms.

## 0.11.0 (2026-11-04)

Combat and the crafts, deeper.

**The body and the guard**
- **More of the body:** people have a face, ears, a nose, fingers and toes, and bones: a skull, ribs, hips and the long bones of the arms and legs. Beasts have jaws, fangs, beaks and horns, and bones in every limb and wing. Bodies from older saves gain the new parts whole (a hand lost before fingers existed has lost its fingers too).
- **Bones break:** a heavy blow carries into the bone beneath, a blunt one far more than an edge. A broken bone cripples what it holds up until it knits (half as fast as flesh): a broken arm cannot guard or grip, broken legs cannot stand or dodge. Bones can be aimed at.
- **The guard:** your arms cover your head and vitals. Against an equal, an aimed blow at the head lands far less often (about a third against 63% before); against a greater realm the guard holds less, against a lesser one more. Unaimed blows headed for the head are often caught on an arm. Break or take a foe's arms, or feint them off balance, and the head lies open. Fights are a little longer and less often fatal.
- **Legs matter:** a crippled footing makes a fighter easy to hit, and blurring footwork hides little on broken legs. Others than you go for your arms when your guard is tight and for your legs when you slip every blow, as you can.
- **Hands need fingers:** a hand without its fingers grips poorly; a prosthetic hand brings its own.
- **Skull and ribs:** a whole skull or ribcage turns much of a blow aside from the brain, heart and lungs; a broken one lets it through.

**Divine sense in battle**
- **A pool to spend:** once awakened, your divine sense is a resource in every fight, regathering a little (6%) each round. What it costs is a share of the pool, so it is the same at every realm.
- **Quick actions:** with the Quick button beside an item or weapon, take a pill, burn a talisman, plant an array, unleash a puppet, take up a weapon or snatch back a dropped one, without spending your turn. Each costs a third of the pool, one a round: two or three in a row from a full pool, then about six rounds to gather another.
- **Guided strikes [T]:** a sixth of the pool each: the strike truer, and half a guard's cover over the head and vitals no bar to it.
- **Read intent [R]:** an eighth of the pool: see what your target means to do this round (others settle on their move a round ahead).
- Others with an awakened sense do the same.

**New tactics**
- **Feint [E]:** show an opening that is not there. A foe who bites wastes their counter, afterimage, guard or gathered blow, and is left off balance (no guard, easier to hit).
- **Clashing arts:** two fighters striking each other with arts in the same round may meet head on (likelier the closer their speed): the stronger breaks through weakened, or both are spent in a burst of qi.
- **Disarmed:** a blow on the weapon hand or arm may knock the weapon to the ground. Snatch it back with a turn (or at once by divine sense). If you win, you pick it up; if you flee, it stays where it fell, marked for you to come back for.
