- **Weak spots:** slay three of a beast kind and you know where its hide is soft: easier to strike there, and deeper. The bestiary lists it.
- **Morale:** a side that loses half its fighters loses heart, and some break and run; a beast pack scatters when its leader falls; a beaten leader may lay down their arms.

**Companions, puppets and arrays**
- **Orders:** tell your companions to fight freely, strike your target, guard you (they throw themselves in front of blows), or hold back.
- **Puppets on strings:** each deployed puppet can strike where you aim, guard you, hold, or (from Journeyman puppetry) loose one of your own arts through it each round, at a share of its force that grows with your puppetry, from your own qi.
- **Killing array forms:** plant a killing array as Lacerating, Piercing, Burning, Crushing (it breaks bones), Binding, Freezing, Thunder, Resonant, Yin or Venom, and it strikes in that form every round it stands. Its force is four parts your formation skill (weaker for a disk above your realm) and one part your insight into the form's dao and the disk's element.

**Working materials**
- **Smelt, extract, season, carve:** smiths smelt ingots, alchemists draw extracts, puppeteers season timber, formation masters carve jade plates (the Professions tab, "Work materials"). Ten units in, five out, a month a batch: concentrated, and each material's special nature drawn out (a sharp metal breaks armour, a water ore drinks qi, a wood mends...), which a raw material keeps locked inside.
- **Alloys in a ratio:** work two materials together in the ratio you choose (seven parts of one to three of another): both natures, more of the larger share. What a mix comes out as is hidden until your skill is up to its grade, or you have made it once; then it is a known recipe, to make again in one click.
- **Paper and ink are made:** the wilds no longer hold them. Herbalists press herb paper, puppeteers pulp wood paper, alchemists brew blood ink from cores and herbs, smiths grind mineral ink from ore and jade. Markets still sell what others made, and what you already carry still works.

**The crafts together**
- **Crafts feed each other:** a work holding worked materials from other crafts comes out a tenth stronger for each, up to three tenths (a smith's ingots and a carved jade core in a puppet). Array flags now take talisman ink, and puppets a jade core.
- **Pills feed the fields:** work an alchemist's pills into your spirit soil: three times as rich as raw herbs.
- **Schools:** at Adept and again at Master, choose your craft's school, one of two at each step, for good: Abundant Furnace or Pure Flame, Pill Saint or Poison Sage; the Edge or the Bulwark, Spirit Smith or Heaven's Temper; Quick Brush or Deep Script, Battle Scribe or Seal Master; Killing or Guardian Arrays, Battlefield Master or Sect Architect; War Puppets or Enduring Frames, Soul Thread or Many Strings (three puppets at once); Abundant Harvest or Potent Herbs, Hybridizer or Ancient Gardens; Clear Coins or Veil Piercer, Heaven's Mercy or Far Sight.
- **Failures are not all alike:** a failed craft may still come out flawed (an impure pill that leaves twice the toxin, a brittle blade, a smudged talisman) or save half its materials; only a hopeless attempt loses everything. **Flukes:** now and then a work comes out cloud-patterned or star-veined, an effect a quarter stronger.
- **Cross strains:** from herbalism 30, cross two kinds of seed (half a year) into a new strain of herb: half of each, and a mutation of its own. Its seeds grow like any other.
- **Apprentices:** set a disciple, child or companion to work at your abode: unpaid, by their own skill and realm, and they learn the craft (and your designs) as they work.

**Guardians and secret bodies**
- **A beast to guard the mountain:** standing at your sect or clan's home, leave a companion spirit beast as its guardian (Sect tab → Guardians, or Relations → the beast; three at most). It counts double in the sect's strength, stands first in the line when the sect is attacked, fights beside you when you fight there, turns back beast tides and drinks the spirit veins to grow (more with a Spirit Vein Array). Call it back whenever you stand at the gate; a guardian whose master is gone follows whoever of the sect calls it, and if the sect falls it finds its way back to you.
- **People keep beasts:** cultivators of the world tame spirit beasts of their own, which follow them, fight beside them, and go back to the wild when their masters die.
- **Beast pens:** every city sells a few young tamed beasts each year (Location → Market), and buys yours.
- **A beast with a master:** living as a beast, a tamer may come for you (lower your head, fight, or flee), or you can bow to a cultivator of your choosing (Relations). A master explains the dao (contemplation half again as fast), fights beside you, and each year gives what a beast cannot find alone: pills, essence blood of your line, beast cores, a sliver of their insight. If your master has a sect, you can stand guard on its mountain as one of its guardian beasts. Catch up to your master's realm and you can break free.
- **Natures in the dao heart:** every trait leans the dao heart. Each year it drifts toward where its nature settles it; vices let heart demons stir in a steadier heart and give them their shape (pride, grudges, greed...), and virtues help to face them. A new guide topic, Natures, lists what each trait does.
- **Hunting styles:** each line of beasts kills its own way. The great cats go for the spine to drop their prey, then the throat; venomous lines strike wherever they land surest, and their bites now poison; boars, rhinoceros and apes break the legs, then the head; raptors take the eyes; wolves the throat. The bestiary and the battle screen tell you, once you have met them.
- **Misses leave openings:** a close blow that misses can leave its striker off balance (more so for an aimed or full-force blow, against a quicker foe), and the foe may punish it at once; a counter stance answers misses as well as hits.
- **Soul backlash:** a soul strike against a stronger soul rebounds on its caster, and a warded soul throws part of any strike back.
- **Arts by path:** the Arts tab has a tab for each path (Qi, Body, Soul) with its own arts and its own art making (weapons and fists for the Body, soul arts for the Soul, the elements for Qi), and taking up a new path lives there now.
- **New bodies with a price:** Withering Blood (wounds heal half as fast), Frail Soul (soul strikes land half again as hard), Stone Heart, Porous Meridians (fast, but poison and cold qi bite twice as deep), Glass Meridians (fast, with brittle breakthroughs and tribulations) and Burning Life (fast and strong, and short-lived).
- **Map search:** find any place by name, kind, region or the faction that holds it.
- **Settings:** reset the ancients' memory (who slew them, their rage, oaths sworn) in every world.
- **Shaping a beast's birth:** being born a spirit beast now has points of its own to spend on attributes and bloodline purity, and a nature to choose.
- **Fixed:** a pseudo root's arts clashed with its own method, since one of its five elements always fights another: an art of an element your root holds never clashes now. Point buy showed a frail body's refund as "+2 pt", which read like a cost: costs and refunds now say "costs" and "gives back", and every flawed body gives points back. Arrays set up before you built a home in the same place were left outside it, out of reach even after taking the land: they become the home's own, and existing saves gather them in.
- **Secret bodies are unlocked, not worn:** earning one no longer remakes the life that earned it. Its flesh stays as it was; the body is unlocked, and every life after can buy it in point buy. Bodies earned in older saves are unlocked as soon as you play them.

**Interface**
- **Location rows only where they help:** a place's actions are grouped under headings only in a town, city or sect gate with many of them; in the wilds and quiet places they stay one row, as before.
- **Map tiles keep their colours:** wilds are green and spirit veins blue again; the realm icon sits at a tile's lower right corner instead of covering it.
- **A new life, step by step:** first choose how your fate is decided (roll it, choose it with points, or be born a spirit beast), then that way opens its own screen in three columns: who you are, the way your fate is shaped, and the fate itself. Back returns to the choice.
- **Readable everywhere on that screen:** the seeking status (and the leanings' balance) have a line of their own, instead of being squeezed beside a button into one letter per line; the seeking choices each have a full row; a beast's lines are listed with all they are, instead of in a cut-short dropdown.
- **Seeking a fate has no limit:** it rerolls until it finds what you ask, or you press Stop (it used to give up after 50,000 fates). As it starts it tells you how rare what you ask is (about one fate in so many), and it stays smooth however long it runs.
- **Seek your attributes:** ask for every attribute at 50, 60, 70, 80 or 90 and up at once, and for any attribute on its own. Attributes you ask for are rolled again on their own until they come up (the same roll as ever, so the rest of the fate need not match at the same moment): every attribute at 80 and up is found at once, where before no fate in a lifetime of rolling would have had it.
- **Crafting folds away too:** in the Professions tab, each craft's designs and inventor sit under a heading of their own (Pill making, Forging, Talisman making, Array making, Puppet making) that folds away like Work materials.

## 0.10.6 (2026-11-04)

- **One hand is enough:** a weapon is held if any one hand can grip it, a fitted prosthetic included. Before, grip was averaged over both hands, so a single grade-1 prosthetic arm with the other arm lost left a soul banner (or any blade) "improvised".
- **A dao companion never runs off with your stones:** kin, dao companions and sworn siblings always repay a loan in time, however treacherous their nature. Before, a companion could "vanish" with a loan while still travelling at your side.
- **Tournament bouts name the winner:** "X lowers their weapon. The bout goes to X." instead of "...goes to them".
- **A beast's creation says so:** choosing to be born a spirit beast no longer shows the human village youth's origin text.
- **The time to the next stage counts your avatars:** avatars set to cultivate add their share, so the estimate no longer runs years long.
- **Herbalists and diviners in the rankings:** they no longer show "0 works". Herbalists count herbs harvested and diviners count readings (readings do not count toward the Master Artisan destiny).
- **The sect library sorts like the treasury:** highest grade first.
- **Take everything:** the abode's storehouse has a button that moves all it holds into your bag at once.
- **A tidier side bar:** Breakthrough, Quick breakthrough and Purge pill toxins appear only when they can be used.
- **Twin paths:** under the portrait, someone who walks two paths shows "Twin Paths of Qi and Flesh" (or Qi and Spirit, Flesh and Spirit), and all three show "The Three Treasures".
- **What traits do:** the Character tab's Overview explains each of your traits, and hovering a trait at character creation tells you what it does.
- **Destinies, earned and still to earn:** the Destinies section lists what you have earned first, then the rest, closest to done first.
- **Hide the dead:** the Relations tab's friends, rivals and enemies list can hide the deceased and the ascended.
- Only the Character tab's Overview shows your name, sex and age at the top. The other sections go straight to their content.
- **Time passes faster:** a year of the world takes about half as long to work out (seclusion and long journeys run faster), with the same results as before.
- **Smaller, quicker saves:** saves leave out what is worked out from the rest when loaded (an item's summary, a character's lifespan and titles), about a fifth smaller and quicker to write. Older saves load as before.
- **The Location tab in rows:** a place's actions are grouped under small headings (Seek and gather, In town, Trade, Contests, Sects, thrones and land, Ancients and other worlds), so a city's many buttons are easy to scan.
- **Ten tabs:** History now shares the Chronicle tab, Rankings the Records tab, and the Family tree the Relations tab. A row above the page switches between them, and each tab button returns to the view you last had open. Every tab has a key again (F1-F10).
- **One divine sense menu:** the side bar's Divine sense button [V] holds the sweep, seeking one treasure here by name (it used to sit inside Gather), and a way to Divination.

## 0.10.5 (2026-11-03)

- **A beast lives like a beast:** in beast shape, the things of people are closed to you. Towns bar their doors: no market, shop, teahouse, physician, work, request board, guards, auctions, merchants, fortune-teller, lectures, festivals, mortal callings, masks or adopting. No sect gates, sect assaults, immortal sects, thrones or preceptorships, land claims or seizures, arrays, inheritance trials, tombs, seals or fallen sects' vaults, and no audiences with the ancients (their avatars can still be fought). You carve no abode; one you made as a person is still your den for seclusion and your hoard. The Sect and Professions tabs wait for your human shape, as do dao companions, dual cultivation, masks, prosthetics, founding a bloodline, writing manuals and teaching. With people you can only look them over, part ways or attack.
- **Paws hold no blade:** a beast cannot wear gear, read a jade slip, fit a prosthetic, plant an array, coat a weapon, sell, donate, study, mend or plant. It still eats pills, absorbs materials, purifies its blood with cores and refines its own line's essence blood. It no longer tames other beasts.
- **A beast's arts are its body:** claw, fang and roar arts were treated as "improvised" without a weapon in hand, losing 35% of their force. Every beast, wild or played, now strikes with its full force, and no weapon carries a beast's arts. The art creation screen offers a beast only attack arts.
- The side panel calls a played beast "a wild Bloodfang Wolf" instead of "Rogue cultivator".
- **A pack and a mate:** in the wilds a beast can **call for a pack** (a month: one or two of its own kind fall in behind it and fight at its side) and **seek a mate** (a month: one of its kind and strength joins it). With a mate it can **raise young** (a year: likely a cub of its line, as pure as its parents' blood). Its kind is found far more readily where they roam, and luck helps.
- **Realm icons on the map:** every wild place (wilds, ruins, spirit veins, secret realms) now shows the icon of the realm its threats reach: a plain ring for mortal, a qi wisp, stone foundations, a golden orb, a seated nascent soul, a blazing sun, a cloud, and a radiant crown. The map's legend lists them.
- **Soul banners:** a crafted soul banner is named as one ("Myriad Ghost Banner"), not a jian. A weapon you have equipped but cannot grip (born without arms, or with them crippled) now says so in battle and in the bag, instead of claiming to be in hand while its arts are improvised.
- **Pill tolerance follows what a pill does:** the body grows tolerant of each lasting gift (lifespan, foundation, each attribute) at each grade, whatever the recipe, so changing one herb no longer gives a fresh pill at full potency. A pill of a higher grade still reaches further. Pills that were not crafted from a recipe also no longer lose their tolerance each time the game is loaded.
- **The fighting pit takes a month:** each bout, and each bet on one, is a month on the pit's card.
- **Children only when you try:** with a dao companion, the Relations tab has a toggle, "Children: as heaven wills" or "Children: only when you try for one". With the second, no child comes by chance; only a year spent hoping for one brings a child.
- **Bows and war fans:** two new weapons, and two new art forms to create. Archery arts loose qi-laden arrows from afar and pierce (Agility and Strength); with a bow in hand even a bare shot reaches a distant foe, and a poison coated on it rides the arrows. Fan arts sweep a gust of qi across every foe (Spirit and Agility). Like blade arts, each is improvised (65% force) without its own weapon in hand. Forge them at the smithy ("Form: bow", "Form: war fan").
- **A beast's pack has a limit:** it can lead three of its kind at Qi Condensation, one more each realm, at most eight, its mate and young among them.
- **Winning people over:** a new choice when you meet someone, **Spend time with them (2 months)**: walking the roads, sharing wine, talking the dao. It builds their regard well past what small talk reaches, even with cultivators far above you (the stronger they are than you, the slower they warm). At 50 they will travel with you; at 85 even an elder of another faction sets their duties aside to follow you. At 80 they are devoted: those at or below your realm run your errands even when not at your side, while those above you never run errands (only your own disciples and children do), but now and then send you a gift instead, a pill or material that suits your realm, or spirit stones.
- **A cyclical world:** in world creation, "Fate of the world's qi" has a new choice, **Cyclical**: the qi rises for centuries toward a flourishing peak, then sinks toward a withered trough, about the age the world began in, over and over (a great cycle of about 1,200 years).
- **Like things fold together:** puppets, weapons and other gear of one design no longer each take a row in the bag. They fold under one row ("Basic Wood Puppet ×5 (all) · Flawless ×1, Superior ×1, Common ×3"); open it to see each one, grouped by quality, or sell them all at once at a market. The sect treasury shows one row per design and quality.
- **Talk of something:** "Chat" is now **Talk with them...**: discuss the dao (one who knows it better lets slip a sliver of their insight), ask about their life (their sect, master, companion and what they want), trade rumours of the road (where a treasure stirs, where one of the strong was last seen, when a secret realm opens), praise their cultivation, talk of treasures, speak of your hardships, or share a jest. Each answers in their own words and lands by who they are: the arrogant love praise the honourable see through, the compassionate hear out what the ruthless despise. One talk a month with each person.
- **Children of one nature:** a couple of the same sex can have children when one of them holds yin and yang in balance within (20 insight in both the Dao of Yin and the Dao of Yang) or has reached Nascent Soul, whose soul can shape new flesh. Matchmaking now lists people of either sex (with whether children could come said beside them) and has a filter: men and women, men, or women. Arranged marriages for your children may now be of either sex too.
- **Seek a person by the thread of karma:** the divination "Seek a person" now needs skill 10 (was 25), and the stronger your bond, in love or in hate, the clearer it reads and the more it pierces their veil: opinion 100 or -100 leads far better than 25 or -25. The list shows each thread's strength. To find who killed someone, "Trace a death" (skill 30) names the killer and where they are now.
- **Crafting tells you why:** recording a design that cannot be made (a mystic material beyond your skill, a missing kind of material) now explains it in a popup instead of failing silently, and so does a craft that cannot begin. While inventing, what the materials would make (its grade, element and effects) stays pinned beneath the list, in view as you scroll the ingredients, with its own Record button.
- **More names:** places, sects, secret realms and worlds draw on far larger lists, and on new patterns ("Valley of Weeping Ghosts", "Mount Kunlun", "Dragon Harbour", "Seven Star Mirage Realm"), so worlds feel less alike and fewer places end in a number.
- **Composing an art (a minigame):** an attack art can now be composed by hand ("Compose it yourself" in the Arts tab), in two stages. **The qi path:** lead the art's qi from your dantian up through a grid of meridians to its release point; meridians of the art's own element strengthen it, those that feed its element steady it, those that overcome it shake it, and torn ones block the way (you can draw the qi back a step, at a cost). The path decides how much qi the art can carry. **The form:** choose its four movements, in order, within that qi. Whether one flows into the next is read from what each movement is (high, middle or low; gathering or releasing; yin or yang), never from a fixed list of pairs, so many chains flow and no art need be like another. Any kind of movement can be chosen: those natural to the form cost their usual qi, foreign ones a qi more. **The traits you choose become the art's moves**, replacing the pair its form used to hand it. The score is shown as you go (the path 35%, the form's flow 45%, variety 10%, the art's own movement 10%, less 8% for each qi over the budget), and a high one makes the art up to a quarter finer (a low one, a quarter coarser). A neutral art draws strength from meridians of pure qi. A good composition makes the art likelier to come together and finer. A fluent art strikes up to 15% harder, but from its third use in one fight a foe begins to read it (up to 30% weaker); an art whose form breaks its own flow gains nothing and is never read. "Let it come by instinct" still creates an art without the minigame.
- **Tap twice on touch screens:** on phones and tablets a button now acts on a second tap within three seconds ("Tap again: ..."), so a finger that brushes a button while scrolling does nothing. Folding headers and pickers still act at once. Turn it off in Settings.


## 0.10.4 (2026-11-03)

- **A beast's spoils:** played as a beast, you now tear the cores out of what you kill: a slain beast's own core, and any beast cores your prey carried. Before, a beast took nothing from its kills, so it had no cores to purify its blood with.
- **Devouring:** devouring a beast now feeds your cultivation as much as devouring a cultivator of the same realm and stage. Before, beasts gave 40% less.

## 0.10.3 (2026-11-03)

- **Arts from a beast's own body:** the art forms a beast can create now come from the body it actually has, and the forge names them its way: its natural attack, claws or talons (saber), fangs or a beak (spear), venom for spiders and serpents, a heavy tail (fist), coils or a charge (body), wings or fins (gale), a roar, cry or hiss (sound), and its element's breath. Only beasts with hands get fist, palm and finger arts. Lose a part and you lose its form. A beast's arts are named for the part that strikes ("Crimson Claw Rending").
- **Purifying by the five phases:** refining a beast core into your blood now follows the five phases. Your own kind's core is kin blood (×3). Your own element flows straight in (×2.2, a variant of it ×1.8). An element that feeds yours nourishes it (×1.6), and one yours overcomes is mastered (×1.1). One your element feeds drains you (×0.7). One that overcomes yours fights your blood (×0.3) and tears at your meridians. Higher grades give far more, up to two grades above your realm's own, and the purer your blood, the less each core adds. The core list shows what each would give and why.

## 0.10.2 (2026-11-03)

- **Shift at will:** a beast that has won human form can now take either shape whenever it likes, from the Character tab. Each shape keeps its own body, wounds and names, while the soul (meridians, dantian, sea of consciousness) is shared. In beast shape you live as a beast again: your blood works twice as strongly, and you cultivate by instinct.
- **A beast's life:** people's affairs no longer find a beast. No merchants or auctions, proposals, wounded strangers, sect summons and tournaments, wandering masters, armies, caravans, quests, remnant souls or heaven-and-earth treasures. They find you again in human shape. What still befalls a beast is told its way: a rival beast contests your hunting grounds, beast hunters with nets and talismans come for your core (there is no toll to pay), bounty hunters come for your hide, and a beast finds no jade slips in caves.
- **Your own blood only:** a beast (in either shape) can no longer refine another beast's essence blood. Only vials of its own line deepen it.

## 0.10.1 (2026-11-03)

- **Born of any line:** a beast life can now begin as the hatchling of every kind of spirit beast, not only the four of Qi Condensation: flood dragons, golden crows, phoenixes, the kunpeng and the rest. A great line ripens slowly: it cultivates a little slower for each realm its kind stands above Qi Condensation (down to half), until it reaches that realm, and it is born without a great core of its own yet. The beast list says how slowly each grows.
- **Changelog:** the main menu has a Changelog button that lists what changed in each build.

## 0.10.0 (2026-11-03)

- **Be born a spirit beast:** a new way to begin. Choose a young spirit beast's kind and live as it. You cannot craft, join a sect or clan, trade in towns, or make any art but those of your own body (claws and fangs, hide, a roar, your element's breath). You cultivate at half the pace by instinct and grow by devouring what you kill (no karma or infamy: it is a beast's nature), and devouring your own kind thickens your blood. Purify your blood with beast cores from the Character tab (your own kind's or element's do far more); your bloodline gives you twice what it would a person. The dao comes slower to a beast, except the dao its blood is steeped in and its own element.
- **Beast or man:** at Nascent Soul a beast chooses: remain a beast, or face a harsher tribulation (8 bolts instead of 6, and fiercer) to take human form, after which every road a person walks is open. Fail it and you stay a beast, hurt, and may try again from the Character tab.
- **The guide** has new topics: shaping your fate, playing a beast, land, sects and hidden abodes, demonic and righteous bodies, and walking below in an avatar.

## 0.9.35 (2026-11-03)

- **Walk the world below in your avatar:** from above the clouds, an avatar you have sent down can now be ridden ("Walk the world below in it" in the Avatars menu). You play it yourself, as yourself: every art at your own mastery, every dao, your name, karma and bonds. But the lower world presses you down to Deity Transformation, and the avatar sets out with an empty bag. Your main body sits in seclusion above. Return whenever you like (the Avatars button becomes "Return to your main body"), bringing back everything the avatar gained. If the avatar is destroyed, your soul snaps back with a cracked sea of consciousness, and the avatar and what it carried are lost.

## 0.9.34 (2026-11-03)

- **Room for more members:** the Sect tab now says how many members your faction can support and why (15 for a sect, more with prestige; 30 for a clan). A new building, Disciple Dwellings, adds 8 more per level.
- **Sect policies:** as leader, set whom the gate takes (anyone, or a root of some grade or better), the faction's creed (righteous, neutral or demonic: changing it costs a fifth of its prestige, and some members leave), and a yearly tithe on members' spirit stones (above 10% they resent it).
- **Territory:** the Sect tab lists the land your faction holds and the unclaimed land bordering it. Claim it (three months and some treasury) and every holding pays the treasury each year. "Claim this land" also appears in the Location tab.
- **Seize land:** at a faction's holding, call out its guards and beat them to take it: for your own faction, or, alone, as land held under your protection. Factions leave protected land be unless they think themselves half again as strong as you, or already hate you. The faction you took it from will hate you.
- **Hidden abodes:** you can build an abode on another faction's land. It is hidden: each year they may find it (a concealment array, concealment arts and divine sense hide it better). Then you fight for it. Win, and the whole faction hates you; stay, or move the abode to unclaimed wilds nearby with its facilities. These searches can be muted with "Don't stop me for this again".
- **Unfold your inner world:** once your inner world is grown to its full height below the clouds (level 9), unfold it into the world: six months to a year of seclusion, and it becomes a real place beside you, shaped by what it was fed and the daos in its laws (a caldera, a lake of springs, ore-veined peaks, storm-swept highlands, an ancient forest, or a pocket of folded space), held under your protection. Everyone inside steps out onto it. Your inner world is that land now, and you can fold a new one and grow it again.

## 0.9.33 (2026-11-03)

- **More deep hunting grounds:** every world below now holds at least three Nascent Soul wilds (more in larger worlds) and three Deity Transformation wilds besides the beast lairs and forbidden lands. Older worlds get them on load. Threats are also told in your own path's terms ("Golden Core (Golden Blood on your Body path)").
- **The element you fight by:** your element is no longer just your root's strongest affinity. It weighs your root, your Qi method and the attack arts you practise most, so a water method and mastered ice arts outweigh a metal affinity a point stronger. Pills still answer to your root.
- **Hunt a particular beast:** in the wilds, choose a beast that roams there and spend two months tracking it (sharp divine sense and luck find it sooner; rare beasts are harder).
- **Bestiary:** the Records tab lists every kind of spirit beast: those you have met and slain, how rare each is, how many of note live in the world, and the blood they carry. Kinds you have never met are only rumours.
- **Bloodline progress:** the sheet shows your bloodline's next stage, how much purity it needs, and what it will give.
- **Known for:** the Location tab and the map say what a place is known for: the sect whose seat it is, the capital or former seat of a fallen dynasty, the ruins of a fallen sect, its famous masters of each craft, its grand auction.
- **Tidier bag:** sorted by kind, the inventory is split into sections (weapons, pills, and materials by kind: herbs, ores, jade, wood...) that fold away with a click.
- **Every gift matters:** each art is now mastered and made with Comprehension and its own attribute: blade arts with Strength and Agility, fist and body arts with Strength, footwork with Agility, guards with Constitution, qi and soul arts with Spirit. Blade arts also draw a third of their force from Agility. A known art says which attribute it uses.
- **Mastered minigames:** after five strong plays of a minigame, its quick way (quick craft, quick practice, or letting insight come in contemplation) plays near your best instead of costing much.
- **The Upper Realm:** many more immortals live there; the Sect tab above the clouds lists only the factions you can reach (no more sects of the world below); a sect you found above the clouds now does its yearly duties; and the map can look down on the world below.
- **Phones:** held upright, a phone now stacks the hub (you on top, the tab in the middle, time and actions below). Tablets and PCs are unchanged. Pinch to zoom the map with two fingers.

## 0.9.32 (2026-11-03)

- **Bodies for the demonic path:** four new physiques that can be born: the Blood Demon Body and Nether Corpse Body (devouring with a demonic method yields 75% more) and the Heavenly Devil Heart and Myriad Soul Vessel (+25% cultivation with a demonic method; the Devil Heart's demonic methods wear the foundation half as fast and breed no heart demons).
- **Cauldron Harvest:** on a demonic method, the Yin-Yang Harmony trait becomes Cauldron Harvest. Dual cultivation then draws far more for you (three times the shared gain, more with a stronger trait) and nothing for your partner, who loses a fifth of their lifespan each time: three to five harvests kill them. The button says how many years it will cost them and asks first. Your karma darkens, and a partner who has felt it will not lie down again unless they still love you past reason. Demonic NPCs do it to their own companions.
- **Sects that fall count:** a sect crushed in a war you wage counts toward what you earn as well as one you storm yourself.

## 0.9.31 (2026-11-03)

- **Choose your fate (point buy):** a new way to make a life: 20 points to spend on every part of it. A heavenly root, a legendary body, high attributes, a great beast bloodline and battle talent cost points; hardships give them back (no root, a frail body, low attributes, being born without a limb, blind, or limbless and blind). Choose the root's elements and purity too. Secret bodies cannot be bought.
- **Fate's leanings:** when rolling, lean fate toward what you want by leaning away from something else (they must balance). Leaning toward a root, a body, a bloodline or battle talent takes the best of several rolls, away from it the worst; an attribute's roll moves 3 a point. With no leanings, fate rolls exactly as before.
- **Seek a fate:** choose what you want (a root grade or element, a physique or rarity, a bloodline, an attribute, battle talent, a whole body) and the game rerolls on its own until a fate has all of it, up to 50,000 fates, with your leanings and choices applied.
- **Choose your nature:** pick up to three traits (none: fate decides). Opposed traits cannot be chosen together.
- **Born limbless and blind:** a new choice for the hardest road there is: no arms, no legs, no eyes. Fate never gives it by chance.
- **Bloodlines at birth:** some lives (about 3 in 100) are now born with beast blood, as only NPC cultivators were before, mostly of the common lines and very rarely of the great ones. A parent's beast blood often passes to their children, thinner.

## 0.9.30 (2026-11-03)

- **Soul arts no longer one-shot:** a soul strike's damage now bends with the gap between souls instead of growing in a straight line. An equal foe loses about a quarter of their sea of consciousness, a soul four times weaker about half, and only an overwhelming gap (ten times or more) shatters it in one blow. A cultivator who has tempered their body on the Body path is a little steadier against them.
- **Cultivators learn to guard their souls:** from Foundation Building on, NPCs learn soul ward arts, a little more likely each realm and far more after a soul strike has hurt them; Soul cultivators always have one by Golden Core. They take it from their sect's library or the common arts, or work one out themselves.
- **Puppets last:** a puppet wears only for the rounds it actually fought (about 2 to 4 integrity each, less for a sturdy frame, at most 30 a battle), never for being carried, and fighters no longer bring one out against a foe far beneath them.
- **Woods, papers and beast cores:** every craft that uses wood or paper now has one of each element at every grade (there were only nine of each), and beasts carry a common core of their element and realm besides their own line's (half the time). Where to Find lists the beasts that carry each core.
- **Filter crafting materials by aspect:** the inventor's material list can show only hard, sharp, swift, qi... materials, and sort by the strength of that aspect.
- **Your sect only calls when it needs you:** as a sect's leader (not only its ancestor) you are called to its defence only against a champion beyond its own best or a host stronger than it without you. A new setting in the Sect tab: be called only when needed, to every battle, or never.
- **Teach your descendants from afar:** your descendants and disciples can be taught wherever they are, even in the world below while you are above the clouds, by jade slip: twice the time, and less of your mastery carries.
- **One dao foundation:** your dao foundation is now one for all your paths. Before, each path kept its own, so the sheet could show 100 while a breakthrough on your second path counted far less. Foundation pills, fortunes and failed breakthroughs all touch the one value.
- **No kin as dao companions:** sworn siblings, grandparents, uncles and aunts, cousins and second cousins can no longer become dao companions, for you or anyone (existing bonds stay). Lovers are not offered sworn brotherhood.
- **Why they said no:** a declined proposal says why (too little regard and about how much more, too many realms between you, natures that grate, a proud refusal to be anyone's second) and whether asking again can work. Being out of realm reach no longer costs regard.
- **View their sheet:** events about a person (a proposal, an offer of sworn brotherhood, a challenger) have a "View their sheet" button; the event waits and comes back.
- **Don't stop me for this again:** raids on your abode, beasts, bandits, bounty hunters, sects hunting you and your faction's calls to battle have a checkbox to never be stopped for them again. Fights are fought out for you, calls answered cautiously, and all of it told in the summary. Undo it in Settings.
- **Android:** lists with a search box no longer pop the keyboard up when they open.

## 0.9.29 (2026-11-03)

- **Colour-blind mode:** a new setting that swaps the game's meaningful colours for a palette that stays apart under red-green and blue-yellow colour blindness. Good and allies turn sky blue, bad and foes vermilion. The elements, the kinds of place on the map, battle states and body-part health are spread by brightness as well as hue, and clan and sect outlines on the map use a colour-blind-safe set. It also turns on the symbols beside the map's threat pips (still available on their own as "colour-blind markers").
- **Clearer colours for everyone:** colours that were hard to tell apart with colour blindness, and some even without it, now differ more in brightness and hue: Metal and Wind, Fire, Earth and Wood; spirit veins and secret realms, wilds and abodes on the map; jade text against plain text; Mythic and the red of danger; venom and rooting, charging and burning in battle; and sect robes.
- **Every physique in the guide:** a new guide topic lists every physique, from Uncommon to Mythic, with its description and exact effects (search finds each by name). Secret bodies stay hidden until you have earned one; then it is listed with the deed that earned it.

## 0.9.28 (2026-11-03)

- **Keep a beast in beast shape:** in Relations, bid a tamed beast keep its beast shape and it will never take human form on its own, so it can climb its line's evolutions first (the late ones need Deity Transformation and beyond). Let it go when you choose, or still help it transform whenever you like.
- **Demonic methods devour again:** a beast you meet on the road and kill now feeds a demonic method; before, only beasts that belonged to the world did, so most kills gave nothing. A demonic method on a second path (a demonic Soul method, say) devours too; it used to count only on your primary path. Beasts of every realm count, at half a person's essence. At a bottleneck the log now says why nothing came in.
- **Bounties and masks:** the Character tab shows only the bounties on the face you wear. Those on your other face show as one line saying they are hidden, and whether burning the mask lifts them (it lifts the mask's own notices, never those on your real name).
- **Killing robbers in battle costs no karma:** killing a robber, a thief or a wanted devil in battle is never a stain, whoever struck first (helping a friend drive off robbers used to count as murder). Killing anyone who has begged for mercy still costs karma: a little for a robber or someone who attacked you, more for anyone else.
- **Secret realms you can always reach:** travel to a secret realm's entrance whenever you like, sealed or not (a sealed realm may hold the seal you need to tend). Explore, Gather, Hunt, treasure searching and Delve stay closed while it is sealed or its barrier rejects your realm, and say why.
- **Yin-Yang Harmony:** a new method trait that strengthens dual cultivation: your gain in full, and half of it for your partner too.
- **Help the mortals:** in any town or city, spend a month mending, healing and carrying for the poor, for a little good karma (more for a physician), and a few more settlers if it is your own town.
- **Newest first:** the inventory can be sorted by when things came into your bag, to find the latest loot or purchase.
- **A palace in your own city:** in a town you founded or a city of your kingdom, raise a palace (3,000 stones): an abode like any other, with herb fields, workshops, arrays and seclusion, behind your own walls.
- **Faster towns:** towns grow a little faster on their own. Grant land once a year (a month) and settlers arrive at once and the town has room for 400 more. Hire a builders' guild for five years of much faster growth.
- **Shops that take commissions:** hang a profession's sign over your shop and clients leave orders sized to your skill in it: "a pill worth 20 months of cultivation", "a pill that mends 10 dao foundation and heals 100", a blade of so much force, a talisman, formation disk or puppet of their own kinds, a basket of fine herbs for a spirit farmer, a reading for a diviner (a month, and it can fail). Each pays well above market, and every order can be met by good work at its grade.
- **Soul banners:** forge a weapon as a soul banner. It is inert until you create a demonic attack art of the new Soul Banner form; then it binds the soul of everyone you kill and looses them with every strike of that art, up to double force. Its grade sets how many souls it holds (5 at Yellow, 3 more each grade). The art's grade sets the mightiest soul it can bind (never past your own realm, but one realm beyond when your Yin insight and mastery far outgrow the art). Beast souls weigh less than people's until Nascent Soul, where they are equal. A full banner lets its weakest soul go for a stronger one.
- **The heavens' edict:** if your breakthrough minigame went at least 90% well (or your chance was 90% or more), on a realm with a tribulation, and the roll still fails, you choose: accept the edict (the breakthrough fails gently, with no backlash, most of your qi kept and a calmer heart) or defy the heavens and face a crueler tribulation (half again as many bolts, 15% harder). Survive it and you break through.
- **Android:** the back gesture closes the menu on top instead of quitting the game. Menus with long lists (Calendar, Contemplate the Dao) keep their title and Cancel on screen at any interface scale, and a drag that reaches the end of a list carries on scrolling the panel around it.

## 0.9.27 (2026-11-02)

- **Devouring is for demonic arts only:** the art creator no longer lets an attack art that isn't demonic take the Devouring trait. Before, picking it in the trait list gave you devouring without the Yin insight, the karma or the infamy a demonic art costs. Tick Demonic and an attack art's trait becomes Devouring (the creator says so, and the trait you had picked no longer costs extra insight), and the expected art shown before you create it is the demonic one, with its name and power.
- **Touch scrolling on phones and tablets:** drag a finger over any list to scroll it, wherever it lands: on text, on a button, in the chronicle or the guide. Before, only a list's bare background scrolled and touching text selected it, leaving the thin scrollbar. A flick carries on a little, and a drag that starts on a button never presses it.
- **Phone layouts fit the screen:** at a phone's interface scale the right-hand column no longer runs off the screen. The line of world, era and place in the top bar now shortens with "…" (hold it to read it whole) instead of pushing everything wider. The tabs are one row you slide along, the side columns are slimmer, so the open tab has room. Settings wrap their long options and keep every slider on screen; battles, duels, the guide, Seeking a Dao Companion and your circle fit; and in a minigame "How to play" takes the board's place until you go back to the game. Key hints ([B], [H], [I]) no longer show on touch screens.

## 0.9.26 (2026-11-02)

- **A celestial phenomenon calls you out of seclusion:** when a heaven-and-earth treasure is born, your cultivation, seclusion or journey stops with the phenomenon, what kind of treasure it is, when it ripens and how far away it is (and whether you can make it in time). Set out for it at once, or stay where you are and let the strong fight it out. It always stops you, whatever your interruption setting, and so does the moment it ripens with you there.
- **NPCs use what you give them:** a gift that helps someone on the path is put to use on the spot, and the gift message says what they did. They refine a heaven treasure into their root or body (if it would raise them), study a manual (and take up a method that serves them better than their own), wear a finer weapon, armour or accessory, and take a pill when it helps: healing when hurt, breakthrough pills at a bottleneck, longevity once past half their years, never poison. Essence blood is refined if its risk suits their nerve. Herbs and cores are absorbed, but breakthrough key treasures are kept for the breakthrough itself. They treat everything they carry the same way each year, loot and treasures won included.
- **NPCs strive for ascension:** sect disciples study the best art of their library that their rank opens and they can grasp. Cultivators with stones to spare buy common manuals in towns, and one stuck at a bottleneck buys the key treasure for it at a city's rare-treasure stall. How hard they push follows their nature: the diligent and ambitious cultivate most and seek hardest, the lazy and lustful least, the greedy keep more stones back, and anyone whose years are running out redoubles their effort.
- **NPCs face the Ascension Tribulation their own way:** at the peak they use everything that helps first and never go while wounded. The ambitious and arrogant go soon, the cautious wait for good odds, the lazy keep putting it off, and those near the end of their years go at once. As for you, a failed attempt kills only half of them; the rest survive, refused, and must climb back to the peak.
- **Ascension is not death:** friends and kin who ascended now show as ascended to the Upper Realm in the summary when you return, in Relations (and among your clan's ancestors), and in a divination of their death, never as dead.
- **A nemesis heals before trying again:** a nemesis now waits for a wounded spirit to mend before facing a bottleneck again, instead of failing breakthrough after breakthrough with its odds ruined.
- **Fifteen new beasts and seven new traits:** the Thornback Pangolin, Stone Rhinoceros, Blade-Feather Eagle, Ghostfire Fox, Mountain-Bearing Tortoise, Mirrorscale Basilisk, Ancient Treant, Nine-Colour Deer, Voidblade Mantis, Golden Cicada of Ages, White Tiger of the West, Vermilion Bird, Void-Devouring Taotie, Nine Nether Wyrm and Candle Dragon. A thorned beast pricks whoever strikes it with fists, palms or a close blade; mirror scales blunt qi arts and turn a quarter of their force back on the caster; a massive beast takes a fifth less from every blow; a blinking one steps through space and is harder to hit even with sweeping arts or when held; a timeless one shakes off stuns, binds and slows; a shadowed one sometimes sinks into its shadow so the next blow strikes only that; a radiant one burns venom and death qi out of itself every round.
- **Blood steeped in a dao:** some beasts' blood quickens insight into a dao: the ghostfire fox and nether wyrm Yin, the nine-colour deer and vermilion bird Yang, the voidblade mantis and taotie Space, the golden cicada and candle dragon Time, the White Tiger the Sword. A line you found over such blood keeps that insight.

## 0.9.25 (2026-11-02)

- **No world shuts every gate on you:** when a sect's intake is full it still makes room for you, as long as you meet its root standard and come in its intake season. A Golden Core guest elder is welcome even when the halls are full. Before, if every sect in a world had filled up, the player could not join any of them. The sect list says when a sect is full but would make room for you.
- **Heaven-and-earth treasures:** the only way to remake a spiritual root or a body, and graded by what they give. A Five-Colour Chaos Lotus Seed leaves a five-element pseudo root (Yellow), a Four Seasons Spirit Fruit four elements (Mystic), a Three Talents Spirit Pearl three (Earth), a Yin-Yang Twin Lotus two (Heaven), and there is one treasure for every single element (Immortal), so you can choose yours: five for the Heavenly roots, three for the Variant roots. Physique treasures (Origin Essence of the Jade Bone, of the Nine Yang Body...) give any physique but a flawed one, graded by its rarity. Refining one takes three months and shows first exactly what would change.
- **Celestial phenomena:** when a treasure is born, the whole world sees its sign (its colour tells the element, a body in the clouds a physique, its reach the grade). The strongest who could use it gather, band together by kin, sect and friendship or strike deals, and when it ripens the strongest band takes it. Be there to fight for it: ask contenders to stand with you, and if you win, decide later who uses it (refine it yourself and your allies resent it, or give it to one of them). How often they come follows the world's qi as it rises and wanes, from about once a century when qi is at its richest to about once in a thousand years when it has withered, and every world has its own fortune: some see them half again as often, some only two thirds as often. Some lie sealed in secret realms with no sign at all, until someone who went deep enough carries one out: then the phenomenon follows them, and the strong come for whoever holds it. The News section lists every phenomenon in the world and how far it is.
- **Seize a living body:** a Soul cultivator of Golden Core with 40 insight into Yin who knows a demonic method can tear their soul out of their own living body and into someone else's, face to face (Relations: Seize their body). Won, the body, root, physique and age are theirs and the old body is left a husk; lost, their soul is torn and the host knows what they tried. A deed of great infamy. Seizing any body, living or from death, costs the soul dearly: its Soul path falls back to Foundation Building.
- **Secret bodies:** physiques no one is born with and no treasure gives, hidden until earned by a great deed, with a popup so you cannot miss it. What earns them, you will have to find out.

## 0.9.24 (2026-11-02)

- **Venom arts:** attack arts have a new form, Venom: poison qi spat, breathed or flung as needles from afar. Its natural trait, Venomous, leaves poison in the wound that eats the flesh (and the liver) for three rounds and lingers as poison after the battle. A poison-immune body shrugs it off and venom-resisting blood takes half. Mastered, a venom art also learns Withering, Draining and Lacerating moves. A neutral venom art rests on the Dao of Yin. Sects teach venom arts too.
- **Spirit herbs come by the handful:** a ripe herb field always gives at least 3 herbs (one cut early, half that), and a patch of spirit herbs found in the wild (gathering, or seeking with divine sense) gives at least 3.
- **Draining essence blood counts as a kill:** a beast you drain for its essence blood now counts as slain for a board's beast cull (and for Beast Slayer and your records). Beasts met on the road also count when you finish them; before, only beasts that belonged to the world did.
- **Beast culls in one step:** taking a beast cull from a request board (or picking it under Your requests) tells you where the beasts are, how long the journey takes and their realm ("a pack of 3 at Foundation Building, 1 month away"). Set out, and you travel there, track them for a month and the fight begins. Each trip faces up to three of the beasts still to slay. The hunting ground is the nearest wilds whose beasts are no stronger than you.
- **Hands at your cave abode:** hire retainers (a mortal labourer, a Qi Condensation retainer or a Foundation Building steward, 3, 15 or 60 stones a month) or set your puppets to work in your abode (Location tab). Miners dig ore and jade from the land around it. Gardeners keep the herb fields tended while you are away, so they never wither, and bring in and replant whatever ripens. Craftsmen make one of your designs over and over in the abode's workshop, from the storehouse's materials. Everything they make goes to the storehouse. Retainers leave when their wages go unpaid; puppets work for free but wear down and stop when worn, until you repair them. An abode keeps three hands, plus one for each level of its puppet workshop.
- **Stewards assign duties:** leading a sect or clan, let the stewards assign duties (Sect tab, Duties) and every member gets one now and again each year. In war the strongest guard. Those skilled in a craft the faction has designs for craft. A senior or two teach while there are juniors. The most advanced third cultivate undisturbed, and the rest gather. A duty you set by hand is kept.
- **The ancestor is called only when needed:** as a faction's ancestor you are called to battle only when the attackers' champion is of a higher realm than all of the faction's own, or fights more strongly than its strongest. A Deity Transformation clan leader no longer sends for you over a mortal.
- **Every bloodline in the guide:** Bloodlines and physiques now lists all fifteen beast bloodlines, the beast that carries each and from what realm, and everything it gives at full purity. It also explains founding, tempering and folding your own line.
- **See what changes before you commit:** founding your own bloodline, tempering it, and folding beast essence blood into it now show, before you begin, each gift that would change, from what your blood gives you now to what it would give (attributes, art power, healing, lifespan, travel speed, purity). Refining essence blood as an ordinary cultivator shows what that bloodline gives at full purity.
- **Three new daos: Poison, Sound and Time.** Poison makes your weapon poisons and venom arts up to 50% stronger, lets poison take up to 50% less hold on you, and helps alchemy; venom arts now draw on it. Sound is the dao of the new sound arts: up to 25% likelier to stun, and every note breaks the foe's momentum. Time makes you up to 20% faster in battle and adds up to 10% to your lifespan. Each has its own contemplation phenomena and places where it is grasped best (marshes for Poison, valleys, mountains and towns for Sound, ruins and secret realms for Time).
- **Sound arts:** a new attack form, killing notes played on an instrument. They strike from afar, sweep the whole field, stun by nature and later learn Dread, Echoing, Sealing and Shattering moves. Without an instrument they are hummed at 65% force.
- **Hidden weapons and instruments:** weapons can now be forged as hidden weapons (needles, darts, throwing knives), a flute, a guqin or a zither, each with its own effect. Hidden weapons are thrown (bare strikes reach a distant foe), strengthen venom arts flung with them by 15%, and hold a poison coating two blows longer and 30% stronger. A flute plays sound arts as one piercing note at a single foe (+25%). A guqin's sound arts also cut at the soul and restore 3% of your qi with each note. A zither's sweep every other foe at 80% force instead of half, and slow those they strike.
- **Myriad Selves:** a new method trait (forge it into a method like any other) that makes avatars cheaper: 8% per grade off their stones, qi and seclusion, up to 75% with several methods. Build a method around it to split off avatars cheaply.
- **More talismans:** vital materials make healing talismans, qi materials qi-restoring ones, toxic ones a poison miasma over every foe, essence a surge of battle momentum, and longevity a shadow veil (harder to hit for three rounds). NPCs burn them too.
- **Market categories and bulk trade:** the market now has category, grade and sort filters for both the stalls and what you sell (price low or high first, grade, name). "Buy everything shown" and "Sell everything shown" act on whatever the filters and search leave showing, after a confirmation with the total. The Inventory can sell or donate everything shown at once, and donate a whole stack to your sect. Or tick the lots and items you want (a tick box on each row, in the market and the Inventory): the bulk buttons then buy, sell or donate just those.

## 0.9.23 (2026-11-02)

- **Auto-resolved fights are never lost:** a fight against a weaker realm now skips only when you far outclass the foes (three times their combined strength), so a lesser realm with a great talent, fine gear or numbers is fought by hand. And if a skipped fight turns against you (you are hurt, or it drags on), it stops at once and opens the battle screen for you to finish it.
- **Great talents:** a rare few are born with a gift for battle (Gifted, Prodigy, Heaven-Defying; comprehension and luck make it likelier, and talented parents often pass it down a step) and fight at ×1.3, ×1.7 or ×2.4 the strength of their realm. Walking two or three paths together within a realm of each other adds ×1.2 or ×1.45 more; together they can let one fight a whole realm up. NPCs are born with talents too, and the talented among them often take up a second path (the Heaven-Defying sometimes a third). Shown on the fate screen, the Character tab and character sheets; Reroll Fate can ask before throwing one away.
- **Dao of Slaughter:** a dao learned only in battle, never by contemplation. Every fight you survive teaches a little, each foe your side fells more (the mightier against you, the more; spars a third as much), up to what your realm holds. It gives up to +20% damage and +10% to hit, and from 50 a killing aura that may cow foes of a lower realm at the start of a battle. NPCs who fight a lot grow it too. Shown under Dao Comprehension.
- **Choose what Reroll Fate asks about:** under "Ask before rerolling away" on the fate screen, pick the root (never, Earth or better, Heavenly or Variant, Variant only) and physique rarity (Uncommon, Rare, Legendary, Mythic) that make Reroll ask first, and whether flawed bodies (Sickly, Withered Meridians, Star-Crossed), a body born incomplete or a bloodline count too. Your choices are remembered.
- **Battle tokens:** whenever a fight has more than two fighters, puppets, a formation or a domain, the battlefield shows every fighter as a token: face, health and qi bars, short status tags (shield, burning, slowed, sealed...), your side and the foes' in their own rows, your target ringed in gold. Puppets stand beside their masters as figures with their integrity. Formations planted mid-battle and unfolded domains are listed over the field with whose they are, what they do and how many rounds they have left. Click a foe's token to strike it; hover any token for the details.
- **Corpse refining:** a puppetry craft scripture can now be written as a demonic art (needs Yin insight; brings infamy). With it you raise slain foes mid-battle as corpse puppets on your side, or kill a beaten foe and refine their body afterwards with your cultivation qi. The art's grade sets how mighty the dead you can bind (never above your own realm, unless your puppetry runs 25 past what the art asks and you have mastered it: then one realm above you), and how many you drive at once. Your prowess (puppetry skill and mastery) decides how much of their old strength comes back: a novice's corpse shambles at well under half its strength, a master's fights at full. Raising costs a fifth to four fifths of your qi, and each corpse drinks more every round; when your qi runs dry it falls still. Surviving corpses are kept and can be raised again from the battle bag.
- **Weapon poisons:** the alchemist's inventor can now make a weapon poison instead of a pill. Toxic herbs make venom, and other herbs lend their nature: Swift numbs and slows, Spirit seals meridians, Vitality rots the wound, Qi dissolves the foe's qi, Sharp thins the blood. Coat your weapon from the Inventory (or the battle bag, costing the turn): the coating rides on blade blows for three blows plus one per grade. A poison below the victim's realm bites weakly, and those immune to poison shrug it off. Sect alchemy halls and NPC alchemists brew some too, so poisons turn up in markets, and NPC fighters coat their blades.

- **Marked beasts still evolve:** a tamed beast renamed in a pack fight (the Scarred Thunder Ape) or a legendary beast with an epithet (White-Browed…) no longer reaches "the peak of its line" by its name alone: it evolves, breeds, drinks its line's essence blood and gains double purity from its own line's cores like any other of its kind. A pack fight's marks (Scarred, Young, Grey…) now last only for that fight, and beasts that kept one from an earlier fight lose it when the save loads. Legendary beasts keep their epithets.
- **Professions list only your crafts:** opening the Professions tab no longer adds crafts you never took up (Alchemist 0.0, Novice, 0 works) to your Character sheet, and blank entries older saves picked up this way are hidden.

## 0.9.22 (2026-11-02)

- **macOS build:** a Mac version, one app for both Intel and Apple Silicon Macs (macOS 10.15 or newer). It is not signed by Apple yet, so the first time, right-click the app and choose Open (if macOS says it is damaged, run `xattr -cr "Ascension Sandbox.app"` in Terminal).

- **Professions tab:** the Crafting tab is now called Professions.
- **Herbalism section:** extract seeds from the herbs you carry right in the Professions tab (Herbalism), with the skill each herb needs. The button is no longer in the Inventory.
- **Spirit soil:** at your abode, spend a month enriching the soil of your herb garden (Professions tab, Herbalism): till the beds and pour in qi, or work raw materials into them for much more. Richer soil grows herbs up to twice as fast and up to 20% finer. Every harvest drains it a little, finer herbs more, and your herbalism sets how rich you can make it (30, up to 100 at skill 100). The Location tab shows the soil under your herb fields.
- **Destinies granted on time:** a destiny whose requirement you meet is now granted within the month. Before, it waited for a full year of passing time, so a stretch cut short by an event, a bottleneck or recovering from wounds (or fame won through a choice) could leave a destiny like Renowned at 300/300 but not earned. Saves where this happened earn them the next month.
- **Calendar filter:** Choose what to show at the top of the Calendar turns each kind of entry on or off (secret realms, festivals, merchants, auctions, tournaments, sect gates and more). The choice is kept between games.
- **Children take after their parents:** a child's spiritual root now follows its parents' instead of being rolled like a stranger's. It most often has as many elements as theirs on average (a little more often one worse than one better, and now and then fate ignores the parents), draws its elements mostly from theirs, and has purity near theirs. Wind, lightning and ice roots now pass down: an ice-rooted parent and a fire-rooted one have mostly single-element children, about a third of them ice. Special physiques pass to 30% of children, 40% when both parents have one, 50% when they share it (was 25%). A companion's children outlook says what grade of root to expect.
- **Method traits:** cultivation methods now do more than set your speed. Each carries as many traits as its grade (Yellow one, Mystic two, Earth three, Heaven four, Immortal five), each stronger the higher the grade: an attribute (+2 at Yellow up to +10 at Immortal), Iron Skin (toughness against blows), Soul Bulwark (soul defence, steadier against heart demons), Surging Qi (power in battle), Flowing Shadow (harder to hit), Verdant Vitality (faster healing), Clear Mind (insight), Smooth Passage (breakthrough chance), Swift Gathering (cultivation speed) or Long Life (lifespan). Every method also has its path's gift: body methods give constitution, soul methods soul defence, qi methods more power for arts of their element. Traits count for the method of every path you walk and leave when you stop practising it; the Character tab shows what your methods add to each attribute.
- **Forging a method:** when you create a method you forge its traits: reroll the ones you have not locked while your rerolls last, then keep them. Insight into the method's dao beyond what its grade needs gives more rerolls and locks; comprehension adds rerolls. The creator shows how many you will have. A method refined up a grade gains a trait, all its traits grow with it, and you forge it again: lock the traits worth keeping and reroll the rest.
- **Body tempering arts removed:** their toughness and healing now come from method traits. Saves drop the tempering arts characters knew, and every existing method is given its traits.
- **Numbered months:** month names show their place in the year, e.g. Osmanthus Month (8).
- **Rare fates are safe from a stray reroll:** rerolling a fate with a Heavenly or variant spirit root, or a special physique, asks first, unless you have kept that fate.

## 0.9.21 (2026-11-01)

- **Android groundwork (tablet first):** an Android export preset (landscape). On phones and tablets only, the PC build unchanged:
  - **Press and hold** any button or place on the map that has a tooltip to read it, disabled buttons included (why they are disabled). A held button is not pressed when you let go.
  - The interface starts larger on small screens, and the scale goes up to 2× in Settings.
  - Buttons no longer show keyboard shortcuts that a touch screen does not have.
  - Run the PC build with `-- --mobile` to try this on a desktop.

## 0.9.20 (2026-11-01)

- **Save slots:** every journey keeps three slots besides the autosave (Save slots, right column). Save before a risky breakthrough or a hard fight and go back if it goes wrong, from there or from the death screen.
- **Character tab sections:** Overview, Cultivation, Health, Dao, Destinies and Journal, so what you check most is one click away (the last one you opened is remembered).
- **Location tab sections:** the place's actions and arrays stay on top; below them, Here, Market, Home, People and News.
- **Gear explains itself:** every weapon, armour and accessory says what it does in battle: which arts a weapon carries, which elements it strengthens or clashes with, and which elements armour resists or is weak to.
- **Breakthrough grades:** the next breakthrough shows its quality, the grade it reaches, the next grade up and how far off it is, and (on hover, and in the Character tab) every thing that shapes it, how much each adds now and at most, and how to raise it. Formed realms show their quality.
- **Destinies:** a Heavenly breakthrough into Foundation Building earns the new **Heaven-Blessed Dao Foundation** (+8% cultivation speed). The old "Heaven-Blessed Foundation", earned by any Heavenly realm, is now **Heaven-Blessed Breakthrough**. Saves that already formed a Heavenly dao foundation earn the new destiny.
- **Battle screen:** arts are grouped (attacks, defence, movement, healing), and their marks are words, not symbols: "(no sword: improvised)", "(weapon +15%)", "combo", "forbidden", "hits all".
- **Tournaments:** you are healed between rounds of a sect tournament, clan contest or the Grand Tournament, and every opponent steps in whole.
- **Before You Set Out:** a new journey opens with advice for the weak: roads are dangerous, learn a movement and a defensive art so you can flee, carry healing pills, keep a save slot. The guide's Getting started says the same.

## 0.9.19 (2026-11-01)

- **Arrays must be powered:** when you set one up, choose how.
  - **The land's qi:** free, but it works by the place's qi (a third of its strength at ×0.5, a little more than full where qi is rich), and each one takes 5-17% of the land's qi from everyone there. Piling arrays up thins the place they were meant to help.
  - **Spirit stones:** full strength anywhere and the land untouched, for 3 stones a month (three times as many each grade). In a month you cannot pay, it lies dormant.
  - The Location tab shows each array's power and the land's qi, and lets you switch. Arrays already set up draw on the land.
- **Weapons have forms:** sword, saber, spear or gloves, chosen at the forge (or left to the metal). Older weapons take their form from their name.
  - Sword, saber and spear arts need a weapon of their form in hand; without one they are improvised at 65% force, and the weapon adds nothing.
  - Fist, palm and finger arts use gloves (which add their force) or bare hands; qi arts can be cast through any weapon.
  - The weapon's element meets the art's: the same element +15%, one that feeds it +5%, one that clashes with it -15%. The battle screen marks each art (✋ improvised, ⚔+ or ⚔- for the element).
  - Others are taken to carry a plain blade for their arts, so they never improvise.
- **Battle bag:** items no longer crowd the battle screen. Open the Bag (I) to use a pill, talisman, disk or puppet, or to take another weapon in hand (or put it away): either costs your turn. Opponents who carry a fitting weapon change to it too.
- **Search and sort:** the Sect library and treasury have a search box, kind and element filters, and sorting. The Inventory and the crafting inventor can filter materials by type (herbs, ores, jade...).
- **Members:** your own sect or clan's members show their cultivation instead of "Unfathomable", as the Ranking already did.
- **Young Talents:** only those 50 and under, and strength counts more than youth, so a child no longer outranks a Golden Core twice their age.
- **Comprehension:** each new stage of your deepest dao adds +2, and Wood-element attribute pills (Enlightenment Pills) raise it. The Comprehension tooltip lists every way to raise it.

## 0.9.18 (2026-11-01)

- **Character creation:**
  - Choosing Male or Female no longer rerolls your fate: only the sex changes (and the given name, unless you wrote your own).
  - **Keep this fate** sets the current fate aside; reroll freely, then **Return to the kept fate** if nothing better came.
  - The fate panel shows your name, sex and age at the top.
  - Dao foundation and lifespan explain themselves on hover.
  - Clan and sect lists name what each is famed for with capitals ("Forging", "Sword", "Dao of Water"), and the sect list no longer says a sect is full, since your letter of recommendation lets you in anyway.
- A clan scion's opening line says "son of" or "daughter of" as you are, not "son or daughter of".
- **Puppets gather for you:** in the wilds, send a puppet out from the Inventory tab. It combs the land month after month, mostly for materials of its own element (seven finds in ten, where there are any), up to a grade above its own. It wears down as it works (faster in dangerous places, slower for sturdy puppets), and before it breaks it walks back and puts its haul in your hands wherever you are. The Inventory tab shows each puppet out, and you can call one home early. Up to five at once.
- **Inner world:**
  - **Feed it** raw materials: they become its land. Enough makes it grow, up to four levels beyond what the Dao of Space gives (more room, richer qi, a bigger harvest). Each grade is worth four times the last.
  - It bears more of what it was fed: ore veins, jade beds and groves, not only herbs, and the elements you fed it most.
  - **Weave a dao** you have grasped (30+ insight) into its laws, in half a year of seclusion: one, and one more every three levels. Each thickens its qi and slowly teaches you and everyone inside. An element's dao makes that element's treasures grow three times as often; Space makes room for three more; Wood or Yang make it bear more. A dao can be unwoven to make room.

## 0.9.17 (2026-11-01)

- **The interface fits the window at any scale:** nothing is pushed past a panel's edge any more.
  - Rows of buttons and dropdowns wrap onto the next line when a panel is too narrow (the smith's Shape, Attune and For pickers used to run off the side).
  - Dropdowns no longer stretch to their longest choice; a long choice is cut short with "…" (the open list shows it whole).
  - The right-hand column's buttons wrap their words instead of widening the column, so the tabs in the centre keep their room at large interface scales.
  - A button too long for its tab wraps and takes a full line, the Records table wraps long values, and the name fields no longer demand a fixed width.
  - If anything is still too wide, the tab scrolls sideways instead of cutting it off.

## 0.9.16 (2026-11-01)

- **No more hard caps on dao insight or crafts:** the old limits are now where growth starts to slow, not where it stops.
  - Past your realm's insight limit, or a craft's bottleneck, gains come at a third of their pace, and that halves again for every 5 points further. A century of contemplation at Golden Core now ends a few points past 50 instead of at exactly 50.
  - Breaking through (or proving a craft with fine work of the next grade) still lifts the limit. The Crafting tab, the dao menu and the Character tab show how fast you are growing past it.
  - Avatars contemplating a dao keep going past the limit, and only ask for a new task once insight there has slowed to a crawl (10 past the limit).
- **Profession tools:** a smith can forge a **tool** for a craft: a cauldron (alchemy), hammer (forging), brush (talismans), array compass (formations), carving knife (puppetry) or **divination coins**. Pick "Shape: Tool" and the craft it is for.
  - Hard and Qi materials lend skill in that craft; Spirit and Swift lend quality of its works.
  - The best one you carry is used automatically, up to what its grade allows (+6 skill at Yellow grade up to +24, and +2% to +8% quality; half the quality for works finer than the tool).
- **Workshop arrays:** a formation master can lay an array **for a craft** instead of a battle or qi array. Set up at a place like any array, it lends skill in that craft to those it serves there.
- **Assistant puppets:** a puppeteer can carve a puppet that **assists a craft** instead of fighting. Carry it and it lends skill at the bench.
- Forging sects now keep a few tool designs, and formation and puppet sects a workshop array or an assistant. The Crafting tab lists what is helping you with each craft.
- **Avatars:**
  - No limit on how many: your realm (or Soul path) sets how many your soul *easily* sustains. Each avatar costs twice the one before, and each one beyond that number costs four times as much again, takes half a year longer and a third more of your gathered qi.
  - Avatars no longer have an element: it only ever gave them a name. They are now the First Avatar, the Second, and so on, and older saves are renamed on load.

## 0.9.15 (2026-11-01)

- **What a sect or clan teaches:** factions now show their element, what they are famed for (an element faction names its element, e.g. "the Dao of Water"), which roots their qi methods suit, the other paths they teach, and the elements of their arts.
  - Character creation shows this for every clan, and for the three greatest sects. Lesser sects show as "little known".
  - In play, the three greatest sects and clans on each side of the clouds are common knowledge. You learn the rest by belonging, visiting their gate, being friendly with one of their members, or hearing it in a teahouse. The Sect tab's faction list shows what you know.
- **Clans look after their young:** if the clan's library has no qi method for your root:
  - With a fine root (Earth grade or better), a high seat, standing 60+, or a recent clan-contest win, an elder composes a method and an attack art of your element for you. It takes a year, or two for Earth grade and up, and both go into the library.
  - Otherwise the clan tries to buy a copy from a friendly faction, or goes to war with a weaker hostile one to take it. The Sect tab says which applies to you and how to earn the elders' favour.
  - A clan that conquers a library also keeps arts that suit its members' roots.
- **Clan contests:** every clan holds a contest every five years, in autumn. The champion wins a purse and a treasure from the storehouse, and the elders' favour.
- **Summoned home for competitions:** in the month of your sect's tournament or clan's contest, if you are away, a summons comes and the faction's transfer array carries you home to compete. It doesn't reach into secret realms or across worlds. The Calendar and Sect tab show the next date.
- **Flight:** Golden Core cultivators (and Foundation Building ones with a weapon to ride) fly straight to where they go instead of following the roads. The map says whether you fly or walk.
  - **Arts of flight:** a travel art of Wind or Lightning lets you fly from Qi Condensation. You create it with insight into the Dao of Wind or Lightning; other travel arts still use the Dao of Space.
- **Upper Realm:** beast kings above the clouds are now immortal beasts of their domain's own tier. Foundation-grade kings left from older saves are driven out. Hidden caches above the clouds no longer hold Yellow-, Mystic- or Earth-grade finds, and old ones are cleared on load. Manuals found in caches are now within two grades of the place.
- **Grand auctions:**
  - An auction is now a catalogue of lots: crafted works, materials, art manuals and breakthrough treasures. Bid on whichever you like, round by round, then move on to the next lot or leave.
  - A city's wealth decides how many lots it has (modest 5, prosperous 7, great 10, and two more above the clouds) and how many suit your realm (40%, 60% or 80% are your realm's grade or the next). The rest are only one grade below, so no more Foundation flowers at Golden Core.
  - The Location tab shows each city's wealth and how many lots to expect.

## 0.9.14 (2026-11-01)

- **No method, no cultivation:** every path you walk needs a method made for it. A Qi path needs a breathing method, a Body path a blood-forging method, and a Soul path a soul-nurturing method.
  - A method of another path does nothing, and you can't practise one for a path you don't walk. Before, it worked at half strength.
  - A path without a method doesn't advance. The cultivation speed tooltip says so.
  - Older saves sort themselves out on load: methods sitting on the wrong path come off, and each path takes the best method you know for it (or the world's common one). NPCs who lack one find one each year.
- **Crafting bottlenecks:**
  - Your realm limits every craft: 20 as a mortal, 15 more each realm, 100 as an immortal.
  - A craft must be proven by fine work (60% quality or better) to grow: 15 at first, 35 after a fine Yellow-grade work, then 20 more for each grade above.
  - Work far beneath you teaches nothing, so quick-crafting low-grade items can no longer carry a craft to 100.
